- **A new life in a territory no longer reads "No reviewed state identity"** A
  new life in Guam, the U.S. Virgin Islands, American Samoa or the Northern
  Mariana Islands opened on a territory card that ended with "No reviewed state
  identity matches this selection." under its voting section. A territory's card
  now leaves the voting section out, Puerto Rico's included, since the numbers
  it shows are for the fifty states and the District of Columbia.

- **A trip says when it leaves and how long it takes** In Elko, Noah's 15-minute
  trip to a friend's house on Saturday read "6090 minutes, including any wait
  before it begins," and in Lewiston a county party meeting read "1980 minutes."
  The number was every minute until the activity was over, starting from now,
  and it looked like a trip lasting days. Each row now says when the activity
  starts and how long it takes, for example "Starts Saturday, January 10, 2026
  at 2:45 p.m. and takes 15 minutes." Where the visit includes a trip, the row
  also says how long the trip there takes.

- **A vacant vice presidency is filled, and the Vice President succeeds a
  President who dies** When the Vice President dies, the office no longer stays
  empty for the rest of the game. The President nominates a successor, both
  houses of Congress confirm them, and the new Vice President serves out the
  same term. A nominee who sat in Congress leaves the seat, which is then filled
  like any other vacancy. When a President dies in a new game's opening term,
  the Vice President now becomes President for the rest of the term. The vice
  presidency then falls vacant and is filled the same way. Before, the
  presidency stayed empty. The amendment lets a President nominate anyone
  eligible. Whom a President picks, how long the nomination and the vote take,
  and how Congress votes are the game's own stand-ins until the research comes
  back: an even draw among everyone old enough other than the player, ten days
  to nominate, 75 days to confirm, and both houses confirm.

- **A seat you win in your state legislature puts you in the chamber** When you
  won a state legislative seat in a chamber the game had already filled, the
  member who held your district kept the seat after your term began, and the
  chamber's roll never listed you, so you had no floor votes. Now, on the day
  your term begins, that member leaves the seat and you take it. You appear on
  the chamber's roll and vote on the bills put to it. If you are re-elected, you
  keep the same seat. A reminder to decide a vote that passed while you were
  busy elsewhere, such as out campaigning, no longer stays on your calendar
  after the vote.

- **Saying yes to a Saturday invitation now counts** Saying yes to a Saturday
  invitation now counts. The afternoon goes on your calendar with a short trip
  there, and when the day comes you go, and it is remembered as time spent with
  the person who asked. Before, a yes was recorded and then the day "passed
  without an answer". Invitations now say who asked and when, from your side:
  "Rachel Kim asked you over on Saturday afternoon. Going is optional." The
  Invitations panel has a button to say you will come as well as to decline, and
  it is no longer the same neighbor asking every time.

- **Eviction judgments require actual seated judge facts** Missing seated judges
  now leave eviction case facts unsupported before a court name is constructed.
  The existing judgment writer keeps the filing pending. Saved judgments name
  the actual judge; they no longer receive a generic court actor. The focused
  civil-actor fixtures reuse the shared court finder and retain vacancy,
  reappointment, provenance, counsel, repeat and reload checks.

- **Charging decisions require a recorded prosecutor** A seated prosecutor
  reviews a saved referral through the shared decision function and records the
  reasons for charging or declining. Cases remain pending when no prosecutor is
  seated or the decision is unresolved. Reopened saves retain the recorded
  review.

- **Cash bail requires an operative or saved amount** New money-bail charges now
  read offense-specific numeric terms from the law in force. The existing
  adopted-text reader consumes `cash-bail:<offenseKey>` under the cash-bail
  question, in USD minor units. No national median is used as a legal schedule.
  Missing numeric authority leaves the pretrial money consequence pending rather
  than inventing a deposit or detention decision. Payments reuse the amount
  already saved on the charge, including old saves, through the existing
  full-cash payment and refund writers. Commercial bond premiums remain
  separate. Current starting-law records contain no structured bail amounts, and
  the existing detention decision sets release or hold rather than cash. This
  change supports recorded authority; sourcing those terms or an amount-setting
  court producer remains a separate input gap.

- **Electoral allocation reads each place's recorded allocation method.**
  National election rules now read allocation method and jurisdiction kind from
  the existing place reference. District splits, electoral totals, and
  contingent-election state membership remain unchanged. The current
  presidential count keeps its recorded national-mood inputs and does not
  restore the former regional residual. Canonical identity data also retains the
  existing legacy state aliases without changing saved IDs.

- **Patronage support reads recorded campaign help** Town patronage support
  counts distinct adult residents in outstanding debtor households who actually
  donated, volunteered, or provided chapter backing to the candidate's current
  campaign. Debt bands and household membership no longer invent vote weights,
  and a fixed half-share cap no longer clips recorded help. The reader exposes
  the people and favor records it counted. These are campaign help records, not
  evidence of secret ballots or a new support producer.

- **Missing income records no longer crash the money clock** The money clock
  continues when a production world has no aggregate personal-income definition.
  Completed payments remain recorded. Without compatible recorded income for the
  exact jurisdiction and month, public payments produce no macro shock. Worlds
  with compatible income keep the existing calculation.

- **Campaign fieldwork follows the recorded crew and their hours** Campaign
  shifts now count the people recorded on the completed activity and use their
  actual work time. Organizers find outreach dates from the saved calendar and
  wait for outstanding invitations to finish. Ballot guidance no longer states a
  blanket age requirement.

- **Outcome effects use their recorded coefficient** A place uses its recorded
  central effect size, falling back to the existing link-wide estimate. Seeds no
  longer change the coefficient, and research ranges remain evidence rather than
  a draw.

- **Law projections share the live outcome web** Law effect paths now read their
  projection rows through the same outcome web used by live outcomes. Saved
  cause and effect records retain their writers and integrity checks in a
  separate storage module; the compatibility evaluator remains available for
  explicit legacy records.

- **Preserve stated birthdays and opening family choices** Custom childhood
  starts now let you choose one parent, two parents, or a guardian. Unspecified
  opening families use the game's recorded family spread, with the estimate and
  its source retained in the save. Continuing the creator preserves your
  birthday fields: choose both month and day, or leave both blank to begin from
  an age without drawing missing birthday fields.

- **Starting laws reach teacher, privacy and saved-tax readers** The teacher
  floor, privacy expense and tax consequence readers now evaluate starting laws
  through the same dated law query as enacted laws. A teacher starting law must
  carry its structured annual amount. Privacy keeps the approved one-time
  estimate and refuses unsupported own-law coverage. Tax effects still require
  an exact saved proposal or statutory levy join; this change creates no tax,
  rate, liability or payment.

- **Pension budget allocations use the shared amount evaluator** Opening and
  newly adopted budgets evaluate the same saved annual obligation and minimum
  contribution share through the shared amount evaluator. A full-contribution
  law preserves larger measured allocations. Sourced opening pension
  observations and actual cash settlement remain intact; the retired
  pension-only module no longer computes payments. Recorded public-program
  payments now preserve an exact canonical budget category before interpreting
  older descriptive program keys. Paid pension contributions and interest remain
  in their own categories instead of being counted as other programs; authority
  and unpaid commitments still create no settled expense.

- **Same-day activities stop repeating daily consequences** Completing multiple
  activities on one date no longer re-enters date-only government consequences.
  Crossing into a new date still runs the existing daily chain. National office
  transitions retain their exact-moment handling, and activities retain their
  saved completion events.

- **Tuition price consequences leave unrelated payday payments alone**
  Price-cost consequences ignore statutory tax payment activities and resource
  flows whose basis does not match the row before requiring a person payer.
  Ordinary town paydays continue with the tuition row registered. A referenced
  resource-flow terms record whose flow is missing still reports the integrity
  error; tuition caps and recorded backfill retain their existing behavior.

- **Paycheck taxes use the adopted flat income-tax rate** A state income-tax
  bill with an adopted flat rate and annual taxable-income threshold uses those
  terms on actual paychecks. A missing deduction remains a labeled estimate.
  Existing tax-year timing, withholding receipts and saved liabilities are
  preserved.

- **Statehood no longer subtracts a frozen Medicaid aid estimate** Budget
  adoption no longer invents a financial certificate from cash held or subtracts
  a fixed fiscal-2024 Medicaid amount. Actual saved program payments continue to
  supply the government's spending rows. Historical certificate bytes remain
  readable when a saved game continues; they authorize no payment.

- **Adult charging reads the dated juvenile age** Adult charging reads the law's
  numeric juvenile-age ceiling and preserves its operative date. Where an age is
  unread, the reader labels the observed same-answer peer-rule mode and exposes
  its contributing jurisdictions instead of stopping all adult charging.
  Ordinary sponsor filings persist that peer-derived age through the existing
  bill-section writer. This general age does not authorize an individual
  adult-court transfer.

- **State budgets start with a saved public cash account.** New worlds open one
  public cash account for each state, D.C. and territory. Federal and uniquely
  identified local governments use the same writer and their saved opening cash
  profile. Unsupported or ambiguous local ownership creates no replacement
  account. The saved world’s opening cash profile takes precedence, with
  researched general-fund and rainy-day balances used only where that profile
  has no value. All openings are labeled as estimates. Existing saved government
  accounts are reused without changing their identities or money, and opening
  cash is never recorded as tax revenue. Cash-settled months retain law-effect
  attribution without treating modeled effects as payments.

- **Paid public programs retain their law and payment records in local
  budgets.** State and local budget months retain the saved appropriation,
  commitment, installment and actual payment behind a public-program cost. The
  existing federal reader supplies the same validated chain for every
  government. Cash amounts and payment schedules stay on their original records;
  an unpaid appropriation creates no expense.

- **Paid wages supply the existing tax-base records automatically.** Ordinary
  payroll records each saved wage-income liability's actual gross wages as its
  tax base before law attribution. The original liability and completed
  collection remain unchanged. Repeated processing and Continue retain one base
  per source, including catch-up pay's historical occurrence date.

- **State budgets require saved cash before recording a month.** State budgets
  no longer record forecast money as received or spent when their public cash
  account or recorded payments are unavailable.

- **Published starting-law effects can reach residents through the news** The
  existing story-to-exposure consumer and news writer now validate a starting
  law through the same dated legal-identity query as ordinary exposures. A
  starting-law prefix alone still grants nothing. News retains its saved
  knowledge, publication, lead and effect references, carries no money or
  opinion, and writes nothing twice.

- **Read the starting wage law separately from later enactments** The wage
  comparison reads the canonical starting law through the existing final term
  reader, instead of treating the current enacted wage as its own baseline.

- **Read cannabis tax jurisdiction scope from its declared rule** Cannabis
  revenue readings now follow the tax rule's declared government scope. Local
  budgets continue to receive no state tax revenue without a governing basis.

- **Keep tuition changes on recorded school bills** Tuition freezes cap the
  affected public college's unpaid bill using its recorded ownership, applicable
  state law and dated tuition price. They no longer reduce every government fee
  by a state tuition share. Paid school bills and government appropriations keep
  their existing cash recipients; no extra government revenue or automatic
  backfill is created.

- **Statehood certification follows the admitted government** Statehood
  certification now selects the government named by the existing admission
  record rather than rejecting it because of its budget level label. Other
  governments do not inherit that government's certification or aid change.

- **Inclusionary housing uses the enacted affordable share** New affordable-home
  allocations now use the governing law's recorded share instead of a universal
  fifteen percent. Adopted amendments determine the allocation, and the
  affordable rent record retains their source chain. Missing or mistyped shares
  do not invent an affordable-unit requirement. Existing affordable leases
  remain intact. Starting D.C. and New Jersey laws now carry cited share
  estimates. Existing opening stock does not count as new construction in the
  set-aside allocation.

- **Private rent renewals use sourced caps and recorded construction coverage**
  Private lease renewals now apply the recorded governing cap through the shared
  price-cost dispatcher. Starting-law formula components come from the cited
  California, D.C., Oregon and Washington records and use recorded game
  inflation. Later enacted caps use the adopted scalar and explicit tenancy
  coverage. Opening stock is pre-existing under the approved game model. In-play
  dwellings use their saved creation date and the sourced construction exemption
  window: 15 years in California and Oregon, 12 in Washington. D.C. has no
  rolling expiry. No construction date is invented. Owner-occupied exemptions
  remain a follow-up. Existing payments and saved rent terms are preserved.

- **Open health business books through surviving town finance writers** The
  surviving town-finance module accepts recorded staff quarter-pay to open
  business books at the start of a game. Clinics and care homes use the same
  existing industry calculation as other town businesses. Their opening sales
  advance and later quarterly receipts use canonical resource transfers with
  stable employer/date keys. Existing government, customer and player payments
  count toward sales rather than adding a second credit. The town's existing
  aggregate customer constructor has one shared home; its current local-business
  consumers retain their identity and behavior. A tracked customer balance still
  caps payment. The adapter does not create employer cash accounts or estimate
  unsupported pay periods; the owning payroll caller supplies its recorded pay
  and cash setup before calling it.

- **Rival cash estimates and organizer follow-up use saved records** A new rival
  committee estimates its opening cash from saved committee accounts for the
  same office and currency. An organizer can offer another organizing meeting
  after recorded attendance, using the same decision and calendar checks as
  other campaign activities.

- **Recorded floor ballots obey the shared session-end rules** Player and
  council floor callers retain the shared bill driver's session-end decision.
  Supplying a recorded roll call cannot move a bill after adjournment; the saved
  procedure still decides whether it dies or remains pending. Open sessions
  retain the supplied members, ballots, provenance and sponsor control.

- **Council and player actions obey one session-end reader** Automatic D.C.
  council actions and legacy player legislative actions use the same session-end
  reader as the institutional bill driver. Recorded procedure and adjournment
  decide whether a pending bill dies, carries over or waits for a later sitting.
  Open-session vote writers and supplied ballots retain their existing behavior.
  No legal end date is supplied where one is unestablished.

- **The private client keeps main's artwork when main's code moves on** The
  private client's updater now builds accepted main with the runtime artwork
  another track already plays, once main's code supports runtime art, instead of
  requiring an art pack pinned to one source revision. A code change on main no
  longer strands the owner's build on an older revision with its artwork refused
  as incompatible.

- **Time stops when accepted work is due to start** Day and Week advances stop
  on the first day of an accepted job, including after loading a save. The Work
  screen opens on that date so the player can start the job before the employer
  treats the absence as a missed start.

- **An accuser is no longer quoted disputing their own allegation** When a rival
  accused a candidate of misconduct, the newspaper treated the rival as a
  subject of the story and printed them denying their own claim. A story about
  an allegation is now about the person accused; the accuser is its source.

- **Law consequence dispatch retains canonical registrations** Law consequence
  dispatch validates the catalog rows for the requested activity, so unrelated
  tax assessment rows do not block coverage renewals. Optional caller
  registrations retain canonical handlers; conflicting owners and unsupported
  requested kinds, selectors, and actions still fail validation.

- **Keep an adopted income-tax rate in the paycheck schedule** An adopted flat
  income-tax rate such as 7% stays in the existing paycheck schedule when its
  decimal representation introduces a multiplication residue. Rates finer than
  the schedule's whole-basis-point precision remain unsupported.

- **Alabama's Governor, Senate and House ask for Alabama's own ages and
  residence** A 22-year-old in Alabama was told he could run for Governor, and
  the state Senate asked candidates to be 30. Alabama now holds candidates to
  its own requirements. A Governor must be at least 30 and have lived in Alabama
  for seven years. A state Senator must be at least 25, and a state
  Representative at least 21. Both must have lived in Alabama for three years
  and in their district for one. When a candidate is too young or has not lived
  somewhere long enough, the refusal now says so plainly for every state whose
  requirements the game has read.

- **Alaska now judges a legislative candidacy on its real requirements instead
  of refusing every date** A candidate in Alaska was refused for the legislature
  on any date before September 2026, whatever their age or how long they had
  lived there. Nothing was wrong with the candidate and nothing was wrong with
  Alaska's rules; the game simply had no record of when those rules began, only
  of when we read them, and it would not claim a requirement applied before the
  day we saw it. That record now exists. Alaska's constitution became operative
  on January 3, 1959, and the two sections that say who may stand for the
  legislature have never been amended since, so the rules apply on any start
  date the game offers. A lifelong resident of Sitka filing in January can be
  assessed on the actual requirements: twenty-five for the senate, twenty-one
  for the house, three years in the state and one in the district. Where a
  rule's start date is still unknown, the game continues to refuse rather than
  guess. That caution was never the bug; the missing date was.

- **Amendment ballots are decided by the voters' own views** When a state's
  legislature refers a policy amendment to the voters, the result now comes from
  what the state's eligible residents actually think about that policy: each
  supporter or opponent counts once, and an even split fails. When nobody on
  record holds a view, no result is invented and the amendment waits. Term-limit
  amendments wait for now, since no one can hold a recorded view on them yet.

- **The game is written in American English throughout** Color, center,
  neighbor, behavior, canceled, gray: the game's words, its notes, and the
  guides its writers follow now spell things the American way. Dates read month
  first, so a day shows as September 22, 2026. Population density on the map is
  given in people per square mile instead of per square kilometer. A check now
  reads every file in the project for British spellings, day-first dates and
  metric measurements in player text, so they do not creep back. Saved games are
  untouched: the few words a save stores keep their old spelling, and an old
  save still loads.

- **A date asked for before you were with somebody does not go ahead after**
  Before, a date asked for just before becoming a couple with somebody else
  could still be answered yes and go ahead. Now it is withdrawn when its answer
  would come, the People screen says the date did not go ahead, and nothing is
  put on the calendar.

- **A page left over from before an update now asks you to reload instead of
  showing no saved lives.** If a tab or a cached copy of the game from before an
  update is opened after the update has already run, it cannot open the saves
  the newer version kept. It used to show no saved lives at all. The title
  screen and the saved games list now say the page is an older copy, that
  nothing was deleted, and offer a Reload button.

- **An ask nobody answered no longer reads as time spent together** In a San
  Antonio life, James last saw Stella Shaw in January 2039, yet for two years
  his Journal kept saying "You and Stella Shaw were in and out of each other's
  business more than once." Stella had only asked to meet, and the day passed
  without an answer. The game also writes a note that it offered the scene, and
  that note was being counted as something the two of them did together. It no
  longer counts. The line now reads "Stella Shaw tried to reach you," and it
  says "more than once" only when she asked more than once.

- **When somebody offers another day, you see it and can answer** Before, when
  somebody answered an invitation or a date with "not that day, but another,"
  the player saw nothing: no reply, and no day offered back. Asking again got
  the same silence. Now the other day shows on the People screen as their
  question to answer, and a yes to a date says so.

- **The creator says plainly when appearance cannot be chosen yet** In a build
  with no approved character art, the appearance step at character creation said
  "No compatible artwork is available in this catalog." That read like a fault,
  and it used a word the player has no reason to know. It now says "Choosing how
  you look is not available yet. Your character can still begin." The Begin
  button was never blocked, and it still is not. The step also gave its only
  sentence a 69-pixel strip on a phone: it kept an empty column for a figure it
  had nothing to draw. With no figure, the text now has the whole step, and it
  sits on its own backing so it can be read over the room. The appearance
  section under Personal had the same sentence in different words. It now reads
  "Changing how you look is not available yet." Nothing is drawn differently.
  Development fixtures are still never offered as a choice.

- **Appropriations keep the program they fund** Enacted appropriations for two
  named standing programs in one state now keep separate program records. A
  later supplemental appropriation for the same program adds its own spending
  authority to that program. Where a docket bill does not identify one program
  target, enactment no longer assigns its aggregate ceiling to a generic state
  program.

- **Around you shows only your own town's police reports** The Around you page
  on the News desk no longer lists police reports and arrests from other towns.
  A player in Clarksdale, Mississippi was seeing reports from Washington, D.C.
  Crime now appears there only when it happened in the player's own town.

- **Making a journey no longer strands the meeting it was booked for** An outing
  appears in the calendar as two rows: the journey, and the thing you are going
  to. Carrying out the second one travels and attends in a single action.
  Pressing the journey's own row instead — the first control on the screen —
  completed the travel and then left the meeting refusing to happen, saying the
  game could not tell where you were standing. It stayed refused for the rest of
  that life. Asking a party for an organizing meeting, walking to it, and then
  being unable to attend it happened on the first press. Arriving somewhere is
  now recorded the same way whichever control you pressed, so the meeting at the
  far end can be attended. A place the game has no authored route to is still
  refused exactly as before; nothing new was invented to reach it.

- **The Art Desk no longer lists notices for withdrawn candidates** A candidate
  that has been archived no longer leaves its notices in the Art Desk's
  notification list. Notices for candidates still under review are unchanged.

- **The browser and the installed game now show the same people** The installed
  game loaded its selected artwork at startup. A browser did not, so the same
  save opened in a browser could draw a different cast than the installed game
  drew from the same artwork, with nothing on screen to say which one you were
  looking at. Both now read the same artwork the same way. Two ways the game
  could quietly mix one generation of people with another are closed. Artwork
  the selected pack does not contain is now reported as missing by name, instead
  of being filled in from whatever older artwork the folder still held, so a
  figure can no longer be assembled out of two generations at once. Saved games
  are unaffected either way.

- **Prosecutors decide which corruption findings become charges** Every
  supported misconduct finding reaches a prosecutor, who decides whether to
  charge using the evidence, recorded traits, relationships, and available
  offense range.

- **Bank failures reflect their share of town deposits** A bank failure tightens
  town credit in proportion to the bank's recorded share of deposits. The
  failure keeps its original amounts when later bank books change. Older
  failures without those amounts supply no guessed share.

- **Town bank profiles follow their recorded modeled size** New town bank books
  use the observed bank with assets nearest their modeled deposits, with
  certificate ties. Small source groups use the national observed pool as an
  estimate. Paired ratios and existing saved bank profiles remain intact; world
  seeds do not choose a bank's financial profile.

- **A bargaining sitting describes the colleague in front of you, not a town in
  Kentucky.** When a bill you configured reached the members' room, the sitting
  argued about your bill but introduced the member arguing for it as
  representing Ashland and the counties around it, whichever state you were
  sitting in and whatever the bill was about. The subject of the negotiation was
  already drawn from your own bill; the reads of the two colleagues were not, so
  a broadband bill in Nevada seated an advocate who wanted what a Kentucky
  transit authority needed. The member who wants the narrower section is now
  introduced by the section they want and who they want it written for, taken
  from the bill on the floor. The member counting the cost reads as before. The
  authored Kentucky transit sitting is unchanged and still names Ashland,
  because that sitting is about Ashland.

- **BG-05 scene framing is fixed on main** The room camera now lowers to keep
  figure crowns visible. That fix is already on main; the assignment board now
  records the completion.

- **The conversation record completion is recorded** This entry records the
  conversation display fix in the pool.

- **Record the completed BG-12 fixes** The blank morning note was removed, and
  the status card now follows the current room after a day passes. The pool
  records both fixes as complete.

- **One candidate reads as "1 person entered the race"** When a single candidate
  files for a congressional or state legislative seat, the filing summary now
  says "1 person entered the race" instead of "1 people".

- **One member, one candidate or one sick person now reads in the singular**
  Party split, primary and illness summaries no longer say "1 members", "1
  candidates" or "1 people" when the count is one.

- **Opening story counts members without a party as independents** The opening
  story's chamber line now reads "2 independents" (or "1 independent") instead
  of "2 No party".

- **A payment line now says who you paid or who paid you** Before, the day
  summary read "Paid $193.46." with no one named. Now it reads "Paid $193.46 to
  the Reyes household" (or the person or organization), and a receipt names who
  it came from.

- **Offering another day to a friend no longer reads "I can't do on January
  15"** Before, turning down a far-off meeting day said "I can't do on January
  15, 2026." Now the line reads "I can't make it on January 15, 2026," and the
  yes answer and the friend's own request read the same way for every distance.

- **A new life's front page starts with the news recorded on its first day** The
  opening archive skipped public events dated on the first day of a new life, so
  a fresh world began with no published news and the opening story had no
  headlines. Events dated that day are now published too.

- **Mark person card copy as fixed** The person card no longer describes someone
  in the room as away from the current location.

- **BG-14 is recorded as already fixed** The mortality correction was already
  present on main before this assignment status update.

- **A council reading that cannot be voted no longer stops the calendar** When a
  council or county board could not take a scheduled reading, because no seated
  member could decide it or the vote was refused, passing time failed with an
  error instead of recording the block. The reading is now recorded as blocked
  with its reason and the calendar moves on.

- **A legislature's budget bill can pass again** An appropriation answers no
  policy question, so a legislator with no view of its programs had no reason to
  vote, and the Alaska budget stopped reaching the governor. A budget bill is
  now decided by each member's principles on public spending, the party that
  carries it, and how near the fiscal year is when the government's offices
  close without one. On the town government screen, a colleague who abstains is
  now listed as "Answered present", so the player's own "Present, not voting"
  choice is the only line with that text and the prose-anchor check can bind it
  again.

- **Budgets count every police officer and teacher in town** A town's budget
  counted only the officers and teachers its job market had hired, so those
  written with the town's residents at the start were never laid off or counted.
  A Duluth, Minnesota world kept 16 public school teachers against a funded
  count of 4. The budget now counts everyone living in town who holds the funded
  role, and it never ends the player's own job, which the player's career
  decides.

- **The screen redraws after a press in a tenth of a second instead of two
  seconds on a long save.** After every press, the game works out which calendar
  entries the player can see. It did that once per entry, going through the
  whole life's activities each time. On Parker Brooks's 84 MB Woonsocket save,
  the calendar options took 1.5 seconds and the list of places to go took 0.5
  seconds. They now take under 0.1 seconds together, and the answer is reused
  until the calendar changes. The bill clock also finds a free record key
  without comparing every candidate to every key it has written.

- **Let a player call off an upcoming campaign appointment** An upcoming party
  or campaign appointment that the player requested can be called off from
  Calendar. The choice is saved and releases its appointment and journey without
  spending time or claiming the host was notified.

- **The field memo says when it was counted and moves from the count you last
  saw** After a condensed campaign week, the field memo compared its number with
  a count taken earlier in that same week, one you never saw. A Seattle memo
  said "Up 8.1 points" when the number had fallen from about 47 to about 30
  since the last one on the screen. A week's counts now move from the last count
  before that week, which is the one you saw. Other counts move from the last
  count taken on an earlier day, so a memo no longer says "since the count on"
  its own date after several afternoons of work in one day. The move is now
  given in the whole points the memos print. Texas memos that read 63 and then
  60 said "Down 2.2"; they now say "Down 3 points". A count is only taken when
  the campaign works, so a memo can sit unchanged for weeks. It now opens with
  its own date, for example "Counted September 14, 2026, 52 days ago. Nobody has
  counted since." The strategy brief gives the memo's date in words, not as
  2026-09-14. When a party organizer goes over running for office, the Journal
  no longer shows codes such as "MINIMUM_AGE for a LOWER_CHAMBER in US-WA" or "1
  years". It now reads "To stand for the House of Representatives, you must be
  at least 21; you must have lived 1 year in the state immediately before
  filing; and a term is 2 years." Entries already in a saved game read the same
  way. Your save still stores the original wording.

- **Campaign sessions can be attended from the calendar** A campaign's booked
  sessions can now be carried out from the calendar. A field shift, a
  fundraising call session or an advertising sign-off booked by a committed week
  (or on its own) no longer refuses with "You have no way to get from … to The
  campaign's call desk" and no longer offers "Give up on this". Attend in the
  Calendar, Places, or Today's "Use your time" does the session in place, the
  same way the Campaigns tab's "Do it now" does: the money raised, the doors
  knocked and the buy placed are recorded, no journey is invented, and time
  moves on past it. A session that cannot be done says why — an earlier session
  in the week comes first, the committee can no longer pay for the buy, or its
  time has already passed ("Let it go from the campaign's week") — and nothing
  is written. Measured running for mayor of Eufaula, Alabama; it affected every
  campaign.

- **A House campaign shows its own race and its own district** The campaign
  screen's list of what the other campaigns have done in public now shows only
  the rivals in the race you are running. Someone who ran for city council and
  then for the U.S. House saw the council opponent's old advertising filling the
  list while the House opponent was missing. An earlier race's rivals stay with
  that race; if nothing has been heard yet about the current rivals, the list
  says so. A U.S. House campaign is now placed in its congressional district,
  such as Washington's 7th Congressional District, rather than across the whole
  state. The weekly plan offers the district, and field shifts and call sessions
  are held there. Statewide races, including the U.S. Senate, still campaign
  across the state.

- **Campaign helpers stay for election night** Campaign staff remain active on
  the night their recorded election is decided. Their campaign work ends on the
  following day through the existing clock. Replaces: ending staff work at the
  same instant as the election result. Candidate work, saved results and the
  cancellation of future campaign actions stay intact.

- **Campaigning no longer carries a candidate to 96% of the vote, and your own
  polling can be wrong** Every afternoon on the doors used to move the same
  share of the vote whether a candidate stood at 40% or 90%, so a candidate who
  kept canvassing could run from 48% to 96%. Campaign work now moves support
  fully up to half the field, less and less above it, and not at all past
  three-quarters. The same rule applies to rival campaigns. The campaign's own
  read of where you stand is no longer always close. How far off it can be
  depends on who on the campaign has done survey work, and for how long: a
  volunteer's count can miss by as much as twelve points.

- **Campus pictures require compatible place tags** Campus selection now checks
  region, climate, terrain and size for exact campus identities and same-state
  stand-ins as well as regional stand-ins. The room picker no longer bypasses
  those checks with an untagged shared quad. An exact registered institution can
  use its existing painting when its recorded employer name and state agree.
  Other colleges need recorded target tags before they can borrow a painting;
  those producer fields are still missing from legacy worlds. The existing 52
  paintings, source identities, hashes and art approval records are unchanged.
  This partial fix does not establish ordinary generated campus attendance or
  new visual approval.

- **Candidate guidance opens its conversation in the room** Going to a scheduled
  conversation about running for office now opens the community room with the
  recorded host. You can ask the available questions, stay for the talk, or
  leave and return home. Asked questions and answers remain visible during the
  visit. The party work list no longer says something blocked your trip when you
  reached the room.

- **A review catalog built from nothing says so instead of producing an
  unreadable number** Composing banked artwork for review against a publication
  that contained no generations gave every part a catalog generation of negative
  infinity. Nothing downstream could match a part numbered that way, so the
  review surface came up empty and said nothing about why. An empty publication
  means nothing has been published yet, which is the same situation as never
  having published at all, so parts now join the first review generation exactly
  as they would have on a first run. No shipped artwork was numbered this way;
  every caller in play passes a publication that already has generations in it.

- **Fictional legislative candidates meet the recorded residence requirement**
  Fictional legislative candidates now receive the residence duration required
  by the chamber's sourced rule instead of a random number of years. Calendar
  months preserve month-based requirements and leap-year dates. When no duration
  has been read, the background records that requirement as unknown without
  inventing prior years. Existing candidates and saved residence history remain
  on record.

- **Office salary laws use the common payroll authority path** An operative
  office salary law now changes recorded weekly pay terms through common
  payroll, preserving increases, decreases and limits on existing terms. The
  saved terms name the actual law and retain its provision and enactment records
  through canonical attribution. Recorded NPC office holders now receive salary
  agreements and use the same payday settlement as the controlled office holder.
  Operative office-pay laws reach every matching recorded official; saved public
  accounts remain the payers where their ownership is recorded.

- **Report saved coverage eligibility from canonical law stamps** Resident law
  news reads both canonical coverage-eligibility stamps and older
  health-coverage stamps. Its wording identifies legal eligibility rather than
  claiming an unrecorded enrollment, while keeping source attribution, privacy
  and duplicate-story protections.

- **Keep city appropriations in their funded public treasury** Replaces:
  same-jurisdiction public-account matching with compiled government stable-key
  resolution and dated ownership evidence. Existing account IDs, saved keys,
  balances and payment records remain intact. Local appropriation admission uses
  that same canonical account reader.

- **Council callers share the canonical government lookup** The government-unit
  reader resolves existing municipio identities through the same lookup as other
  catalog governments. Council callers reuse that reader instead of maintaining
  a separate fallback. The identity lookup reads the existing county and
  population banks directly, so loading legislative rules no longer starts the
  playable-world graph.

- **Paycheck tax records use one assessment path** Removed unused parallel
  paycheck preparation and partition helpers. Wage-tax checks now read the
  existing statutory liabilities and collections, including repeat assessment
  and saved-world continuity, without a second assessment path.

- **Weekly wage-law pay uses its recorded legal authority** Weekly job pay now
  applies wage-law changes through the registered payroll consequence,
  preserving the saved law stamp on its pay terms and payment. The separate
  weekly floor writer and unused town floor writer are retired. Withholding and
  replay continue through the existing settlement path.

- **A person's card names only what you have learned about them** A card no
  longer lists the whole temperament of someone you barely know. It names a
  trait only once you have seen it decide something they told you, such as a
  neighbor agreeing to come to a meeting because they like company. The small
  card names up to three. The full card says, for each, when you saw it and why
  they answered as they did.

- **Criminal cases wait for a sitting judge** A due criminal case stays pending
  when no sitting, unrecused judge can hear it. A vacant court no longer
  conducts a trial or imposes a substitute sentence.

- **A reporter lied to about campaign money now finds out when the finding is
  published** Choosing "Deny it (this is a lie)" when a reporter asked about
  campaign money used to be checked once, before any finding existed, and then
  forgotten. Now every reporter who was given that denial reads the finding when
  a watchdog publishes it, learns the denial was false, and takes a follow-up
  story to their desk, which asks the candidate to respond before it runs.

- **Census-designated towns now have the businesses their size supports** A
  census-designated town such as Kualapuu, Hawaii or Lime Ridge, Pennsylvania
  used to get one of every kind of business, with made-up staff and sales,
  because the game had no population estimate for it. It now uses the American
  Community Survey count for the town, and its grocery stores, diners, garages,
  law offices and other businesses follow the county's published establishment
  counts like any other town.

- **A census-designated town now starts at its Census survey count instead of
  1,000 people.** About two in five of the towns a life can start in are
  census-designated places, such as Kittery, Maine, Urban Honolulu or East Los
  Angeles. The Census Bureau's annual estimates do not cover them, so each one
  was seated as a town of 1,000 people. Each now starts at the Census Bureau's
  American Community Survey 2020-2024 count: 5,110 people in Kittery, 345,482 in
  Urban Honolulu, 842 in Lime Ridge, Pennsylvania. The town's households, homes
  and workforce are sized from that count.

- **Towns are called what their residents call them** Eight consolidated
  city-county places were showing the Census Bureau's filing label instead of
  their own name, so a life in Tennessee opened in "Nashville-Davidson
  metropolitan government (balance), Tennessee" and its local party was named
  after the same phrase. Nashville is Nashville now, along with Athens, Augusta,
  Butte, Indianapolis, Louisville and Milford. The full legal label is still on
  the place's record and still matches when you search for it.

- **Character contacts retain admitted household dates** Character contacts can
  retain an explicitly supplied existing household and its recorded start date.
  Current home fields alone do not create earlier residence. Repeated admission
  preserves the same person and saved household evidence.

- **Childhood health episodes do not ask for an adult disclosure decision** A
  childhood health episode no longer stops time to ask the child for an adult
  disclosure decision. The decision uses age when the episode began.

- **The classmate from your childhood is no longer born on your birthday** A
  grown character's summarized childhood gives them a parent, a classmate and a
  teacher. The classmate was born on the player's exact birthday in every save,
  the parent exactly 28 years earlier to the day and the teacher exactly 30, so
  every new life had a stranger sharing the player's birthday. New games now
  give each of them a birthday of their own. The classmate is born within half a
  year either side of the player, the parent 22 to 39 years earlier, and the
  teacher 24 to 56. These ranges are chosen for the game, not measured. Saved
  games keep the dates they were created with.

- **Growing up no longer invents a sick relative** A child who shared a home
  could be handed a scene about illness in the house, or a teenager a scene
  about caring for someone, even though nobody in the household had ever been
  recorded as sick or in need of care. The scene simply assumed it because other
  people lived there. Those two situations now wait for a real cause: a named
  person in the home and a recorded illness or care need. Until the life has
  one, the growing-up years offer other moments instead.

- **Childhood moments are remembered in the past tense** The choices you make as
  a child were saved in the present tense ("You ask Riley how they know the
  story is true"), so the Journal switched tense in the middle of a paragraph.
  They now read as memories: "You asked Riley how they knew the story was true."
  People also "call" each other now instead of "ringing" each other. The
  spelling check also missed a British spelling that ended a sentence inside
  quotes, as in a declined favor or a rule the game has not modeled yet. It
  catches those now, and the ones it had let through are fixed.

- **A new character can be created again** Creating a character failed with an
  invalid citizenship history error, because the starting citizenship was dated
  to the start date instead of the earlier World the character is built into. It
  is now dated to the day the character enters the World.

- **Civic attendance follows the town’s recorded council calendar.** Quarterly
  civic reviews bind attendance to the latest council meeting held in that town
  during the quarter and date it on the meeting. A still-scheduled meeting is
  eligible only on the review date. Canceled, future and past unheld meetings
  produce no attendance. Contacts keep their existing route, and saved
  attendance retains its meeting identity. Saved attendance keeps the same
  meeting ID and date after reload.

- **Worlds no longer stop when a clemency petition comes due** A saved clemency
  petition schedules its next step on the world's clock, but the clock had no
  handler for it, so the first petition to come due stopped time. Watched worlds
  in Louisiana and Indiana ended about 18 months in. The world clock now carries
  the clemency petition handler.

- **Show progress when retrying a failed update check** Retrying an update check
  now shows the new attempt's progress instead of keeping an earlier offline or
  failure message on screen while preparation is running. A failure in the new
  attempt remains visible and the current game stays playable. Canceling
  background preparation is reported as canceled, with the current game
  retained, rather than showing a generic failure after a safe cancellation.

- **Correct update status and reuse the update workspace** The private game
  client recognizes an installed update instead of asking you to press a missing
  install button. Installation shows progress and explains when it cannot
  finish, so you can retry. Compatible game updates reuse one preparation folder
  instead of creating another project copy each time. Unchanged verified
  dependencies and valid compiler cache are reused while update verification
  remains in place. Newly prepared game payloads retain current, waiting, active
  and rollback versions. Unreferenced payloads are retired only when their
  ownership receipt still matches every file. Older folders remain protected for
  separate review. Sources requiring the old pack installer remain playable if
  installed, but cannot create more project copies through automatic
  preparation.

- **Remove clock-only notices** Routine notices no longer repeat the date and
  time already shown in the room. Other outcome notices fade after a short
  interval and dismiss automatically.

- **A county's powers are no longer left blank** Before, the powers table left
  52 county powers unknown, so a county could debate things no county can
  decide, and nobody could tell what a county was allowed to do. Now each county
  power is set from how county government works. State or federal matters such
  as marriage, abortion and court size are closed to a county, powers like
  zoning and policing are limited, and powers a state's home rule decides still
  follow that state's law. Counties no longer get a payroll-tax question.

- **College applications get an answer after a wait, and classes start in the
  fall** Applying to a college used to offer a place on the spot, and accepting
  it started classes that same day, even for a high-school senior in March, who
  then read that they were enrolled in two programs. Now a college answers an
  application after a wait, and an accepted place starts with the fall term once
  high school is over. Until then the place is not counted as a program you are
  enrolled in and no tuition is due. A senior can apply from the start of their
  last year of high school. The college screen no longer says where its listings
  came from.

- **Committee reports retain recorded attendance** The shared committee driver
  supplies attendance counted from its complete recorded roll call. A committee
  rule based on members present uses that count; absent members remain in the
  vote record, and appointed-member rules retain their compiled denominator.

- **Large worlds keep smaller saves without losing a member's views** The
  principles generated for officeholders before play use a compact form in new
  save files. Loading reconstructs each recorded view with the same person,
  principle, date, provenance and identifier. Older saves still open, and a
  principle changed during play keeps its full record.

- **Saves stay smaller as the country's officeholders weigh their careers** The
  temperament a person has from their upbringing, written down the first time a
  decision of theirs needs it, uses a compact form in new save files. Loading
  rebuilds each record with the same person, trait, value, date, note and
  identifier, so every trait reads the same after a save as before it. Older
  saves still open, and a temperament that changed during play keeps its full
  record.

- **Open missing employer accounts from recorded comparables** At opening, an
  active paid employer without an existing cash position or annualizable payroll
  can use the mean recorded spendable cash of active paid employers with the
  same saved classification. Each donor is counted once, including saved zero
  balances. Existing positions are preserved. The opening pass uses the existing
  comparable-cash reader and canonical account writer. Its donor snapshot is
  fixed before admitting estimates, so a newly estimated account cannot become
  another estimate's donor in the same pass. Without a recorded matching cohort,
  the cash gap remains explicit; no balance or cash receipt is invented. Save
  and reload retain the same accounts.

- **Completed activities enter the shared service consequence path** Completed
  scheduled activities dispatch their saved participants and completion event to
  the shared law consequence engine.

- **Completed shifts use the shared paycheck writer** Completed shifts now post
  through the same compensation period writer as town and weekly job pay, with
  the existing federal and state withholding. Saved work events and earned terms
  identify each shift; completed work is paid without an inferred sickness
  absence deduction.

- **Background congressional seats follow their saved district views** When no
  played or separately recorded contest decides a congressional seat, its party
  outcome now follows the world's saved seat lean with a disclosed incumbency
  game assumption. The former random incumbent-return and party-flip rolls no
  longer decide those seats. Candidate slates, primaries, money, and
  person-level campaign quality still need their own records.

- **A member of Congress is paid for the seat** A player who won a seat in
  Congress earned only what their old job paid. From January 3, the seat is now
  the member's job, paid weekly, and it ends with the term. Other jobs are left
  as they were until the rules on outside work are on file. The Government
  screen told a new member only that their House district was not recorded. It
  now shows the district of the seat they hold. Pay is the same placeholder
  office salary every office uses until real salaries are researched.

- **A legislator in any state can sponsor a change to their own constitution**
  The constitutional changes screen offered sponsorship only to a member of the
  California Legislature or Congress. A seated member of any state's legislature
  can now sponsor a proposal to change their own state's constitution, and the
  proposal names that state's own legislature as its sponsor.

- **A state amendment waits when its actual chamber lacks a quorum** Ended
  legislative tenures and absences no longer cause the amendment producer to
  submit an invalid rollcall. The proposal stays in consideration until an
  actual quorum can vote; the constitutional quorum guard remains unchanged.

- **Missing election counts leave the contest pending** An automatic contest
  without a supported electorate no longer invents ballots or names a winner.
  Supplied election results still use the existing validation.

- **The conversation box shows every reply without a scrollbar, and says what
  you just said** When you talk to someone in a room, the conversation box is
  now sized from its own replies instead of a fixed guess. It stands
  bottom-center when nobody is behind it, docks beside the person you are facing
  when someone is, and keeps clear of faces. It grows wider before it grows
  taller, and in a wide box the replies sit in columns. At 1200 by 720, 1280 by
  720 and 1440 by 900 every reply is on screen with no scrollbar inside the box.
  While you are talking in the room, the morning note and the recap step aside
  and come back, still unread, when the conversation ends. In a quiet room at
  home, the box now shows what you just said above the answer. Before, it showed
  only the answer, because it looked for the turn in an opening scene that a
  quiet room does not have.

- **A conversation shows the words you actually said** When you answer someone
  in a conversation with a recorded statement, such as denying a reporter's
  question, the transcript now shows the words you said instead of a summary of
  what you chose to do. The words stay the same after you save and reload.

- **Cost-law fixtures respect legislature session windows** Cost-law regression
  fixtures file on the existing legislature calendar before their intended
  operative date. They retain the actual executive decision and refuse a filing
  opportunity after the session closes. Multiple fixture bills file at one
  existing opportunity and share a forward clock through scheduled hearings,
  floor readings and the actual executive desk. Independent runs retain the
  single-law baseline without rewinding the world or bypassing a legal gate.
  Budget cash and recorded-flow assertions retain their existing contract.

- **Council agendas reuse recorded history lookups** Council agendas reuse the
  existing history indexes when reading their own measures, rejection votes and
  pending enactments. The lookups keep record order, the supplied council
  snapshot, cooldowns and operative dates. Legislative timing, member choices
  and legal session gates retain their existing rules.

- **Council readings retain their declared interval and shared session guard**
  An early council reading explains its delay in the compiled procedure's units,
  including whole intervening days. The shared floor writer still enforces the
  date from the saved previous reading. Supplied member ballots pass through the
  shared session-end guard without rebuilding an unused story roster. The
  clock's existing confidant reader uses the shared history indexes for
  household members, recorded interactions and deaths. It reads the same records
  and retains the same recipient order and dated exclusions without scanning all
  of those histories for each person.

- **A save with a town council race opens again** After town councils were given
  their own names, a save holding a council race from before the change would
  not open, and the game only said it could not be opened just now. Those saves
  open again, and the race is still for the same seat.

- **Your county board votes its levy with every member seated and in session** A
  county board now votes under a rule pack that seats the same number of members
  the county's law sets, so a board of seven is no longer refused a vote for
  having more members than a fixed five. A county board is also no longer shut
  for the year on the day its state's legislature adjourns, so a levy heard in
  April is voted in April in every state.

- **The browser tests load again** Before, the citizenship data file was
  imported without the JSON import attribute, so no browser test could load. Now
  it is imported the way every other data file is, and the browser tests load.

- **A county's formal name is the record's own string again** A county's formal
  label had the state appended to it, so the Census row "Baltimore city" was
  carried as "Baltimore city, Maryland". The state is already shown separately,
  and the formal label exists to be the source's exact string. Towns are
  unchanged: their formal label still carries the state.

- **Coursework you shared with a classmate ends when you leave the course**
  After a person left school, the Journal could still say a shared piece of
  coursework "is due, it counts as one piece, and the other half is not done."
  The coursework also kept one of the two slots a life has for things that are
  still going on. Once the course is left, whether it ended, the person withdrew
  or transferred, or they completed it, the coursework is no longer open.

- **The weekly court and clemency pass no longer slows with a long life** Each
  week the game reads every open court case, every sentence and every clemency
  request. It used to scan the whole history of the world for each one, so a
  tenth year of play spent far longer in court than the first. It now reads
  cases, sentences and requests from an index that grows with history. No
  outcome changes.

- **Coverage records enrollment without assigning a population mortality effect
  to each person** Gaining or losing Medicaid expansion coverage still records
  the person's eligibility and enrollment. It no longer applies a
  population-level mortality finding to that person's death strain or
  reschedules a fatal illness during the monthly coverage pass. Existing saves
  retain their coverage records.

- **The creator shows your whole figure, and the morning note waits for the
  tour** On the "How you look" step, your whole figure now stands above the
  Reset appearance, Undo and Begin buttons at any window height, and each choice
  (Feminine, Average, a hair color) sits on a dark backing so it reads clearly
  over the room. When a new life begins, the morning note no longer opens over
  the opening tour; it appears once the tour is over and play has begun.

- **Creator navigation stays in view** The character, place, and custom
  background steps now keep Back and Next together at the bottom of the creator.
  Next stays visible while a step is unfinished and becomes available when its
  required choices are ready. Back returns to the previous creator step; the
  first step offers Return to title.

- **Creator primary actions show their approved corner while pressed** Creator
  navigation primary actions now show the existing Kit13 brass corner mark while
  activated. Resting and hovered controls remain unmarked. This restores the
  approved button interaction without changing the Creator steps, question
  routing, art or menu layout.

- **Creator towns show county and population** Before: Town subtitles repeated
  the state, and helper text repeated list status. After: Town subtitles use
  recorded county and place population. Estimate provenance stays in canonical
  reference data. List helper copy is removed. Replaces: State-only town
  subtitles and place-list helper paragraphs. Impact: Presentation only; no new
  World records, clock changes, or household cohort edits.

- **Crime history reads the homes and ties recorded at the time.** Crime
  exposure, reporting, and offender readers now use the supplied date and
  history boundary. Later moves, relationships, school records, and police
  referrals no longer change earlier evidence. Sampling stops at the current
  date, so an unfinished month cannot create a future incident. Missing
  historical residence remains unknown; this repair adds no researched crime
  rates.

- **Coverage records retain their saved lease-renewal cause** Crisis validation
  recognizes existing resource-flow terms when checking a coverage record's
  causal parent. Missing parents and parents at or after the coverage record's
  sequence remain invalid. Lease renewal and coverage records are preserved
  through saved-world reload.

- **The note about a decision only you can make goes away when it is over** The
  note saying something came up that only you can decide now goes away once that
  decision is made or its window closes. Before, a governor who let a federal
  disaster request lapse kept seeing the note, with its "Go to it" button, for
  months after there was nothing left to decide.

- **Cuffs keep the sleeve color instead of taking on skin tone** Skin tone no
  longer bleeds onto sleeves and cuffs where cloth meets exposed hands and
  wrists, so a person's shirt keeps its own color at the edge.

- **New cash-bail charges reuse comparable saved charge amounts** New money-bail
  charges can use amounts recorded in this game's comparable charges when an
  operative numeric law term is absent. The amount and donor provenance are
  saved before the existing pretrial payment writer consumes them. The
  first-charge empty-donor input remains unresolved; no research median is
  promoted to court authority. An optional recorded charge cohort uses the
  shared applicability contract to match court, offense class, and region
  exactly. Missing cohort evidence stays unsupported; older callers and saved
  amounts retain their existing behavior.

- **Dark clothes keep their color along antialiased edges** Partially
  transparent cloth edges now take the selected garment color instead of
  retaining bright source colors around dark sleeves. Skin, other garment parts
  and the original silhouette remain protected.

- **Earlier births retain their recorded household** A birth or adoption now
  reads the parent's household on the event date. A later move does not supply
  the child's earlier residence, and missing earlier membership remains
  unrecorded.

- **Starting wage terms follow their effective dates** Wage terms already in
  force use the same dated legal-text reader as laws passed in play. Historical
  queries exclude later enactments, court rulings and constitutional actions,
  preserving the law that applied when work was done.

- **Wage tax authority respects its recorded dates** Wage-income authority uses
  its own jurisdiction and sourced validity dates. Missing or unverified
  evidence remains unavailable; this adds no tax rate or duplicate withholding.

- **Dates say what the record says, and study partners give their own reasons**
  A date the game could not actually hold no longer arrives as a different real
  one. February 30 was reaching you as "March 2", a year before 0100 was shown
  as a year in the 1900s, and a year that was simply not on record came through
  as the first four letters of the words saying so. All three now come back
  exactly as they were written, so an unusual date reads as unusual instead of
  quietly becoming a plausible one. When someone you have agreed to study with
  says they would rather settle the outline first, or read everything before
  deciding, that is now the reason given by a person who thinks things through.
  It used to belong to the ones who act on impulse, which is the opposite of
  what the words say.

- **Day advances avoid repeated work for distant people and jurisdictions** The
  Day clock and its screens now reuse county and territory identity indexes
  instead of searching every place on repeated reads. Congressional party reads
  index seat-roll history by person. State executive reads build their
  organization, tenure, vacancy, and work lookups once per read rather than
  scanning each history for every state. Saved place, seat, and office
  identities are unchanged. The screen prepares appearance snapshots for people
  present in the current scene instead of every distant legislator in the world;
  a person's saved appearance remains available when they come into view.

- **Keep daily notes clear of meetings and person records** The recap and
  morning note wait while meeting controls or a person's record are open.
  Closing that panel brings the unread note back.

- **Days move faster and saved games open sooner** Pressing Day no longer
  re-checks the whole world after every day. The game now checks only what that
  day added or changed, and still checks the whole world when you save and when
  you open a saved game. Opening a saved game is much faster in a long life. The
  check of each person's remembered feelings, goals and values used to compare
  every record with every other record; it now compares each record only with
  the same person's own. Several everyday lookups also stopped searching every
  person and every place: finding a person's housemates, colleagues, club
  members, home town and Congress member now goes straight to the records that
  can match.

- **A life in Washington, D.C. goes on past the first mayoral election** Every
  life in the District of Columbia stopped on November 2, 2026, the day before
  the first mayoral election: each press of a time button failed with "The
  contest was not run in the jurisdiction this office governs." The game checked
  the Mayor's election against a district-wide stand-in rather than the
  District's one government, which is where the election is actually held. The
  Mayor's term is now dated against the District's own government, and time
  passes through the election and into the new term.

- **Default clock advances carry scheduled consequences** Day and minute
  advances now use the existing complete world handler registry by default.
  Registry initialization keeps the same validation and routine composition
  cache in modules that can load before the clock.

- **Deferred groundwater caps no longer change irrigation outcomes** Groundwater
  caps remain out of play. Their outcome row, research, starting levels and
  recorded laws are retained, but the deferred link is not consumed by outcome
  calculations or reported as an active law-effect path.

- **The game stops describing everywhere as if it were one town** Several
  screens had a single place, state or school year written into them, so they
  described the rest of the country with the wrong details or refused to
  describe it at all. The economic panel now names each figure's own geography
  as the source names it, instead of saying Fayette County and Lexington-Fayette
  wherever you live. An ethics complaint is routed by the state it belongs to
  rather than by the word "Kentucky" appearing in a name, so a complaint filed
  anywhere reaches the right body, or honestly says nobody has recorded one. The
  school directory can read any published year, not only the two that were
  written in. Conversation subjects and the municipal screen ask the records
  instead of a fixed list. Underneath, a state's identity now answers the same
  way whichever path created it, and each state's candidacy rules cite the
  constitution they were actually read from and the date that text was seen — so
  no state's requirements can be published under another state's source. Where a
  rule has not been read, the game still refuses to let someone file rather than
  waving them through.

- **Someone who thinks things through no longer behaves like someone who acts on
  impulse** People described as acting on impulse were the ones insisting on an
  answer before they would agree to anything, and people described as thinking
  things through were the ones going along with a change on the spot. Each
  person's card said the opposite of how they behaved. They now behave the way
  they are described, in the places their temperament is read: arrangements
  being renegotiated, plans to study together, family plans, and keeping in
  touch.

- **Keep the private game current while its hub stays open** The private hub now
  checks the selected game and main for new builds while it remains open. When a
  verified update is ready, it installs automatically only at the game's own
  title screen; an open life keeps its existing build. An unchanged GitHub
  revision returns after a remote-head check, without fetching or rebuilding the
  same game again. Preparing a code update no longer verifies the same artwork
  snapshot twice before publication. The full artwork check still runs before
  the new build is offered, and the hub checks it again before switching. The
  hub also shows the short build revision beside the release number, so a code
  update is visible even when the release number remains the same. Desktop
  release checks now inspect the saved database at its current schema version
  instead of attempting to reopen it as an older version. The package build
  gives the TypeScript check a 4 GiB heap on hosted runners.

- **A life that has always lived somewhere has always lived in its district
  too** A character who had lived in the same town since childhood was treated
  as having lived in that town's legislative district for no time at all,
  because the district clock started on the day the life was written rather than
  on the day the records say the life came to live there. The two clocks now
  agree: both read the same household history, so a forty-year-old Sitka
  resident has been in their house district for forty years, not for nothing,
  and does not have to wait out a residence requirement they already meet. Where
  a town is split across several districts — Duluth, Columbus, Lincoln — the
  game still refuses to say which district its resident lives in, because it
  genuinely cannot. What changed is what it says: it now tells you the district
  is the thing it cannot establish, instead of saying it never recorded when you
  came to live here, which was not true.

- **Filing for a seat that is filled by district** In states where a legislative
  seat belongs to a numbered district, the list of offices would say you could
  run and then refuse to take your name, because nothing on the screen let you
  say which district you were running in. Choosing one of those offices now
  opens the district seat beside it, showing the district your character is on
  record as living in and since when, and your name goes in for that seat. Where
  the records do not say which district your town sits in, the screen says so
  instead of choosing one for you.

- **Council members and candidates drawn from town have homes and jobs** Before,
  a resident picked to sit on the town council, or to run in a town election,
  sometimes came from a household that had no home and whose adults had no jobs,
  unlike everyone else in town. Now that household gets what every other
  household in town has: its children in school, its working adults in jobs, and
  a home.

- **A city council no longer passes state laws** A town council used to take up
  questions that only the state can settle, such as whether the state should
  have an income tax, how many terms state legislators may serve, or who draws
  the legislature's district lines. It now votes only on questions that are its
  own to decide, such as its budget, its transit, its zoning and its police
  oversight board. The same rule holds in every state, in the District of
  Columbia and in the territories. An ordinance a town passed on a state
  question in an older save stays on the record but no longer counts as the law
  there. Where a state has no law on a question and leaves its towns free to
  act, a town's own ordinance now counts in that town. Where a state bars its
  towns from acting, the state's rule still holds.

- **Completed pay uses its recorded public payer** Completed-work transfers use
  the canonical payer recorded at the saved earning cutoff, including a public
  account distinct from the employer. The work and assessment retain the actual
  employer; wrong-payer refusals, authority, terms, amount, event and historical
  availability checks remain intact.

- **Completed shifts keep the terms earned** Pay for a completed shift uses the
  saved terms in force when that work finished. A later change on the same date
  does not rewrite the amount earned.

- **A won governorship says to qualify, and takes you to the button** A player
  who won a governorship read "An offer of work as Governor is waiting for your
  answer" on every screen, and the line opened Work, where there is no way to
  accept it. The answer is "Qualify for the term" on Campaigns, and a player who
  never found it lost the term on the day it began. The line now says "You won
  the race for Governor. Qualify for the term under Campaigns before it begins
  on …", and pressing it opens Campaigns with the Qualify button on screen. Once
  qualified it stops asking and says when the term begins. A term whose start
  date passed without a qualification is no longer listed as waiting.

- **Your own election day stops the clock and tells you the result** Passing a
  day or a week, or letting the weeks run on, used to run straight through your
  own election, so a win could arrive with nothing said. Time now stops the
  morning after your election, the report says whether you won and by how much,
  and the result goes into your journal.

- **Election night adds up** The shares on an election result were each rounded
  on their own, so a two-way race could print 72.0% and 28.1% and leave you
  adding up to 100.1. Nothing was wrong with the count; the table now totals
  what it should.

- **Election night keeps the floor the campaign kept** Every candidate holds at
  least one percent of support for the whole of a campaign, but the result on
  election night could still print a share below that — a runaway race finished
  99.99 to 0.01, which is one vote in ten thousand. The count now respects the
  same floor the campaign did.

- **A new game no longer stops when a local board has nobody to seat** A new
  game could stop while opening when a county board or town government had no
  resident who could take a seat. The seats now stay open for a later pass
  instead.

- **Appropriations keep their written dates** An appropriation opens when its
  law takes effect and keeps the last day written in its unchanged clause. A
  later appropriation can fund one enacted program authorized by the same
  government, using its own amount.

- **Money passed by Congress, a council or on a governor's signature gets
  spent** Spending passed by Congress, by a city or county council, or signed by
  a governor now goes to the office that spends it, the same way money from a
  state legislature always did. A law that takes effect later reaches that
  office on its effective date. A state transit law filed by another lawmaker is
  funded like one the player files. In a watched world, those offices now commit
  that money and pay it out of the government's own account. An office that
  chooses "Commit nothing for now" is asked about the same money again a month
  later, for as long as the money can still be spent. Before, that choice closed
  the question for good.

- **Watching a world, and every scripted run, gets the long-save speed-up too.**
  A world nobody is playing, and every scripted playtest, now passes time as
  fast as the player's own clock. In an observed Columbus save the second year
  took 31 seconds and now takes about 3. Day-by-day advances check the world
  once at the end instead of after every change, and only the test suite and
  development builds keep the slow proof that no scheduled event rewrites the
  world it was given.

- **A conversation updates the person's shared history** After you exchange a
  line in a room or answer an invitation, the person's record reflects that you
  spoke. Repeat exchanges on the same day stay one conversation. What you do for
  each other still determines how the relationship changes.

- **Filed excise taxes use their adopted terms** An excise proposal filed from
  an eligible office now records the tax question and its exact levy terms.
  Declaring a taxable occurrence uses the shared tax path to assess what is
  owed. The existing payment system collects only available money, and loading a
