  save or repeating the declaration does not assess it twice.

- **The fairness ordinance check agrees with home rule again** Two new rules
  disagreed in one check. Tennessee and Arkansas bar their towns from passing a
  fairness ordinance, and states that follow Dillon's rule now withhold powers
  their legislature never granted. The check that towns elsewhere may still pass
  one now compares each town with what it could do if its state already covered
  the grounds, so a Dillon's rule state no longer reads as a fairness bar.
  Nothing in play changes.

- **Family readings reuse unchanged recorded pay dependencies** Family-context
  cohorts retain their existing records and estimates when new withholding flows
  do not change recorded compensation. Compensation changes, revised histories,
  household and work changes still invalidate the reading. Future dated records
  and the existing year boundary retain their rebuild rules. When a cohort
  rebuild is needed, repeated reads of one person's household context are shared
  only within that build. Existing sample and membership order, household
  representatives and dated dependency checks are preserved. Family pay proxies
  reuse the existing parent-first household selection to read donor members
  without computing unused pay, work or congregation context. Household cohort
  representatives use that same selection and the existing adult age cutoff
  without computing unused income, type or congregation facts. Recorded parents,
  membership order, dated residence and cohort inputs remain unchanged; this
  introduces no retained cache or new family facts.

- **A new character's family no longer shares their birthday** Every parent,
  brother, sister, housemate and classmate a new game wrote for the character
  was born on the character's own birthday, a whole number of years apart. Now
  each has a birthday of their own. Brothers and sisters are still two to four
  years apart. This applies to new games. A game already in progress keeps the
  family it has.

- **A long life's memories no longer go blank after the parents die** An older
  life whose parents died and who had no brothers or sisters used to show no
  family memories for decades before the start. Those years now fall to the
  life's own partner and child. The fixed template family is gone. A save made
  before this still opens. A world rebuilt from an older setup now draws its
  family the new way.

- **A passed law that sets aside money in its own words now provides it** Some
  bills set aside money in their own words, such as a fund for replacing water
  service lines or support for households' internet bills. When one of these
  passed, the state still could not spend the money, and the law's page said
  nothing acted on that section. The money is now available to spend like any
  other appropriation. A section that only allows spending up to a limit now
  says it provides no money itself, and how much later laws have provided
  against it.

- **Farm subsidy caps no longer invent savings in the federal forecast** A farm
  subsidy cap no longer subtracts a historical percentage from the federal
  agriculture forecast. Saved farm-authorized commitments now pay the recorded
  recipient through the existing cash writer, limited by the adopted annual
  dollar cap after that recipient's actual same-year payments. Budget outturn
  uses transferred cash, and payment records retain the law and term sources
  across Save/Continue. Ordinary farm eligibility and recipient-commitment
  production remains unsupported; the game does not invent recipients from
  aggregate research.

- **Federal spending readers use the amounts in adopted bill text** Federal
  spending readers now use the final annual amounts written in a bill, including
  recorded starting law terms. They no longer substitute the size of an old
  debt-limit deal, a historical defense growth rate, or an old rail ridership
  forecast. Missing amounts, recipients, or delivery records remain unsupported.
  A defense appropriation is compared with a complete recorded year of federal
  defense spending. The preserved federal defense reader now records the bill’s
  annual target against its complete saved spending base, with its law
  attribution. Debt offsets use adopted dollars over recorded total spending.
  Federal categories retain their existing order and exports through one shared
  list. Foreign aid now sets only International Affairs from its adopted annual
  amount and recorded base; it never multiplies federal aid receipts for state
  or local governments. Debt-limit offsets use the adopted amount over recorded
  eligible categories, including valid zero-spending lines. Their annual
  difference restores the deficit-to-borrowing link using the existing sourced
  GDP denominator. Final annual rail terms reach the existing appropriation
  writer and payment ledger, with the same federal account and rail program key.
  Authority is recorded separately from actual cash and completed service.

- **Federal income tax has one receipt path** The federal top-income-tax law no
  longer has a second treasury-share estimate. Income-tax receipts continue
  through saved paycheck liabilities and payment allocations into the common
  government account and monthly cash settlement. Replaying a paycheck or
  reopening its save preserves the same allocations, transfers and receipts.
  Other federal-law sizing remains separate work.

- **Federal opening cash follows the average state budget ratio** New games
  estimate federal opening cash from federal annual outlays and the average
  state ratio of opening balances and reserves to annual spending. Existing
  saves keep their recorded amounts.

- **Federal payment proof prepares its shared adopted authority before cash
  checks** The federal state-payment regression fixture prepares its existing
  shared canonical adopted-law authority in the normal suite-setup hook. The
  first cash test no longer includes all cold legislative and executive setup
  work. The existing project hook limit and each case's original timeout remain
  intact. Payment, budget credit, provenance and reload assertions are
  unchanged. No payment writer, legal amount or adoption validation changes.

- **Federal privacy compliance retains its saved cost and law** Federal privacy
  compliance now saves its actual quarterly cost and controlling law stamp on
  business books, using a stable cost size for each world and place. Repeal
  stops the next quarter's cost without changing earlier snapshots.

- **Paid federal programs reach the Treasury books** The Treasury records the
  actual posted installments of enacted federal program appropriations,
  including the law and payment records behind each cost. Spending authority
  alone adds no expense; a completed payment increases the recorded outlay and
  borrowing by the amount actually transferred.

- **Federal top-rate laws use their adopted rate** A federal law changing the
  top income tax rate now taxes the top bracket at its adopted rate, instead of
  always using 39.6 percent. The change begins at the existing tax-year
  boundary. The other brackets and deductions keep their starting schedule. A
  missing, conflicting or unsupported adopted rate leaves the withholding
  unsupported rather than inventing a rate. Repealing the change restores the
  starting schedule.

- **Candidate filing day is published in the newspaper** On the day candidates
  file for Congress and the state legislature, the slate filing is now published
  as public news. The front page used to say nothing had been published after a
  day with fifty or more filings.

- **Filing for your own district counts your whole life there** A lifelong
  resident could be told on the office list that they could stand for their
  state legislative seat, then be refused when they filed for their own district
  with "lived here 1 month". The list dated how long they had lived in the
  district from their household record; the filing dated it from the day the
  world was written. The filing now dates the district the world recorded for
  their home the same way the list does, so the two agree. Found in Waterbury,
  Nebraska, and in Massachusetts, Minnesota and Alaska lives.

- **Finding liability survives missing cash without inventing payment accounts**
  Findings no longer create a respondent's cash account or a government
  recipient while ordering repayment. An actual order with an existing creditor
  retains its unpaid obligation when payer cash is missing. Recorded payments
  reduce that same debt. Existing funded repayments retain their amount and
  record order. A missing creditor remains unsupported.

- **A teenager's first job pays, and job offers no longer wait forever or lapse
  unseen** A teenager's first job now pays every week from the day it is taken,
  at a placeholder hourly rate, and ends when an adult job begins. An unanswered
  offer lapses after its reply window, and an employer follows up once or
  withdraws after a missed start. A job whose shift overlaps one already held is
  refused. Skipping days stops on the last day to answer an offer, the day
  overview names that date, and the life record keeps the offer and how it
  ended. A plain day skip now also brings the employer's answer to a job
  application.

- **First leases follow their recorded owners** First leases use a unique active
  person or organization owner recorded for the home. Homes without recorded
  title use the existing representative ownership mixture across their saved
  roster. Household owners and co-owners receive no invented recipient. Existing
  leases keep their landlord and saved identities.

- **New leases use household size for bedrooms** New town leases use the
  recorded household size to assign representative bedrooms. A home keeps its
  recorded bedrooms and landlord on renewal. Existing leases keep their amounts
  and identities until their ordinary renewal.

- **Rural transit funding reaches paid service** An enacted rural transit
  appropriation creates one saved spending authority from its effective date.
  Executive commitments and transit payments share its amount. Payments still
  require cash. A posted operating payment records exact modeled vehicle-service
  hours at the game's provisional cost and publishes those hours. Maintenance
  payments do not create service hours. A separate modeled local ride is
  recorded in one existing resident's history. Only a later encounter with a
  public report supports that resident's private reaction to the funding
  decision. No car status, travel-time change, or election effect is inferred.
  Reading the published funding report from News takes time and records what the
  controlled resident learned. Their Journal keeps that public report separate
  from the modeled rider's private trip.

- **Starting graduated tax schedules reach ordinary paycheck withholding.**
  Nineteen places carry their read 2026 single-filer deductions and complete
  bracket schedules on the existing income-tax adoption and graduated-tax law
  rows. The dated reader supplies those schedules to the existing payroll tax
  writer, retaining both starting law identities. Enacted rates and reshaping
  keep precedence. Unread deductions remain explicit source gaps.

- **Starting fiscal terms reach pension budgets and flat income-tax
  withholding.** Starting pension laws carry their cited full-actuarial
  contribution share into the existing budget calculation. Fifteen states with
  sourced flat income taxes carry their 2026 rate and taxable-income threshold
  into the existing paycheck assessment and collection path. Existing payments
  remain unchanged on replay. Graduated schedules and unrelated starting-law
  questions keep their existing behavior; this does not supply missing category
  or bracket contracts.

- **The game loads again after the crime-rates rename** The pressure layer still
  imported the old name of the town police-log rate, so the game failed to load
  on main. It now reads the renamed rate.

- **Childhood pacing follows its anchor budget** The clock uses the expected
  number of anchors in each accepted childhood budget instead of drawing a
  count. Steps remain aligned to their own age band and stop at its boundary.

- **A meeting due now no longer freezes the day** A life could get stuck on one
  day. A fourteen-year-old in Wellsboro, Pennsylvania had a meeting she had
  arranged starting at six o'clock. Time will not step over a commitment like
  that, and a child's moment offered no way to go to it. So "Let the year run
  on" came back unmoved, 1,764 times in one lives run. The same happened to a
  fifteen-year-old in Washington, D.C. Children now get the same "Attend" choice
  beside the moment that adults got. Going to the meeting lets the year run on
  again. A commitment whose time has already passed now says so plainly and can
  be let go, instead of showing an error with no button. That happens with
  meetings an older version carried past. When there are several, one choice
  lets go of all of them.

- **People sit in the room's chairs and remain selectable when it fills** People
  in place pictures now follow the room's furniture and the conversation.
  Sitting figures meet the painted cushions at the room's scale. Additional
  present people remain in an “Also here” list when no supported position is
  available. Counter staff keep the service position when a customer appears
  first in the room roster. The grocery cashier stands on the hidden floor
  behind the counter, with the lower body covered by the painted counter.

- **Winning a legislative seat in Maine, Georgia and other generated states now
  seats you, and swearing-in is an event** A player who won a state House or
  Senate seat in a state whose legislature the game generates, rather than one
  of the nine it has written out, was never seated. This was true in Maine and
  Georgia, among others. They saw no "Before you take office" section, the seat
  never began, and Your office read "You hold no office in this life yet" beside
  a House role. Now these winners wait for the term like everyone else and are
  seated on its first day. Their office opens onto the legislature's work.
  Swearing-in is now something the player does. From the first day of the term,
  a legislator or governor sees a Swearing-in section that describes the
  ceremony. Taking the oath is recorded as a public event. The ceremony follows
  a general description until each state's own is researched. Not taking the
  oath does not yet withhold the office.

- **A generated person's name now matches the gender the world gave them** The
  owner met a man the game called "your dad" and named Maria, and said it was
  not a one-off. It was not: on an adult start in Lexington, 29 of the 72 people
  generated across 24 saves carried a given name from the opposite pool, and in
  one seated world 237 of the 516 people in Congress did. Nothing was wrong with
  the name pools. Sixteen routes — the parent, peer and teacher of a summarized
  earlier life, the seated Congress and its turnover, party chapters and party
  evolution, state executives and their turnover, bill sponsors, office
  continuity, campaign contacts and the opposing campaign's field lead — drew a
  name on one stream and a gender on another and never introduced them, so each
  person was a coin flip. The draw that honors a gender had existed since the
  decision that settled the ordering, but honoring it was optional and these
  routes did not. The two draws are now one call, so a route cannot take the
  name without the identity that shaped it, and a test fails if any route pairs
  them apart again. The same counts after the repair are 0 of 72 and 0 of 516.
  The seated Congress half of that arrived separately, through the client line's
  own versioned member names; this change is the other fifteen routes, and the
  test now covers both. A save written before the repair still rebuilds the
  world it recorded. The earlier life's three people are drawn the old way when
  the save says so, the same way the seated Congress already gated its member
  names, so nobody's existing character is quietly renamed. A new game declares
  the repair. Nothing is read backwards: a gender still decides a name and a
  name never decides a gender. Names that are genuinely shared across genders
  are a separate question, now filed for research, because drawing strictly from
  one pool cannot produce them and guessing a rate would be its own invention.

- **Generated officials look like the person their name describes** Officials
  the game creates (a President, Vice President, governors, lawmakers and
  candidates) now get who they are first, and then a name that fits. Before, a
  Vice President named Alan could be drawn with a woman's body.

- **The schools you went to have names** Looking up your own past showed "Local
  Elementary School", "Local Middle School" and "Local High School". A generated
  childhood now went to schools with names — named for your town, a county, a
  piece of local landscape or a figure from American history, the way schools
  actually are. They are the same three schools every time you load that
  character.

- **A commitment you have no way to carry out can now be given up** Committing a
  campaign week booked sessions at places the life had no authored way of
  reaching. The calendar was right to refuse to carry them out, and time was
  right to refuse to step over a commitment that had been confirmed rather than
  penciled in. Together those two correct rules left no legal move at all: the
  clock stopped on the morning the week was committed and never moved again, for
  the rest of that life. A commitment that cannot be performed now offers to be
  given up. Giving up takes no time and spends nothing; it records the choice,
  clears the commitment and any journey booked for it, and lets the days go on.
  Nothing is faked, and nothing is treated as having happened. This is a way out
  of the trap rather than a repair of what caused it. Whether a campaign session
  should instead become something a life can actually reach is a separate
  question and is still open.

- **Going to a Saturday invitation you had not answered takes you there** In
  Lewiston, Jenna pressed to go to a Saturday afternoon at Connor Yoder's home
  and was told "You have no way to get from Home to Connor Yoder's home yet."
  Saying yes to the invitation books the short trip there, but going to it
  straight from Places or the calendar skipped the yes, so there was no trip.
  Going now counts as the yes: it books the trip, and the afternoon is spent at
  the asker's home.

- **Government events retain their governing law** Saved ward-map changes and
  term-limit restriction events retain canonical governing-law attribution
  through the shared history writer. Existing decisions, boundaries and amounts
  remain unchanged.

- **Local government news no longer reads its research notes** Reporters and
  news now say only who governs a town or county, without notes about where that
  reading came from.

- **Governors are elected in their state's own election years** Kentucky,
  Louisiana, Mississippi, New Jersey and Virginia now elect their governors in
  odd-numbered years, and Delaware, Indiana, Missouri, Montana, North Carolina,
  North Dakota, Utah and West Virginia elect in presidential years. Before this,
  every one of them held its governor's race in 2026. The years come from a
  nationwide calendar with each state's constitutional provision. The dates a
  term begins stay the game's own until they are verified. Not modeled, and
  marked so in the code: Louisiana's October primary. Its governor is elected on
  the November general election day of its odd years. A governor is now
  term-limited only where the state's law says so. Before this, a state whose
  law the game had not read got a limit drawn from the states it had read; now
  it has none until its law is read. Every state, the District and the five
  inhabited territories now has a place for its political culture, read when a
  person forms a view on a question. None carries a value yet; each waits on
  research, and until it arrives a place pulls nobody's views any way.

- **A sitting governor who runs again is your opponent** Filing for governor
  used to put you against one newly made-up rival, and the sitting governor
  quietly left the race. When the governor stands for another term, they are now
  the person you run against, and if they win they stay in office. An open seat
  still draws a new challenger.

- **A governor's decisions are no longer covered by an Office work card** Once a
  player took office as governor, an "Office work" card was drawn over the
  decisions waiting in Your office, and the office's programs, staff and
  casework floated over them too. Both now sit in the page below the decisions,
  so every choice a governor is asked to make can be read and pressed.

- **A governor's agenda can include infrastructure** A governor's first-year
  priorities and budget requests were drawn from thirteen kinds of public
  program. The legislature drafts bills in twenty. The seven missing were
  transit, bridges, broadband, water service lines, disaster recovery, utility
  resilience and critical infrastructure, so a governor could never set any of
  them as a priority, and money the legislature adopted for one reached the
  office titled "public work". A governor now draws from the same twenty the
  legislature does. Priorities already recorded in a save keep their subjects.
  The yearly note in a governor's office that no bill arrived also said the game
  "has not compiled" the state's legislature. For Nevada and Illinois that was
  untrue: both legislatures are compiled, and a player can hold a seat in them.
  What is missing is bills written for their other members to file. The note now
  says that.

- **A governor's race opening on an empty seat no longer ends the game.** When a
  sitting governor died or left office and the next race opened with nobody in
  the seat, the game stopped with an error and the life could not continue. The
  race now opens normally against the state whose office it is, and the record
  says the seat was vacant.

- **Governors you are not playing sign bills, and legislatures vote on
  overrides** A governor played by the game used to replay a veto written into
  the few bills legislatures file on their own, so almost every bill that
  reached the desk was vetoed and nothing became law. Every sitting governor now
  decides each bill for themselves, the same way you do as governor. In test
  worlds with no player governor, Nebraska and Alaska went from none of eighteen
  bills becoming law in three years to nine of eighteen. How often a governor
  should sign is still being researched, and the current rate is a placeholder.
  When a governor does veto a bill, the legislature now votes on overriding it,
  with each member deciding and the state's own override rule counting the
  result. Before, nobody ever tried. A player who sits in the chamber is never
  voted for; until they cast their own ballot they are recorded absent. A
  governor's office history no longer tells the player that the game has no
  bills written for their state; it says only that no bill reached the desk.

- **Governors in seven states take office on the day their constitution names**
  Alaska and Hawaii now seat a newly elected governor on the first Monday of
  December, a month after the vote, instead of in January. Delaware, Florida,
  Louisiana, Maine and Virginia seat theirs on the January day their own
  constitution names, such as the Saturday after the second Wednesday in
  Virginia. Every other state keeps the game's first Monday of January until its
  clause is read. A new check walks every governorship from the start of a new
  game to 2046. Each term begins the day the last one ends, so no state goes
  without a governor, and each election falls in the state's real years. North
  Carolina and Missouri now elect in 2028 and keep their governor from 2025 to 2029. Legislatures whose session rules have not been researched still meet
  every year and drop unfinished bills at adjournment. That is now labeled as
  the game's standing rule rather than the state's law.

- **Graduated tax laws no longer apply an unrelated fiscal-note multiplier**
  Public budgets retain actual saved statutory collections instead of applying
  another place's graduated-tax fiscal-note multiplier.

- **People's pronouns, past meetings and office names read correctly** A
  person's card uses their own pronouns ("She is your mom", not "They are your
  mom"). A conversation recalls what you said in your own voice ("You said you
  would go to the meeting"). Once a posted public meeting has happened, it no
  longer waits on you, and nobody asks whether you are going to it. Offices read
  the way people say them: "A seat in the Nebraska Legislature" in the list, and
  "Put your name in for mayor" on the button.

- **Grandparents share their family's surname** Each pair of grandparents now
  shares one surname. Your father's parents carry your family name, and your
  mother's parents carry the name she was born with. Before, all four
  grandparents could have four unrelated surnames.

- **A grown character's guardian is no longer "your guardian", and buying a home
  moves you out** A character who started a new game as a child kept their
  guardian's or parent's authority for life, and a twenty-seven-year-old who
  owned her own house still had her guardian listed at home as "your guardian".
  Now a grown character is not described as anybody's dependent. The person who
  raised them is still named for it: a parent as a parent, and a guardian as the
  guardian who raised you. A grown character who still lives with a parent or
  guardian and buys a home now moves into it, with a partner and their own
  children if those live with them. The parent or guardian stays where they
  were, and the Household list shows only the people in the new home. When a
  guardianship legally ends differs by state, and the game does not know it yet,
  so the record of it is left open and filed as a research question. Eighteen,
  used for the wording and for moving out, is a placeholder.

- **Open the Guide from underlined prose** Clicking or tapping an automatically
  underlined term opens its Guide entry. Terms marked learned remain plain text
  after the saved preference is restored.

- **Front afro hair keeps an explicit candidate face window** The existing front
  afro style has a candidate face window bound to its original source hash.
  Assembly derives the exclusion from the selected face's eroded alpha within
  authored native rows. Scalp, bangs above the window, outer hair, back hair,
  other styles and all original rasters remain unchanged. The builder rejects a
  changed source hash rather than silently carrying the window onto replacement
  artwork. The boundary is a candidate authored mask, not recovered
  private-source metadata. Actual paired intro evidence and human art approval
  remain required. Missing turned hair is not supplied by this fix.

- **Patients remember their private health episodes** A recorded health episode
  now teaches its onset event to the patient, preserving the event date and
  direct knowledge through saves. Private illness still requires disclosure
  before other people learn about it.

- **Refundable bail stays out of a government's spendable budget** The common
  government budget writer subtracts recorded refundable cash bail from physical
  account cash before allocating balance and reserve. Deposits and refunds
  retain their payment identities without becoming operating revenue or
  spending. No forfeiture revenue is inferred without its own saved event.
  Existing saved budget rows remain readable.

- **Waking at home after a shift shows home, not a blank screen** When the last
  place recorded for someone was a workplace with no room picture of its own (a
  shift the day before), the next morning showed no scene at all. A moment with
  no specific place now shows the person's home, the same home its room already
  falls back to.

- **A town's home prices move from the first month, and keep up as months are
  added** A town's home prices, which set its rents, stood still for about a
  year in a five-year run in Fayetteville, Arkansas, because the town's own
  economy began keeping months only in the second year. The nation's months now
  carry the town's prices until then, with no jump at the switch, and a month
  added to the world later is always read.

- **Home purchase prices follow the housing market** Home purchase quotes now
  scale the county median home value by the modeled housing price level used by
  the town's rent reader. Legacy down payment and monthly mortgage estimates
  keep their existing adjustment. Saved contracts and payments are unchanged.

- **Home purchase quotes use the same housing market level** A home purchase
  quote now adjusts its price, down payment and monthly payment by the same
  existing housing market level. Consumer price changes alone no longer inflate
  the quoted payments. The starting payment amounts remain the existing
  financing placeholders pending researched mortgage terms.

- **A new life records its U.S. House district when its town lies in only one.**
  A new life in a town the Census Bureau lists with only one congressional
  district, such as Elko, Nevada or Lewiston, Maine, now starts with that
  district recorded as its home district, the Government screen names its
  Representative, and the list of House seats you can run for marks it as your
  district. A city split between districts, such as Peoria or San Antonio, is
  left unresolved: the map and the Government screen name only the districts the
  city touches, the House seats list marks each of those as a district your home
  may be in, and the opening no longer presents Texas's 1st district's member as
  a San Antonio life's own. Lives started before this change keep the history
  they were started with.

- **Overdue household bills preserve same-day cash shortfalls** An overdue
  household bill now uses the shared recorded-cash assessment. Spending followed
  by replenishment on the same day cannot conceal a shortfall and fund an
  earlier bill. Existing provider and outside-seller payments keep their saved
  recipients, and each new payment credits exactly what the household pays.
  Earlier receipts and repeated settlement after reloading remain unchanged. A
  household returning to an earlier place opens a new dated bill while reusing
  that place's existing outside-seller account. Its previous bill remains ended,
  and existing receipts and cash balances are preserved.

- **Household bills pay saved providers or labeled outside sellers** A dated
  household spending reader includes resident children and uses the published
  five-or-more column for larger households. Its marked estimate combines each
  retained size-category mean with the category's regional ratio and reuses the
  existing stable world estimate spread. Published sampling errors remain
  separate from household spending variation. Ended monthly bill terms cannot
  produce a new payment on their ended due date. Existing nonhousing provider
  bills spend recorded household cash and credit their saved recipients. The
  replaced personal estimate ends prospectively without rewriting earlier
  payments. Missing household cash never becomes a zero balance. Categories
  without a recorded provider pay one shared counterparty per place, "Sellers
  outside this town's simulated businesses," through the existing account and
  transfer records. Its account starts at zero, and household payments credit
  the same amount they debit. Saved category invoices exclude their category
  from the fallback estimate; saved whole-basket invoices replace the fallback
  basket. Future local-seller category receipts remain with their existing
  producer. No personal cash pooling or additional accounts are introduced.

- **Household housing charges come from their recorded contracts** New
  food-and-bill charges exclude the old estimated housing share. Actual leases
  and mortgages charge housing separately; missing housing contracts no longer
  create estimated rent. Existing charge histories stay intact, and old
  living-cost flows change prospectively. Nonhousing costs remain a marked
  stand-in while regional spending data is unavailable.

- **The Journal's illness line reads right when you passed the illness on** When
  the player gave the illness to someone at home, the Journal read "after I, at
  home, had it". The line now reads "after I had it at home", and an illness
  caught on a family visit reads the same way.

- **In touch counts conversations, not turns** A private aim aimed at a person
  reported how often the player had been in touch since setting it. It counted
  relationship interactions, and a conversation writes one of those for every
  turn that changes anything between the two people, all of them on the day it
  happened. So one conversation that ran three turns told the player they had
  been in touch three times. It now counts the days on which contact actually
  happened. One conversation is once, however long it ran. The nearby test that
  checked what a household conversation records began by returning early if
  nothing had been recorded at all, which is the same as not checking. It now
  requires the interactions it goes on to inspect.

- **An inclusionary housing law's affordable homes go to households under the
  income limit** A town with an inclusionary housing law set aside every seventh
  or so new apartment or rowhouse as affordable, but when the household that
  moved into that home earned too much, the home was let at the market rent and
  the set-aside was lost. The law now fills its set-aside with the covered homes
  that households under the income limit rent, so its affordable homes appear as
  those households move in.

- **New income taxes collect from recorded pay** Adopting an income tax no
  longer creates a revenue level from other states' collections per resident.
  The existing adopted-term paycheck writer assesses actual recorded wages and
  withholds payment; the government's existing cash reader carries completed
  receipts into its monthly budget. Missing accounts or payments do not become
  forecast cash, and Save/Continue keeps the same receipts.

- **A state that repeals its income tax stops collecting it** A state that
  repealed its income tax kept booking income tax revenue for years while every
  paycheck withheld nothing. The budget now collects none from the tax year the
  repeal takes effect in, and the next budget expects none. A law that restores
  the tax collects again from the level the state opened with. A state's income
  tax law now moves the budget from January 1 of the tax year, the same day
  paychecks start withholding under it.

- **Indiana veto overrides now require the constitution's strict majority**
  Indiana's sourced constitutional reading now carries through to override
  eligibility and the saved vote label.

- **A choice that takes no time shows only its words** In the story view, a
  choice that takes no time printed a second, smaller line under its button. In
  some builds that line said "No time passes"; in others it repeated the
  choice's own words. Neither told the player anything. That line is gone for
  instant choices. A choice that really takes time still shows how long it
  takes, and a choice with its own note still shows the note.

- **Institutional rule changes honor saved effective dates** Institutional rule
  changes use the shared enactment date reader, including saved game intervals.
  A sourced enactment with an incomplete date stays unavailable.

- **Use the resident name in the intro locality projection** The intro's
  locality projection uses the saved place's resident name and its recorded
  parent. Formal government labels and jurisdiction identities remain unchanged;
  missing parent evidence is not inferred.

- **Intro casts use recorded roles and measured scene slots** Intro casts shown
  on registered place backgrounds now use the ordinary measured scene-layer
  reader. Selected people retain their actual slot, pose, facing, depth, source
  recipe and recorded role provenance. State representatives retain the existing
  district-qualified roster and canonical work record. Family illustrations
  retain kinship or child-authority records without claiming room attendance.
  Intro staging never adds scheduled workers. Candidate plates without their own
  measured staging contract keep an explicit physical-placement gap. This change
  preserves every source raster and requires same-place screenshots and owner
  review before scene integration.

- **Iowa starting ESA law supplies its sourced annual award** The Iowa Students
  First starting-law row supplies its sourced 2025–26 annual award in minor
  units, preserves its eligibility evidence, and does not carry that amount into
  another school year.

- **A governor's item veto now follows a view the governor actually holds** A
  governor who strikes a floor-added section with the item veto first forms a
  view on that question from the principles they hold, and the view is kept in
  their history. Anything that reads their views, the veto included, reads that
  same view. A governor whose principles say nothing about the question leaves
  the section in place.

- **Job pay follows the simulation clock** The simulation settles the played
  character's recorded job pay when the date changes. Ordinary-life presentation
  no longer settles that pay a second time. An older job without a pay flow
  starts paying when it is first discovered, without backpay for time before
  that discovery.

- **Remove filler years from the journal** The journal no longer adds an event
  for every year of a person's adult life.

- **Both people's Journals say who they met** When you met somebody new, their
  Journal read "Selena McGuire met you on the same program." Each person's
  Journal now says who they met and where, in their own voice: "You met Selena
  McGuire on the same program." A person who introduced the two of you reads
  "You introduced Selena McGuire to Alice May."

- **The Journal no longer repeats a month** The Journal no longer names a month
  twice when a line already gives its date, and a line starting with a word such
  as "At" no longer keeps its capital after the month.

- **The Journal says a shift's pay rose per shift** When a minimum wage law
  raises what one of your shifts pays, the Journal now says "Your pay rose from
  $72 to $80 a shift" and names the law, instead of leaving out what the $80 was
  for.

- **The Journal reads as your own record** The Journal pasted a conversation in
  as a script ("Selena McGuire: Say hello. Dakota Austin: Hi, Selena."). It now
  says what you did: "You said hello to Dakota Austin." An entry that named you
  in the third person ("Selena McGuire met Alice May") now reads "You met Alice
  May". A posted meeting agenda, coursework still due and a choice still open no
  longer appear in your life story; they stay with your open items. When a crime
  happened to you, the Journal used to show the police log's line ("Police in
  Fairbanks took a report of an assault"), which read like somebody else's news.
  It now says what happened to you: "You were assaulted." A new test also reads
  every choice the Journal records and fails if one is written in the present
  tense.

- **Kinship readings reuse the existing person history index** Kinship queries
  group recorded relationships by their member IDs through the existing
  append-aware history reader. Relationship order, dated and sequence cutoffs,
  and earlier saved snapshots retain their existing behavior.

- **Opening a life no longer crashes when the people code loads first** The law
  registry could be built while a handler module was still loading, which left
  an empty entry and crashed the first law dispatch of a new life. The policy
  packs now read each module's small row file instead of the whole handler
  module, so the load order no longer matters.

- **Loyalty proof shows the same person deciding both ways** A test now follows
  one generated person through the couple's choice with and without a recorded
  loyalty tendency, showing opposite outcomes with the reason traced to the
  tendency.

- **Letting a day go by no longer counts as turning something down** Optional
  things on the calendar that the clock simply ran past were being recorded as
  though you had declined them, with your name on the decision. That included
  holds you were never shown and had no control for, so the game could credit
  you with refusing something you never saw. Three things downstream read those
  records as refusals. An organizer could follow up to say there was no problem
  about a meeting you had supposedly turned down. A chapter invitation could
  show as declined when nobody had ever put the question to you. A campaign
  activity could be counted as refused for the same reason. Time passing now
  records what actually happened — that the time came and went without an answer
  — and only an answer you actually give is recorded as one. Records written
  before the two were told apart are left exactly as they are and now read as
  neither, because the game does not get to decide today what you did then.

- **A calendar hold that ran out still shows up in your life record** A hold you
  were never shown and a hold you turned down are two different things, and the
  game now records them as two different things — which is right, and is what a
  refusal you never made was quietly costing you before. Drawing that line moved
  every lapsed hold out of the one kind of record the life record knew how to
  read, so time running out on a meeting left nothing behind at all. Your own
  account of your life simply skipped it: the hold was on your calendar, then it
  was not, and the record between those two days said nothing. Both now appear.
  A hold that ran out reads as one that ran out, in its own words, and nothing
  in it claims you decided anything.

- **A change in pay frequency is not mistaken for a wage change** Law-related
  pay changes are compared using both recorded pay frequencies before their
  monthly effect is noticed. Missing prior frequency stays unsupported instead
  of producing an invented wage difference.

- **Compare law pay changes only in the same recorded currency** Replaces:
  implicit subtraction of different currencies in law pay noticing. Without a
  recorded conversion, changed currencies do not create a priced gain or cost.
  Same-currency amounts keep their saved source and cadence.

- **Old business payments require recorded cash** Legacy business payments use
  dated recorded payer money instead of completing automatically. Untracked
  money blocks payment; insufficient money records a partial or missed payment.
  Unsupported opening locations no longer receive a fixed business revenue
  table. Existing saved contracts and receipts remain intact. Every registered
  locality can receive an explicitly labeled business estimate from existing CBP
  and Economic Census rates. Missing local population uses recorded 2020
  county-part totals, then a calculated regional or national populated-place
  mean with its source and denominator stated. New owner draws remain
  unestablished until operating costs and distributable earnings are recorded;
  existing saved draw contracts continue to settle from recorded cash.

- **Reuse unchanged history when the legislative clock runs** Legislative
  actions now check occupied operation keys without rebuilding every prefix in
  the saved history. Unchanged legislative record families retain their existing
  lookup indexes. The clock still records every action and applies the same
  deadlines. Legislative clock passes reuse their unchanged chamber roster and
  skip building vote questions when no motion is pending. These reads preserve
  historical cutoffs, saved outcomes and older snapshots. Local council reads
  reuse the same history indexes for organization identity, participations and
  seat status. Resignations still remove members immediately, and reading a
  later roster does not change an earlier saved snapshot.

- **Legislative seats come up in the election for their term** Kansas Senate
  seats no longer appear in the 2026 regular election. They next come up in
  2028, while Kansas House seats still come up every two years. Nebraska's
  even-numbered legislative districts come up in 2026 and its odd-numbered
  districts in 2028. A member whose seat is not due keeps serving; the game does
  not replace them or leave their seat vacant at the wrong term boundary. A
  player filing for a recorded district sees that district's next regular
  election. Special elections remain separate from these regular cycles.

- **Explain commitments that interrupt legislative waiting** Waiting for another
  legislative institution now uses the existing Day and Week outcome message
  when a personal commitment interrupts the wait. It names the blocking
  commitment and reports when no time passed. The commitment remains pending
  until resolved, and the bill keeps its institutional schedule.

- **Legislative work opens a recorded bill with a seated sponsor** Opening
  legislative work reads a pending bill already recorded in the save and checks
  its sponsor's current seat. It no longer draws an opening bill or creates a
  member. When no eligible bill is recorded, the office explains how to
  continue. Older saved office assignments keep their bill identity and
  procedure.

- **A seat that cannot be filed for says why beside the button** When a seat you
  have named on the Campaigns screen still cannot be filed for, the reason is
  shown beside the button and the button is off, instead of a refusal appearing
  at the bottom of the page after a click. The district card also no longer
  shows notes about map data.

- **Lines say what happened, in words a person would use** Several lines read
  like a form being filled in. A candidate now "filed to run for Governor"
  instead of "filed as a candidate for a Governor", and a winner "qualified to
  take office as Governor" instead of "Recorded qualification for a dated
  executive term was entered". When time stops for a decision, the notice says
  what the decision is ("Decide whether to request a federal disaster
  declaration.") instead of "something came up". Dates in these lines read the
  way people say them, "January 16, 2028", in special-election notices, press
  deadlines, story threads, bill stages, term notices, the district-residence
  panel, the life history under Who you are and a judge's office history. The
  district panel also speaks to you directly now: "You have lived in this
  chamber's district since …". Choices that take no time no longer carry a "No
  time passes" label. "In this game" is gone from the campaign and party
  screens. The political map no longer names where its boundaries came from. A
  chief of staff's advice no longer has a stray period in the middle of a
  sentence.

- **Local budgets settle only from their recorded public account** County and
  city budgets validate their saved public account before reading payments and
  cash. A missing, mismatched or ambiguous account leaves the budget unchanged.
  A forecast alone no longer settles a local government's month. Existing state
  and federal account behavior is preserved.

- **Local governments without an opening profile use their game peers’ cash.** A
  local government without saved opening cash now starts from current cash in
  the game’s nearby population-sized government accounts. The estimate records
  its contributing accounts and their spread, rather than using forecast revenue
  or a fixed cash figure. The world’s own saved opening profile still takes
  precedence. Existing accounts and money stay intact, and repeating the opening
  after Continue creates no payment or extra balance. The opening adapter now
  uses an actual compiled township and the existing local-government
  materializer to create its canonical jurisdiction before account admission.
  Multiple served places reuse that township account. Existing served-place
  accounts require a recorded ownership migration; they are not relabeled. New
  township budgets preserve their canonical account identity separately from the
  served place’s law jurisdiction, so the existing cash reader sees actual
  completed payments. Existing legacy budget records are not migrated by this
  change.

- **Local council seats use ordinary town and office names** Winning a council
  seat in a town with a generated government profile now shows its town and
  council name in ordinary English on Work and Government.

- **Local bills use their saved appropriation references** Local councils can
  use verified enacted appropriation references under their admitted local
  authority. Missing or mismatched saved terms remain ineligible.

- **The refusal for an unsupported local tax names the local government** The
  message shown when a local tax filing does not match the state's rule now says
  "local government contract," which keeps the American English wording check
  green for every open change.

- **Remove the picture from the city and county opening card** The opening card
  about the city and county that serve your place keeps its text and navigation
  without the background picture.

- **A life played for about twenty years can be saved and opened again** A world
  played for about twenty years writes out longer than the longest piece of text
  the game's engine can hold, a little over 536 million characters, and saving
  it stopped with "Invalid string length". Such a world is now saved in pieces
  of about 16 million characters, and it opens again from those pieces as the
  same world, with the same name and the same check that nothing changed after
  it was written. A save that fits in one piece is written exactly as before, so
  every existing save opens as it did.

- **A long wait across the filing season no longer stops the game** Passing many
  days at once across Congress's filing season could stop the game when a
  senator had been appointed after a seat's filing day. Each member now decides
  whether to run again from the seat's record as it stood on that day.

- **A refusal to talk prints once on a person's card** When nobody is being
  played, a person's card now carries the reason talking is unavailable one time
  instead of twice.

- **Town workers' pay and old replays work again** A day could stop with an
  error when a grown-up in your household worked for the town or another public
  employer that pays every two weeks, twice a month or once a month. Weekly pay
  now leaves those paychecks to the town's own payday, so the day goes on. A
  game replayed from an old saved setup again records finishing high school the
  way it did when that setup was made, instead of a slightly different record.
  When your own race is decided on a day full of candidate filings across the
  state, the news around town now leads with your result instead of dropping it
  below eight statewide filings. Kentucky's legislative terms again start on
  January 1 after the election for every screen that reads the rule. Days that
  seed the people around you, seat a town's businesses or put a state
  constitutional amendment to a vote run faster: each is checked once when it
  finishes instead of after every record it writes.

- **Opening and life refresh share recorded employers** Ordinary adult opening
  hires from the town's actual recorded employers. Later life refresh preserves
  that roster and its pay contracts instead of seating a second set of legacy
  businesses.

- **Time command preview and opportunistic test typed correctly** The time
  command preview now carries its calendar item's exact moment in its type, and
  the opportunistic trait test builds a decision context with the fields the
  type allows.

- **Maine teacher pay reads the existing legal salary floor** Maine's teacher
  salary floor now reads the statutory $40,000 annual minimum for school years
  after June 30, 2022. Earlier dates keep their existing legal answer without
  applying that amount retroactively.

- **How a legislator bargains is read from several sittings, not one talkative
  one** A member who stated three commitments in a single sitting was being
  described as strongly showing their hand, on the strength of one appearance.
  The game now waits until it has seen them answer at three separate sittings
  before it says anything about how they bargain, which is what it always
  claimed to be doing. A member it has not seen often enough is described as
  exactly that.

- **The government map draws again** The national map loads through the same
  browser path as each state's map, so Politics → Government → Map draws the
  country again. It had failed with "The map geometry could not be loaded" after
  a JSON import attribute was added to its loader.

- **The map calls territories territories and names each place's executive**
  Selecting Puerto Rico, Guam, the U.S. Virgin Islands, American Samoa or the
  Northern Mariana Islands on the political map labeled it a "State". The label
  now reads "Territory", and the District of Columbia reads "Federal district",
  both from the place table rather than a check on one place's code. The top
  executive's title comes from the same table: the District's Mayor, and a
  Governor everywhere else.

- **The map reads each place's Senate seats from the place table** The political
  map knew that the District of Columbia has no senators only because its code
  named the District. It now reads the rule from the table of places that send a
  nonvoting member to the House, so the District and every territory show "has
  no seats in the U.S. Senate" by the same rule, and each place's name comes
  from the same place table.

- **Medicaid eligibility follows its enacted income limit** Medicaid expansion
  eligibility now reads the income limit from the law in force for the person's
  state and date. When the enacted law has no amount, the game uses the shared
  comparable-state estimate and keeps its estimate evidence in developer-only
  decision data.

- **People at a meeting sit in the room** At a council meeting, the members the
  meeting names now sit on the dais in the painted chamber, instead of the
  benches staying empty. Anyone on shift at the place fills the seats that are
  left.

- **An older save with the meeting journey already made can still attend** A
  save written before the Calendar journey recorded its arrival had the trip to
  the posted public meeting marked done and nothing placing the player in the
  room, so Attend refused forever. Attend now records the arrival that trip
  lacks, once, and opens the meeting from it. The tests that still expected one
  Attend to finish a meeting now walk in first and stay second, and the
  placeholder ledger, the literal census, the transfer preference and the opener
  heading match the game again. A new world's opening judges now carry the given
  names of the years they were born, as its legislators and townspeople already
  do. Judges were 490 of the 770 people born from 1935 to 1964 in the measured
  Columbus, Ohio world, and all of them kept the old draw: 18.6 percent of that
  generation had a name from its own decade's top 100. With the judges included
  the share is 64.4 percent.

- **A skip stops at a meeting posted while it runs** A skip used to run past
  meetings posted while it ran. A fifteen-year-old in Washington, D.C. lost four
  party meetings between February and June 2039 that way, each marked as passed
  without an answer. The skip now stops at the first meeting or campaign shift
  posted during it, with the meeting still open to attend. The same holds for a
  meeting someone agreed to while the skip ran, such as a contact who said yes.
  Pressing again from that stop lets the meeting go.

- **A meeting somebody else asked for can be attended** When somebody asked the
  player to meet and the player said yes, the meeting was held as the other
  person's, and Attend never offered it. A meeting the played person agrees to
  is now theirs to attend, whoever asked. The People screen also says what
  became of the last request between two people while it still matters: that
  they said yes to a day, could not make it, or never answered. Before, an
  answered request simply vanished and the Ask button came back, which read as
  though it had been lost. Where two people meet is still not chosen. It is
  filed as a research question.

- **Members and councils file bills on questions with no effect writer yet** A
  state legislator who opposes a question whose "no" answer has no registered
  effect writer now files a repeal bill on it when the law in force says yes,
  instead of filing nothing. A city or county council member who leans hard on a
  question the town may answer, but no local effect writer covers, now files a
  plain position bill that goes on the council's agenda like any other
  ordinance. Mapping coverage no longer narrows what members may file. An
  enacted bill of this kind is law in force and changes nothing else in the
  world yet; that gap is marked PLACEHOLDER in the code.

- **A missing wage-law amount no longer stops the clock** When a saved wage law
  lacks its numeric amount, payroll now records the specific missing term and
  leaves that adjustment unapplied. It does not invent a wage, move money, or
  stamp an adjustment that did not happen.

- **Campaign money a candidate took is still caught after you stop playing
  them** If a candidate paid themselves from campaign money and you then retired
  them from play, their committee's reports, a rival's complaint and the state
  regulator's review of the reports all stopped, because each one only ever
  looked at the person being played. They now look at anybody with such a
  payment on record, so the case can still open and reach a finding. Nobody is
  accused of anything that did not happen: only a recorded payment starts it.

- **The Money page stops naming its sources, and a house costs what prices say**
  In Bend, Oregon in 2039 the Money page listed "BEA regional 33 observations",
  said a figure was "Available to the simulation on 2026-09-03", and still
  offered a $250,000 house next to rent up by half. The page now shows the
  figures and their graphs with plain place names and dates such as "July 2026",
  without the products behind them, observation counts or the day a figure
  reached the game. Graph titles read "Income per person" and "Two-bedroom
  rent". The house price, down payment and mortgage payment are still one
  national placeholder, but they now move with the world's price level, the same
  measure the rent line uses, instead of standing still. The Jobs and study
  screen gives a tuition deadline as a date in words.

- **Money reads as dollars on the campaign screen** The campaign screen used to
  say "The committee has USD 0.00" and then, a line later, "Your committee has
  $0.00." Every campaign line now writes money one way, the way a person says
  it: a dollar sign, whole dollars without cents ("$35,410"), and cents only
  when there are some ("$17.03"). The summary after a routine week does the same
  ("Received $72"), and it gives the day your shift pay is due as a date you
  would say out loud, not a code like 2026-05-09.

- **Relatives ring and new people turn up far more often** A new adult life saw
  about three new people and one or two phone calls in 56 days. Family who live
  elsewhere had no recorded contact on day one, so none of them ever decided to
  get in touch, and everyone met new people on one fourteen-day pace. Relatives
  now decide for themselves whether to ring, from their own temperament, and how
  soon somebody meets a new person follows their sociability instead of one
  number for everybody.

- **Remove the morning note** The morning note no longer appears when a new day
  begins. Its panel, saved preference, dismissal state and morning projection
  have been removed; Today and the day recap remain available in their existing
  places.

- **The person card no longer reads out hidden sentences** The Travel to, Meet
  and Contact buttons on a person card no longer carry hidden sentences such as
  "is in the room. Meet takes you back to that scene." for screen readers; the
  buttons speak their own names.

- **A legislative district seats every member it elects, not one.** North
  Dakota, Arizona, Idaho, New Jersey, South Dakota and Washington House
  districts now seat two Representatives each, Maryland's seat three (with its
  subdistrict splits), and Vermont's Windsor Senate district seats its three
  senators. Before, every district seated one member, so a North Dakota home saw
  one Representative in a two-member district and Maryland's House had no
  district bound to any seat. The counts sit in a data file with a source for
  each chamber, and a chamber that lists none still elects one per district.

- **Municipal offices explain estimated age requirements** Municipal offices
  with unread minimum ages use the state's existing similar-office profile.
  Their eligibility sentence explains the estimate and the unconfirmed municipal
  rule. Filing preserves that provenance in the existing candidacy event.
  Replaces: municipal unknown-age refusal and internal qualification-pack
  wording. Dated enacted rules retain precedence. Residence, term and filing
  requirements are not established by this estimate; county offices retain their
  existing rules.

- **Long saves no longer take minutes to pass a year in the development build.**
  Two checks made every press slower as a save grew. The development build
  proved that no scheduled event rewrites the world by copying the whole save to
  text twice for every scheduled event; it now freezes the world instead and
  walks only the records added since the last check. And after every press, the
  play screen checked the whole save once for each piece of news it published;
  it now checks once for the whole batch. On a 12.8 MB Alaska save, letting the
  year run on went from 130 seconds to 11 in the browser. Saves are
  byte-identical to before.

- **Day and Week keep moving while state legislatures take their seats**
  Advancing time became progressively slower as state legislators took their
  seats across the country. The game now reads their recorded roles and
  memberships without repeatedly scanning every person's history, and reuses
  unchanged state institution, chamber and principle lookups during bill steps.
  State legislatures remain present in the world.

- **Nebraska and New Hampshire legislatures have their real sizes** Nebraska's
  one-house Legislature had 128 members and New Hampshire's House had 93.
  Nebraska now has its 49 members, and New Hampshire has its 400 representatives
  and 24 senators, each citing the state constitution. Every other state the
  game has not read still draws its chamber sizes from the range of the states
  it has read, and those sizes are with research.

- **Nebraskans and Minnesotans can stand for their own seats on an ordinary
  start** A life beginning in Nebraska or Minnesota on the ordinary January 2026
  start could not stand for its own legislature. The provisions behind those
  seats had been read from the states' own publishers in September 2026, and a
  rule read then was treated as unproven for any earlier date — so an ordinary
  life, which begins before the reading, fell into the gap and was refused. Each
  Nebraska section's page carries its amendment history, and Minnesota's carries
  the date its constitution was last generally revised. Those lines are in the
  pages the game already keeps, so the current words are now dated to when they
  took effect — all well before 2026 — instead of only to the day they were
  read. The seats open. A life reaching a seat before those words took effect is
  still told the rule is unknown rather than having a later rule applied to it.

- **Close the posted meeting conversation when its time passes** The doorstep
  conversation about a posted public meeting is available while that meeting is
  still ahead. Completing, canceling, or reaching its start closes the upcoming
  meeting topic, including after a saved reload.

- **A new governor no longer ends up with two chiefs of staff** A governor who
  took over an office whose chief of staff was still employed used to hire a
  second chief. The old chief kept making recommendations, and the new one was
  ignored. The first-day choice now lists the sitting chief with a Keep option.
  Keeping them hires nobody new. Hiring someone else ends the old chief's job.
  Office staff hiring can also take applicants the officeholder has already met,
  so the transition's staff interviews can put those people forward. Positions
  with no one named still get generated applicants.

- **A group that breaks away over a party dispute gets a real name, such as
  "Home Rule League", not "League for follow the wider party line".** When
  members walked out of a party body to found their own group, the group was
  named by gluing "League for" onto the position they disagreed about, which
  made names like "League for follow the wider party line". Each position now
  carries a name a group could actually be called.

- **People who move to a town can get hired there** A person who moved into town
  with no job on the town's record was treated as never having worked, so every
  local laid off after them was hired first and the newcomer never was.
  Employers now count a newcomer as out of work since moving in, and a young
  local with no job since turning 18, so they take their place in line with
  everyone else looking for work.

- **An outlet without recorded reporters has no assignment capacity.** The news
  desk now treats an absent recorded newsroom as zero assignment capacity.
  Existing staff capacity and saved job-ending checks remain unchanged.

- **Election conversation fixtures record actual home presence** Election
  conversation tests now explicitly record an authored home scene with an adult
  from the player's saved household before asking the existing producer to bind
  the campaign briefing. Household membership alone remains insufficient for a
  conversation. Existing election and reporter assertions are preserved.

- **News about vacancies, resignations and reporters' deadlines names the state
  and writes dates in words.** Some news lines printed a state's two-letter
  code, a raw date and the game's own wording, such as "OR's governor calls a
  special election, held on 2031-08-17 in this game." They now read like news:
  "Oregon's governor has called a special election for August 17, 2031." The
  same fix covers resignation notices, a reporter's response deadline, and a
  governor's session with no bills.

- **Newspaper correction regression supplies its initial publication** The
  newspaper correction regression now records a public event and publishes it
  through the canonical writers before correcting the article. A fresh life has
  no published edition, so its empty newspaper is verified separately. The
  existing binding, correction identity, revision and read-only assertions
  remain intact, with saved-world reload coverage added.

- **Newsroom cuts follow recorded cash and payroll** Newsroom owners cut jobs
  only when recorded cash cannot cover recorded payroll. The newest positions
  end first, and missing financial records do not cause a layoff.

- **Newsroom cuts require recorded cash below payroll** A newsroom owner cuts
  staff only when recorded cash cannot cover recorded payroll. The owner ends
  only enough least-senior positions to make the remaining payroll fit that
  cash. Missing cash or comparable payroll records leave cuts pending. The
  existing purchase payment writer also accepts a recorded organization buyer
  with a saved principal and actual cash. Missing identity or funding leaves
  payment pending. When actual buyer and seller principals, outlet books,
  existing asking terms and sufficient cash are saved, both principals evaluate
  the sale through the shared decision function before payment and ownership
  transfer. Missing valuation remains pending; no new valuation multiple is
  supplied. Other recorded owner practices now go through the shared decision
  function for the saved principal. A missing or controlled principal leaves the
  directive pending; the legacy practice probability no longer selects an
  outcome.

- **Nobody can ask out the person who raised them, and the dead are not offered
  Talk or Meet** Before, a grown child whose guardian had no family record could
  ask that guardian out, and the date went ahead. A parent who had died still
  showed Talk, Meet, Travel to and Contact on their card. Now anyone who ever
  held a parent's or guardian's authority over the other, or ever cared for
  them, can never be asked out, whichever of the two asks. The card for somebody
  who has died offers no actions.

- **A child is no longer offered a house, and place figures read as plain
  dates** A child was offered a house. In Alaska, Camila, 10, was offered a
  $250,000 home with nothing behind it. Buying a home now needs age 18, and the
  offer appears only when the game keeps the person's money. The place figures
  on the Money screen read like "Unemployment rate, Alaska: 7.0%, up from 4.3%
  in July 2026" instead of naming "the last published figure" and a code such as
  "2026-M07" or "FY2025", and a footnote asterisk after a place name is gone.
  The Money screen also shows its own date in words.

- **A long game no longer stops when a 15-year-old in town draws a birth** Town
  families drew births from every woman aged 15 to 49, but the birth record
  refused any parent under 16, so a 15-year-old's draw stopped the whole world
  with "would be under 16 at the birth." It stopped watched worlds in Seattle
  and Philadelphia. Now the birth chances and the birth record read one rule for
  the youngest parent, taken from the national birth-rate table, and a birth the
  record ever refuses is noted privately while the world goes on.

- **Winning an office no longer needs a Qualify button, and saves stuck outside
  an office they won are seated** A governor-elect used to have to press
  "Qualify for the term" on Campaigns before the term began. A player who never
  saw the button lost the whole term, with no warning, when time passed the
  start date. That step is gone. The office's requirements, such as age and
  residence, are checked for the winner, and the oath on the first day of the
  term is the ceremony. Missouri House and Senate winners were never seated. The
  term limit counted the member's own first term as one already served and
  refused the seat. That is fixed. A won legislative seat also no longer appears
  as "An offer of work ... waiting for your answer". A save already stuck, for
  example a governor whose term began without them or a member whose first day
  was refused, is seated when it is opened. The seat counts from that day. The
  campaign's closing line now gives a governor-elect the date the term begins,
  instead of saying "the seat is theirs".

- **The World overview no longer shows where a fact came from** Opening the
  details under a public officeholder on the World overview listed links reading
  "Institutional source 1", "Institutional source 2" and so on, pointing out of
  the game. That is reference material about where the office record came from,
  and it was showing to every player in every life. It is gone from ordinary
  play, along with the same kind of block on the national election results
  screen. Nothing is lost: the records themselves are unchanged, and a developer
  opening the game as a developer surface still sees them.

- **A council can vote to reenact a returned act without the player** A council
  sitting now considers an act awaiting a veto override through the existing
  member vote engine. The saved roll call must still satisfy the council's
  membership, quorum, override threshold, and deadline. The player's own
  override action continues to require a council seat.

- **A person's card no longer names a temperament nobody observed** A person's
  card described how somebody tends to behave — reserved, or confrontational, or
  one to think a thing through — before the game had decided anything of the
  sort about them. It was reading the number a person is born with rather than
  anything the world had written down, so at the start of a life it would tell
  you the temperament of four people you had never dealt with. The card now says
  nothing about a person's temperament until the game has actually established
  it, which happens the first time that person makes a decision it bears on.
  Someone the game has established as unremarkable on a trait still says
  nothing, because that is a different thing from not knowing.

- **People move in and out of a watched town** A world watched from the start
  now reviews its town for people moving in and out, as a played world does.
  Before, nobody ever moved in or out when nobody was played.

- **Watched worlds keep moving while the screen stays responsive** Watch the
  world now advances in a background worker. The date updates as weeks pass, and
  the visible world catches up at regular checkpoints. Run, Pause, Save, and
  Save and return use the same world clock and keep the latest completed
  checkpoint. Large watched worlds are saved in smaller pieces so the browser
  does not reject a whole game for exceeding its single-record size limit.
  Closing the tab while unsaved weeks remain now warns the player. The observer
  clock and the recap also stay apart on a desktop-sized screen. Saves now use a
  newer browser storage format. Once this build opens your saves, an older build
  of the game can no longer open them.

- **The Observing bar no longer repeats the date** While observing, the current
  date stays on the player card instead of also appearing in the Observing bar
  next to the clock and run controls.

- **Observing no longer tells you that you haven't spoken to anyone** When you
  are observing a world there is no player whose acquaintance can be described
  as "you", so a person's card no longer says you haven't spoken. Housemates
  still read as a household, and the relationship record underneath is
  unchanged.

- **Every office on the campaign screen shows its election date before you
  file** A state house or senate seat said "Upcoming election timing is not
  established in this save" until you filed for it, so a Maine Senate seat hid a
  date it shares with the House. Each seat now shows the state's next regular
  election before you file, and a town seat says its election is four weeks
  after filing.

- **Overdue office salary uses the role held when its period began** An unpaid
  office salary period remains eligible after the same recorded work changes to
  a nonoffice role. Eligibility uses the period's starting date, matching the
  shared payroll's payer, law and amount readers. A period that begins in a
  nonoffice role is skipped without preventing later office periods. Existing
  weekly dates, cash settlement, taxes and replay identities are preserved.

- **Open office salary agreements before scheduling settlement** Office salary
  agreements can now be initialized at the actual current date without paying,
  repricing existing terms, or backdating an older save. The opening caller and
  monthly scheduler can reuse the existing agreement writer.

- **Office salaries use the shared paycheck writer** Each due office-salary
  period now uses the same payment and withholding writer as other recorded job
  contracts. Existing salary terms, weekly dates, and payment identities are
  preserved. Catch-up includes all recorded due weeks.

- **Candidacy uses each office's recorded minimum age** Candidacy checks the
  selected office's recorded minimum age instead of applying a general age
  requirement. An unresolved age rule remains unresolved. Dated qualification
  assessments and laws enacted in the world keep precedence. Kentucky
  legislative candidacy now reads the office's constitutional age. Other
  researched missing ages retain their own citations and supported dates; an
  earlier date without evidence still leaves the requirement unresolved.
  World-aware office projections now read an operative age law recorded in that
  world through the existing enacted-rule reader. The enacted-law fixture seats
  the actual governor and uses the existing executive desk before recording law.

- **Official reflections retain recorded vote knowledge** When a person reflects
  on a law, their view of a legislator now retains the recorded vote knowledge
  it used. A public roll call can be learned through news or an acquaintance; a
  private vote requires existing event knowledge. Missing or future vote records
  supply no knowledge. The player continues to decide their own view.

- **Ohio characters can run for state office** Every Ohio state office asks for
  a candidate who is a qualified elector, and the game never decided that
  requirement, so it turned away every Ohio candidate for the legislature,
  governor, lieutenant governor, attorney general and secretary of state. It is
  now read from the character's own record: at least 18, a citizen by birth, and
  living in Ohio at least 30 days, as Ohio law requires of a voter. A character
  who runs registers first, as real candidates do, and the filing checks Ohio's
  registration rule. Missouri's requirement that a governor have been a citizen
  for fifteen years is read the same way.

- **A saved life whose state voted on a constitutional amendment opens again
  after the legislature sizes were corrected.** Correcting the sizes of
  Nebraska's and New Hampshire's legislatures made every saved life that held an
  earlier amendment vote in those states fail to open with "That saved game
  could not be opened." A vote already taken is now checked against the chamber
  that voted, and new votes still use today's sizes. When a large save is not
  read on the first try, the game tries again twice before it reports a problem.
  If a save still cannot be opened, the game now says it has been kept, not
  deleted.

- **A reporter asks you about a case once, not three times in a day.** When
  several records about one case arrived in the same week, a paper opened a
  separate story for each, and its reporter asked the subject the same question
  once per story. In a playthrough, the Alaska governor was asked the same
  question three times on the same day and had to deny it three times. Now a
  week's developments on one case become one story that draws on all of them.
  While a paper is still working a story on a case, later developments wait for
  that story to run and then become its follow-up.

- **You can be a couple with one person at a time** Before, a player already
  with somebody could ask a second person to be a couple, hear yes, and end up
