  room. Read the agenda, see who is present, speak, and choose when to finish.
  Existing recorded votes appear where available. Meeting duration no longer
  reads as travel time, Until needed stops before a same-day meeting, and
  completed meetings leave the waiting list.

- **Retain governing-law attribution on state enforcement budget costs** Saved
  state budgets retain the governing-law stamp and existing age-verification
  enforcement cost component without changing spending amounts.

- **Keep consumer privacy laws attached to saved state enforcement costs** Saved
  state administration costs retain their operative consumer privacy law
  attribution. Existing spending and balance calculations stay unchanged.

- **Keep separate recorded rule and service changes in local law news** Distinct
  non-money sections of an enacted law can each produce a local story. Stories
  preserve recorded section and source references.

- **Show your state representatives on the opening legislature card** The
  opening legislature card shows the people already recorded as representing
  your home, with readable name plates and their saved appearances. The
  illustration creates no attendance or acquaintance, and missing seats stay
  missing.

- **Use historical honorees for newly generated school names** New lives use a
  versioned school naming pattern that chooses historical honorees instead of
  random generated people. Explicit older replay versions retain their original
  school names; existing saved worlds are unchanged.

- **Describe saved work starts without implying missing career history** The
  life-so-far paragraph states the current job's saved start and earlier job
  starts without implying an unrecorded unemployment interval or inventing jobs
  between them.

- **Saved pretrial decisions name the bail law they applied** Saved pretrial
  release and detention records now name the operative bail law and the referral
  and charge records that produced the decision.

- **A fundraiser cannot collect money the donor is not known to have** Before: A
  fundraiser could credit the committee from a donor whose available money was
  not established. After: You still meet the named person, but the committee
  receives no gift unless the person has enough recorded money. The result says
  when their available money is unknown or insufficient.

- **Count multi-county city residents in their own counties** City outcome
  weights now deduct residents from each county using the Census population
  parts rather than assigning the whole city to its largest county. The weights
  use the published 2020 boundaries and population proportions.

- **Opening mortgage estimates preserve time already paid** Opening mortgage
  estimates use the home's value, the opening down-payment share and the game's
  mortgage rate. Time already spent paying the loan reduces its remaining
  balance and term instead of starting another thirty-year mortgage.

- **Opening mortgage estimates distinguish recorded and estimated home tenure**
  Opening homes already recorded as mortgaged now get loan terms and payments
  through the existing household-loan writer. The principal uses the home's game
  valuation and sourced down-payment share; the rate uses the saved policy rate
  plus the cited mortgage spread. Recorded residence months, or an explicitly
  labeled same-town game average, determine the remaining amortized balance and
  term. An initialized peer average is labeled separately from observed tenure.
  Home purchases and ordinary life refreshes use the same monthly loan service.
  Existing mortgage IDs and payments remain intact when old saves have no loan
  terms. A contract with unrecorded escalation thresholds records missed
  payments without inventing a default or collections deadline. Opening
  mortgages explicitly use the approved simplified fee-free contract.

- **Mortgage payments follow the game economy** Home-buying estimates calculate
  monthly mortgage payments from the current economy's rate and a fixed
  thirty-year repayment term.

- **Mortgage quotes include a mortgage-rate spread** Home-buying estimates
  include the difference between mortgage and central-bank rates when
  calculating the monthly payment on a thirty-year loan.

- **Bargaining colleagues use the chamber's member chooser** A colleague
  deciding a bill during bargaining now uses the same extracted member chooser
  as chamber votes. Their existing considerations, vote options, individual
  explanation and recorded decision trace stay intact. No vote weights or
  institutional cues change in this caller migration.

- **Executive desks use declared legal deadlines** Bills on an executive desk
  use the action window declared in their rule pack. An unsupported window
  leaves the bill pending without an invented ten-day deadline. The briefing
  shows that no deadline is established. Saving and continuing preserves the
  matter, and a supported lapse still records the pack's consequence without a
  signature.

- **A chief's agenda advice comes from recorded views** Chiefs of staff now
  advise an agenda priority from their recorded principles and the policy
  answers the program bank actually declares. The officeholder weighs that
  advice with their own views, and both the advice and the decision are saved.
  An unsupported or tied choice stays open instead of being picked at random.
  The agenda and budget show every subject in the existing program bank, so a
  random shortlist cannot hide a supported priority. Player choices remain
  explicit.

- **Committee seating follows recorded member tenure** Committee rosters now
  order actual seated members by their saved seating date, then stable member
  identity for equal seniority. State members use validated opening or
  late-entry events; Congress uses its recorded seat-roll event. Changing a
  caller's seed no longer changes who gets a committee seat. Missing seating
  evidence or duplicate member identities leave the committee roster
  unavailable. Generated employment dates never establish seniority. The
  existing compiled committee sizes and round-robin distribution remain the
  disclosed game assignment profile; this change does not invent legal committee
  appointment procedures, requests, or party quotas.

- **Appointment modules load without reading uninitialized constants** Keeps the
  existing appointment constants in one dependency-free module so browser test
  collection can load the governing modules. Existing public exports, profile
  values and decision behavior are unchanged.

- **Supreme Court appointments require a recorded Senate vote** An associate
  justice nominee waits when no Senate is seated. The vacancy and the nominee's
  lower-court seat remain unchanged until a recorded affirmative Senate vote,
  instead of an appointment appearing without a roll call.

- **Chief Justice appointments wait for Senate consent** Chief Justice nominees
  wait when no Senate is seated, instead of taking office without a vote. A
  President with no eligible recorded candidate also leaves the vacancy pending
  instead of choosing an unfamiliar person at random.

- **Constitutional roll calls keep members' reasons** State constitutional
  proposal records now name each member's recorded reason for voting. The roll
  call also states the most common recorded reason, so a general procedure note
  no longer hides why the members voted.

- **Council passage records the executive route** Town and compiled council
  ordinances now share the passage handler. A profile that requires no
  presentment records that outcome without inventing a mayor's signature. A
  council that requires executive action retains its presentment, decision and
  deadline route. An unread presentment rule leaves the ordinance pending. The
  recorded no-presentment outcome survives Continue without duplication.

- **Delivered public work reports its saved result** A saved public-service
  delivery now reports its actual result to the completed governing matter
  linked to that appropriation or enacted measure. Immediate and delayed
  deliveries use the same receipt reader. The report keeps the original service
  and payment identities through saving and continuing; repeating a receipt does
  not create another report. Earlier blocked due records stay intact.

- **Staffing no longer proves fulfillment of an enacted duty** A covered body
  remains compliance-unknown when no qualifying fulfillment record connects it
  to the duty. Employing someone no longer creates a finding that the body
  complied. Unknown coverage, previously saved findings and repeat handling
  remain unchanged. Positive fulfillment still needs an approved receipt
  binding.

- **Executive office matters wait for recorded choices** NPC staff and agenda
  matters stay open when no recorded advice or known candidate assessment
  selects a choice. Explicit player staff decisions remain available. Other
  governing families retain their existing paths.

- **Governing follow-ups report recorded actions and delivery** Governing work
  counts as completed only when its completion cites the matter's saved
  decision. Cancelled or lapsed work does not qualify. Budget and agency
  follow-ups now require a linked enacted appropriation or saved delivery
  outturn; party, staff skill, a spending decision, and a payment alone do not
  create results.

- **Governor term-limit proposals record actual state members** A saved governor
  term-limit proposal now records the actual state chamber's members and their
  reasons through the shared member-vote path. Anonymous scaled seats no longer
  supply its rollcall. Missing recorded bodies remain unsupported; terms,
  applicability, thresholds and the existing filing preflight remain intact.

- **Law-effect fixtures use actual governor desk actions** Downstream law-effect
  and duty tests open the recorded governor's bill matter and record a
  controlled signing choice. The fixtures preserve player-work responsibility
  and use the existing institution transition handlers. A missing-date case
  checks the canonical operative date instead of relying on a null saved field
  alone. Written appropriation dates and actual saved funding targets are
  preserved by the accompanying fiscal repairs. All original fiscal assertions
  remain unchanged.

- **Member vote reasons reuse the shared consideration weights** Four
  member-vote callers now rank displayed reasons with the existing shared
  consideration score. Reason magnitudes and equal-weight input order stay the
  same; vote choices and recorded subjects keep their existing paths.

- **Rejected policy amendments keep their actual chamber votes** A state policy
  amendment supported by legislators' recorded principles is saved before its
  actual chambers vote. A defeated proposal retains its named members and
  reasons instead of disappearing behind a preliminary forecast. Policy and
  Article V proposal ballots share the existing chamber decision path. Existing
  terms, thresholds and consideration weights stay unchanged.

- **Senate nominations use the shared member vote decision** Chief and associate
  justice confirmations carry their actual saved nomination into the shared
  chamber vote decision. Senators keep their existing reasons; the recorded roll
  preserves present without voting and explicit absence. A roll with no yes or
  no votes keeps the nomination pending and leaves the nominee's current office
  intact. Bills retain their existing question identity.

- **Staff assessments use recorded experience** Offering executive staff
  candidates no longer supplies invented careers or degrees. Missing working
  history remains unknown, and steadiness-based advice waits for actual
  evidence. Existing assessments of recorded posts remain unchanged.

- **State policy amendments record the actual legislature's votes** State policy
  constitutional proposals now record the saved state's actual chamber members
  through the shared member-vote engine. Dated institution bindings, active seat
  tenures and recorded vacancies supply the roll call; a congressional
  delegation cannot stand in for the state legislature. Existing proposal
  thresholds and modeled-source labels remain unchanged. This bounded A79
  migration does not replace the legacy term-limit or policy-filing preflight
  evaluators.

- **Governor term-limit filing retains actual rejected rollcalls** State
  amendment filing reviews now validate the actual saved chambers before forming
  legislators' principles. A missing or closed institution cannot borrow a
  congressional delegation. A governor term-limit review saves its existing
  cause-backed proposal before actual chamber votes; a rejection keeps its named
  rollcall, including after Continue or a repeated review. General policy filing
  still retains its older scaled preflight verdict pending a separate ruling.

- **Bills stay pending when no governor is seated** A passed bill stays pending
  when the world has no seated governor for its jurisdiction. An authored
  scenario ending cannot supply a signature or veto. Existing seated governors
  still receive the bill through their bound matter. The player-session wait
  uses the same executive desk instead of recording an authored signature or
  veto.

- **Vice presidential nominations require a presidential choice** An empty
  presidential appointment decision leaves the vice presidency vacant. The
  nomination due item records a blocked reason instead of choosing another
  eligible person. Existing selected nominees keep their nomination records.

- **Cannabis revenue reaches budgets that started at zero** A state with zero
  opening selective sales taxes now records cannabis tax revenue after the
  inherited retail delay. The amount survives fiscal rollover without
  duplication and disappears on operative repeal. The saved budget month carries
  the governing law stamp. Existing fixed revenue and delay calibration,
  individual retail records and nationwide law proof remain incomplete.

- **Party bodies use recorded opinions instead of seeded stances** Party
  strategy and procedure questions have their own canonical subjects. The
  existing political belief formation pipeline saves their selected options and
  reasons alongside policy beliefs, while preserving older policy saves. Party
  bodies read those saved views. Members without a formed view abstain, and an
  all-abstaining review leaves the platform unchanged. The controlled person
  still chooses explicitly. Fresh party views require recorded reasons; party
  membership does not supply an opinion.

- **Vary estimated teacher floors and tuition growth by world and state**
  Teacher salary floors and forgone tuition growth use stable world/state draws
  within their existing research ranges. The same state's towns share its floor,
  and saved games retain the same estimates when reopened.

- **School scenes use recorded teachers and classmates** Growing-up scenes now
  use existing school enrollment and teaching records to find companions. When
  no suitable teacher or classmate exists, the scene is unavailable instead of
  creating a person and school relationship during lookup.

- **School presence no longer invents a project and deadline** Being at school
  no longer offers a project-sharing conversation with an invented unfinished
  task and next-week deadline. That topic remains unavailable until saved
  assignment records supply its facts. Existing recorded school conversation
  turns remain readable.

- **Start the first morning where the work schedule puts you** Your first
  morning starts at work when your schedule says you are on shift. If you are
  home between shifts, the day explains why.

- **Home and doorstep conversations require recorded presence** Living together
  no longer puts every housemate in the home conversation room. A posted meeting
  no longer places an unrelated neighbor at the doorstep. Both conversation
  rooms use current scene or arrival participants, and recorded bystanders
  determine hearing and privacy.

- **Quiet home scenes show people with recorded presence** The quiet-home
  introduction and conversation no longer assume that everyone who lives there
  is present. They use current saved scene or completed-arrival participants, so
  an unrecorded housemate is not offered as someone standing in the room.

- **School conversations use recorded room presence** Sharing a school no longer
  puts every classmate in the conversation room. School conversations use the
  people recorded in a current scene or completed arrival, and only present
  classmates can be addressed. Recorded bystanders count for hearing and
  privacy. Leaving or advancing time invalidates the old presence.

- **Show who is present and keep meeting choices current** The day lists the
  people present in the current scene. Meeting speech choices stay with the
  current scene and are checked again before the comment is recorded.

- **Keep transit payment costs separate from unrecorded service** Actual transit
  payments keep their governing-law budget stamps. A payment no longer creates
  inferred service hours or rider reactions. NTD rural operating-cost
  observations serve as calibration evidence; they do not select a price or
  determine service delivery.

- **Retain law provenance on paid transit service and budget records** Paid
  rural-transit service metrics and the posted government budget outlay retain
  their canonical governing-law and payment-chain provenance across saved games.
  A different governing measure is not attributed to the source appropriation.
  This does not change authored service prices, create actual rider decisions,
  or prove nationwide effects.

- **Party chapter outreach follows its weekly meeting schedule** Initial
  outreach uses the next eligible chapter meeting, and an invitation schedules
  the following weekly meeting for the next outreach.

- **Keep unselected NPC appointments pending** A mayor or President who has not
  selected an appointee leaves the opening vacant. Their appointment decision no
  longer falls back to drawing a resident or naming the longest-serving
  central-bank governor.

- **Keep an unanswered goal call pending** A goal call remains pending when
  either its caller has no selected decision to act or its recipient has no
  selected answer. Neither writes a call event or completed goal step.
  Regression checks retain the ordinary clock and original assertions, with
  recorded business listings and one isolated connection intention. The three
  goal-review decisions no longer add close-choice random rolls.

- **Keep a source's unselected answer pending** A reporter's check of an earlier
  claim records confirmation only when the source selected it. An unselected
  source answer leaves the check blocked instead of recording a refusal,
  confirmation, or discovery.

- **An unanswered meeting request stays open** A person who has not selected an
  answer no longer refuses a meeting by default. The existing scheduled answer
  records a blocked result without inventing a reply or commitment.

- **Unresolved reporter disclosures remain pending** An unresolved
  promise-disclosure decision no longer records a tip or reporter question,
  including saved games.

- **Family plans remain unanswered without a selected decision** An undecided
  family-plan answer leaves the existing due item blocked instead of recording a
  refusal, agreement or future child resolution. Selected answers retain their
  existing writers and dates.

- **Favor consequences require a selected decision** Asking for a favor back and
  telling others about a refusal explicitly require a selected decision before
  recording the request, claim or audience knowledge. Existing selected-answer
  writers keep their behavior.

- **Keep undecided disaster reactions pending** An undecided disaster reaction
  creates no reaction trace or settled marker. Later retries preserve previously
  selected readers and recorded knowledge.

- **Occasion invitations require a selected decision** An occasion invitation
  requires a selected answer before its caller returns permission to ask. An
  unanswered decision keeps the existing no-invitation result.

- **An undecided campaign choice does not invent an action** An opponent or
  party chapter that has not selected an action no longer raises money or
  records a deferred reply by default. The weekly evaluation is recorded as
  blocked instead of inventing a campaign action.

- **Press choices can remain undecided** Unresolved press choices remain pending
  instead of recording a decline or a response that was never selected. The
  matter and story can be reconsidered later.

- **Promise decisions leave an unanswered revision pending** A promise
  renegotiation without a selected decision returns no answer instead of
  inventing a request for an answer. Selected outcomes preserve the existing
  trait evaluation and recording contract.

- **An unanswered study plan stays open** A study partner without a selected
  approach or answer no longer proposes a default approach or records an
  unresolved reply. The existing exchange stays open without inventing an
  agreement or a classmate answer.

- **An unanswered study offer stays open** A classmate who has not selected an
  answer no longer refuses collaboration by default. The conversation stays open
  without recording an agreement, refusal or shared-work relationship. A
  selected agreement records its shared-coursework relationship through the
  existing agreement writer once, without a second scene interaction.

- **Keep Supreme Court nomination decisions pending** Presidents evaluate the
  recorded eligible judge even when only one is available. An undecided decision
  selects no nominee, and an associate nomination remains pending until a
  nominee is selected. The President may leave a nomination pending instead of
  naming an available judge.

- **New study charges use the recorded tuition freeze** New study-period charges
  use the shared outstanding tuition reader, including its recorded
  operative-price freeze. Already saved charge amounts remain unchanged.

- **Scheduled activities use the complete clock job list** Joining, completing
  the remaining interval or performing a scheduled activity uses the same
  existing complete scheduled-job registry as the minute clock by default.
  Explicitly supplied registries keep their current behavior. The Calendar
  regression uses the actual recorded completed-work event as its interval
  provenance.

- **Calendar settings describe unanswered invitations correctly** The Calendar
  interruption checklist now describes an unanswered invitation as lapsed rather
  than declined. The invitation setting still controls optional stops, and
  confirmed commitments remain mandatory stops.

- **Enacted program funding reaches its responsible office** New appropriations
  recorded while applying an enacted law reach the existing office
  program-matter writer without depending on wrapped state or council handler
  lists. Existing spending and availability writers retain their authority.

- **Committee hearing follow-up** Committee hearings use the same scheduled
  follow-up handlers as ordinary time.

- **Pending employee appeal answers** An unresolved employee appeal answer
  remains pending instead of becoming a final refusal.

- **Pending caregiver choices** An unresolved caregiver choice leaves the
  childhood moment pending.

- **Pending commissioner settlement answers** An unresolved commissioner answer
  leaves the actual appeal pending without recording a settlement ruling.

- **Unanswered couple requests** An unanswered couple request no longer becomes
  a rejection.

- **Unanswered job offers** Job seekers wait for a selected answer before
  accepting or turning down an offer.

- **Unanswered earlier matters** An unanswered choice no longer makes another
  person raise an earlier matter.

- **Pending NPC mogul offer answers** An unresolved NPC answer preserves the
  actual open offer without recording a refusal or payment.

- **Pending mogul approach decisions** An unresolved mogul approach makes no
  offer and records no final answer.

- **Organizer outreach decisions** Campaign organizers wait for a selected
  answer before offering an activity.

- **Party chapter invitations** Party organizers wait for a selected answer
  before issuing invitations.

- **Unanswered reinstatement attempts stay unwritten** An unanswered
  reinstatement attempt returns without changing the World or exposing a
  trace-validation error.

- **Keep county election dates and filing requirements distinct** County office
  listings use their read election dates. Filing remains unavailable while the
  office's qualification and district requirements are unknown.

- **Show the current clock beside time controls** The time bar shows the current
  day, date and time from the saved world, separately from skip destinations.
  The label refreshes when the world changes.

- **Day-based waiting follows the game clock** Day advancement and the demo,
  formative interval and successor wait now use the shared minute clock. Local
  target times survive daylight-saving changes. Advancement stops at the actual
  commitment frontier and keeps its saved result instead of skipping it. Default
  day and minute advancement also carry the existing due handlers, including
  payment already earned from a completed work shift.

- **Offer the actual public meeting choice where time stops** The time-stop
  notice offers Go to meeting and Stay home using the existing attendance and
  decline actions. Until needed names the actual due calendar choice instead of
  directing every choice to Work.

- **Keep the actual clock only on the player card** The player card shows the
  actual date and time on separate lines. Duplicate current-clock and
  skip-target blocks are removed.

- **Reporter requests only read existing newsroom staff** Seeking a civic
  reporter now returns a recorded available journalism role or no reporter.
  Asking no longer creates a person, newsroom, employment or event. The request
  form retains its factual no-role message and omits the unavailable creation
  action. Test-only staff setup preserves the current recorded interview inputs.

- **Court cases and clemency petitions advance independently of newspapers** The
  weekly newspaper sweep no longer advances prosecutions or clemency petitions.
  Their existing dated court handlers retain those responsibilities.

- **Court cases use their saved venue** Criminal cases, eviction hearings, and
  law review share one court lookup. A missing venue or seated judge leaves
  judicial action pending. Existing eviction records retain the actual court,
  judge, seat, and tenure that authorized the judgment.

- **Judges choose applicable legal sentence terms** Sentencing uses the case's
  recorded facts, researched legal range and operative mandatory minimum. The
  actual judge chooses a term through the existing decision engine. Missing
  legal applicability stays pending. Life imprisonment has no invented release
  date.

- **Incomplete jury panels leave trials pending** A panel below the existing
  court target cannot deliberate or return a verdict. The original case remains
  pending without a mistrial, dismissal or sentence. Jurisdiction-specific jury
  sizes remain a labeled research dependency.

- **Crime exposure avoids constructing dates outside its requested window** The
  existing crime exposure clock checks whether an offense is due within the
  requested span before constructing its date. Tiny positive rates can therefore
  leave a valid month empty without failing on a distant ISO date. The
  producer's rates, recorded count, replay, and actual sample writers are
  unchanged.

- **Crime integration fixtures retain real clocks in smaller worlds** The long
  crime integration cases use the existing small-world and starting-condition
  writers rather than full playable-life openings. The feed fixture adds a
  canonical second locality and actual households through existing writers.
  Incident, journal, knowledge, police, referral, charge, and foreign-town
  exclusion assertions remain. The positive-fear fixture now joins an actual
  saved report on its occurrence day to the returned fear source ID, under CTO
  10:54; it no longer asserts every year has excess crime. The prior zero-fear
  yearly failure is preserved. Exposure days stop at the requested window before
  constructing a date far beyond it; focused tests cover tiny positive rates and
  replay boundaries.

- **Council readings use their existing amendment author where rules permit**
  The saved council meeting can ask seated members for amendments through the
  existing author before its shared reading driver. Unread amendment permission
  remains unsupported.

- **Preserve the selected workplace in its picture** The workplace picture
  follows the recorded arrival's selected job and employer. Pictured employees
  belong to that employer, and deceased workers are excluded. Expected shifts do
  not grant attendance or conversation eligibility.

- **The country elects a President and Vice President every four years** Before
  this, nothing ever held a presidential election. The President and Vice
  President seated at the start served until January 20, 2029, and then both
  offices stayed empty for good. Now a presidential election is held every
  fourth November from 2028, whether or not you take part. Each party names a
  ticket 60 days before election day. A sitting President who is eligible may
  run again; the Twenty-Second Amendment bars a third elected term. Every state
  and the District reports its vote on election day, the states certify, and the
  electors vote on their statutory day in December. Congress counts the votes on
  January 6, and the winners take the oath at noon on January 20. A saved game
  picks up the calendar where it left off. The popular vote in each state is the
  game's own until research answers how much a presidential result moves between
  elections: it starts from the state's 2024 result and moves by a swing drawn
  each cycle. How nominees are chosen is also the game's own for now. Not
  modeled yet, and marked so in the code: running for President yourself,
  primaries and campaigns, faithless electors, and a tie in the Electoral
  College. A tie leaves the offices empty until the contingent election in
  Congress is built.

- **Signing news names the President, and state legislators appear on the
  Government screens** When the President signs or vetoes a bill from Congress,
  the news now says which President it was, for example "President Brianna
  Savage signed H.R. 12." It used to read "The President signed H.R. 12." The
  members the game seats in the home state's legislature were never shown.
  Represented by, under Government, said no one held the player's state House
  and state Senate seats, and the State screen said no member of either chamber
  was on record. Represented by now names the member for the home's own
  district, and each chamber on the State screen lists its sitting members in
  seat order.

- **Letting time pass is many times faster, and long saves slow down far less as
  they age.** Every scheduled thing that happened as time passed used to
  re-check the whole world after each small change it made, and then copy the
  whole save twice to prove nothing had been altered behind its back. So each
  year took longer than the last. The world is now checked once per press of the
  clock, and the whole-save copy runs only in development and tests. On a
  four-year Columbus save, twelve weeks of play went from 68 seconds to under 6,
  and the saved result is byte-for-byte the same as before.

- **The Journal's "time since" matches the time that passed** When a year was
  let run on, the next line said how long it had been in words a person would
  use, but the steps were too coarse. A Caribou life read "The better part of
  two years later, and you're 6 now" after 13 months, and a Winnemucca life read
  "A year on" after nine. The phrases now follow the real gap: most of a year, a
  year, a year and a half, the better part of two years, then whole years.

- **The title screen no longer says you have no saved games when you do** If a
  saved game could not be opened — most often because it was written by a newer
  version — the title screen counted it as nothing at all. It offered to import
  one, and Continue sat dead with no explanation, while the game had
  deliberately kept that life and said so on the Saved games screen. The title
  screen now says a saved game needs attention, and Continue says why it cannot
  be pressed. Nothing about how saves are stored has changed; the life was
  always still there.

- **A town's council is named for what it is, not "governing body"** Every
  town's council seat used to read "Member of the governing body", on a body
  called "City of Seattle governing body". The office list, the campaign, the
  Who governs page and the day's summary now name the body. Where the game has
  read the town's own government, that name is used, as in the Anchorage
  Assembly or the Bowling Green Board of Commissioners. Everywhere else the body
  is named by the kind of town: Seattle's reads as the Seattle City Council,
  whose members are Council members; a village's is its Village Board, whose
  members are Trustees; a town has a Town Council and a borough a Borough
  Council. Those kind-of-town names are the game's placeholder until each town's
  own body has been researched, and some towns really use another name, such as
  a commission or a select board. Saved campaigns and seats carry on unchanged.

- **Hard times no longer cut births twice** Births in a town were being lowered
  twice when unemployment rose: once by the researched amount, about 1.4% for
  each point, and again by an older rule the game made up, about 29% fewer at
  twice the normal jobless rate. Now only the researched amount applies.

- **Town elections no longer draw a resident who has died** A town election
  could draw a candidate from a household member who had already died, and the
  game stopped with "Election contest candidate is deceased at scheduling date."
  The draw now passes over anyone no longer living, so the next living resident
  files instead. Found in a watched game in Evergreen, Missouri, where a village
  board candidate who died in June 2028 was drawn again for the 2029 race.

- **Town laws keep their on-adoption date** Town ordinances governed by the
  game's on-adoption rule now take effect when adopted. Their recorded effective
  dates remain the same after Continue.

- **A town's proposals can now be adopted, not only withdrawn** A town
  government posts small proposals for public comment, such as the rules for
  reserving a park shelter or a road repair schedule. Nobody could adopt one.
  Unless a resident commented, every proposal was eventually withdrawn: 111 of
  111 in two long runs where nobody was played. A proposal can now end adopted,
  and the news says so. Which ending comes is an even chance among the ones
  still open. That is a marked placeholder until the real shares of adopted,
  amended, tabled and withdrawn proposals have been researched.

- **A town council seat reads as an office everywhere, and councils follow
  national shares** A player who won a seat on their town's council used to read
  "You do not hold a job or an office right now" just above the line naming the
  body they sat on. The seat now counts as their role. Under Who governs, the
  members seated on a town's governing body are named. A party host's advice
  about running for office now includes the town's own council, where it used to
  say that no office was known. Counties read the way Americans say them:
  "Washington County", not "County of Washington". "St." keeps its period, as in
  St. Louis County. Towns whose own rules the game has not read now get their
  council size and term from the national shares of American municipal councils.
  Five seats and four-year terms are the most common. The screen still says
  these are typical rather than the town's own.

- **Trait seeding shares one person's unchanged upbringing** Seeding a person's
  traits reuses one lazy reading of their existing upbringing and its qualities
  while only trait records and catalog definitions change. Core, registered and
  notable traits retain their values, record order, first record dates and
  existing-record protection. Public trait readers and dated family evidence
  retain their existing behavior; no reading is retained across people, calls or
  other world changes.

- **Public college tuition uses recorded annual prices and saved school terms**
  College applications save sourced annual tuition and the institution's
  recorded term count. Billing splits the annual amount across those saved
  terms, with the minor-unit remainder in the last term. Recorded
  state-controlled colleges carry their state identity. The existing price-cost
  engine caps revised unpaid charges at the operative recorded school price,
  preserving prior terms and payments. Extra periods needed to earn credits
  remain charged. The aggregate state forecast is removed. An actual adopted
  tuition-backfill appropriation pays the recorded reduction through the
  existing public-payment writer; without that authority, the school receives
  only the capped tuition.

- **Choices can remain unanswered when reasons do not select an option** When
  constraints leave exactly one available option, that option is selected. With
  two or more available options, a person with no formed answer or equally
  supported alternatives first uses their last eligible recorded answer. If the
  person has no recorded consideration and no eligible answer, exact means from
  comparable people in the current game may separate the choice. Equal recorded
  reasons remain unanswered. Estimates save their actual donor traces, dates,
  cutoffs, counts and spread. Equal estimates keep the choice unanswered.
  Decision results retain saved reasons without random boosts, rounded weights
  or alphabetical selection, including after reload.

- **Unemployment comes back after a downturn** Unemployment used to climb in
  every world and never come down. Six test saves that began at 5.0% ended
  between 6.8% and 10.8% after 13 to 16 years. Now a slump or a town's disaster
  raises it, and it drifts back toward a normal rate of 4.6% once the cause
  ends, about halfway in four years. A town's gap from the national rate fades
  the same way. Recorded months are unchanged; the recovery speed and the 4.6%
  are placeholders filed with the research queue.

- **People in unincorporated towns now form principles from their town's size**
  A person's principles take a pull from the size of the town they live in, but
  the size was read only from the annual Census estimate, which covers
  incorporated places alone. In census-designated towns, such as Gallipolis
  Ferry, West Virginia, the town had no size, so small-town and big-city pulls
  never formed. The size now falls back to the American Community Survey count
  the public budgets already use.

- **A crime nobody reported now reads as what happened to you** When you are
  assaulted or robbed, or your home is broken into or vandalized, and nobody
  reports it, your Journal now says so by name. It used to read "An assault in
  Juneau went unreported." That looked like a crime you could not have heard of.
  Only the people it happened to see it.

- **Pardon bodies without members leave requests pending** A required pardon
  body no longer answers or grants a request from an invented reading of the
  case. The saved request waits for actual member decisions. Sentence expiry and
  sourced advisory deadlines remain available.

- **Every state's veto override bar is its own constitution's** Before, 38
  legislatures the game builds for itself (37 states and Puerto Rico) drew their
  veto override bar from a list of fractions. In 21 of them the draw was wrong.
  California needed only half of each chamber, though its constitution requires
  two-thirds of the membership. Hawaii needed three-fifths where its
  constitution says two-thirds, and Indiana two-thirds where its constitution
  says a majority of the members elected. Now each of those legislatures
  overrides a veto by the fraction its own constitution sets, counted the way
  the constitution counts it: members elected, members present, or members
  present and voting. Each bar cites its section and quotes it. Virginia, whose
  reading was already on file, now applies its two-thirds of members present
  instead of only its second condition, a majority of the members elected.
  Georgia, Mississippi and New Hampshire were read from a search engine's
  excerpt of the official text, because their official pages refused the
  connection. The days a governor has to act on a bill, during the session and
  after the legislature adjourns, were drawn the same way. Each is now the
  state's own figure from The Council of State Governments' Book of the States 2023. Where that table gives no count of days (Maine, New Jersey and South
  Carolina after adjournment), the game uses the most common figure it gives for
  other places and marks it estimated.

- **Accepted jobs, past invitations, race results and the Journal read as they
  should** A job offer you have accepted no longer says it is waiting for your
  answer. The day says when the work begins, and Jobs drops the Accept and
  Refuse buttons once you have answered. Party meetings and other optional
  invitations whose time has passed leave the Places list instead of piling up
  there. At a public meeting, the meeting's buttons sit in their own card above
  the Day and Week controls instead of on top of them. After a race, the result
  comes first. The campaign log says a repeated session once, with how many
  there were and when, and your line in the results is marked "(you)". The
  report on your campaign plan speaks to you, in dollars: "You chose direct
  outreach in Presque Isle, with no money set aside for it." The Journal says a
  sentence that recurs through a chapter once, after the chapter's other events,
  with how many more there were and the date of the last. Employer names no
  longer end in "(fictional)", and a job's national pay reads like "$17.03 an
  hour, $35,410 a year".

- **You can walk home after party work again** After a party meeting at the
  community room, Walk home answered "Nothing changed." A phone shift from home
  had been booked for the minute the meeting ended, and the walk ran into it. 1.
  Party work you ask for now leaves time to walk home first. 2. When something
  starts before you could get home, Walk home names it. 3. A phone shift can be
  worked from Places when you are home. 4. Missed community-room trips no longer
  pile up under Places. 5. Nobody under eighteen is offered party work.

- **Moving between recorded town homes changes your ward** Your ward follows the
  address of your recorded primary town home. Moving between homes with recorded
  positions changes your ward to match the new address.

- **Washington rent renewals read the announced annual cap** Washington's
  starting rent law now reads the officially announced 9.683% annual cap for
  2026 instead of the statutory 10% ceiling. Its next dated phase reads the
  announced 10% cap for 2027. Both retain the sourced 12-year construction
  exemption. Additional property and landlord exemptions still depend on
  recorded facts; this correction does not establish those facts.

- **Saved legislative election results are available to the World record** The
  World record now has a read-only account of saved congressional and state
  legislative general elections, including the bodies, seat counts, and named
  winners. The Watch World screen will display these grouped results when its
  interface change is integrated.

- **Local law stories reach the people living there** Published stories about a
  law's effects now reach living residents through current household residence
  records. Moving into or out of a town changes this reach. The existing routes
  for directly affected people, sponsors and recorded local floor voters remain
  available. Personal differences in news attention are not yet modeled.

- **Clearer filing, news and setup screens** The newspaper no longer explains
  that its officeholders are fictional. Filing for office no longer says the
  campaign committee opens with nothing in it. Shift coverage uses “schedule”
  instead of “rota.” The title screen shows Quit only when the native Quit
  action is available. The place step hides its search after a selection and
  restores it through Change. It no longer shows the federal fair-market-rent
  benchmark. The setup replay disclosure is shown only with diagnostics enabled.
  News no longer repeats current officeholders from the Government screen.

- **Keep the recorded attendance when a public meeting ends** Public meetings
  retain the seated officers' recorded attendance from entry through completion
  and saved games. A meeting without an established chair does not invent a host
  or attendees. Person cards omit unavailable Talk and connection controls.

- **Weekly jobs use the same paycheck writer as town payroll** Weekly job pay
  now posts through the town compensation period writer, including its federal
  and state withholding and recorded paid-leave claims. Existing contracts keep
  their weekly dates and gross pay terms.

- **Lawmakers weigh each law's arguments before voting** Lawmakers weigh how
  much each argument matters to the bill alongside their own convictions. Older
  laws keep their previous influence when no argument weight is set.

- **Plainer labels on the household money, places and office screens** The rent
  graph under Who you are is named for your town ("Lincoln, NE"), not a federal
  rent-area code. The household's shared account says "The household has no
  shared money yet." The places list is headed "Places you can go", and a player
  who holds no office reads "You hold no office yet. Campaigns shows what you
  could run for."

- **Plainer glossary wording, and the Legal tab's sentences written in one
  place** The glossary entries for the minority leader and cosponsors no longer
  lean on the phrase "on the record". The phrase now appears only in the press
  term of that name. The Legal tab's sentences about where the player stands,
  and the Conditions page's empty-state line, are now written by the
  presentation layer instead of typed into the screens. Several charges now read
  "Three charges against you are waiting for your plea."

- **Work omits empty decision headings** Work shows Waiting on you only when an
  outstanding decision is actually listed. Other pending work records remain
  visible.

- **An offer of work now says it is waiting for your answer** Asking an employer
  for work writes the offer straight away, and until now only the career panel
  that made the request ever said so. Everywhere else the day said "You do not
  hold a job or an office right now", which was true and left out the part that
  mattered: there was an offer on the table waiting to be accepted or refused. A
  player could ask for a job on their first day, let three months pass checking
  regularly, and conclude that nothing had ever come of it. The day's own
  summary now names an offer that has not been answered, and it appears under
  what is waiting on you alongside everything else that needs a decision. It is
  still not a job, and it is not counted as one until you accept it. While the
  start date is still ahead the day says when the work would begin. Once that
  date has gone by it is not repeated, because an offer that has sat unanswered
  for weeks should not keep naming a start date in the past.

- **Workplace conversations follow the recorded location and coworkers** An
  opening workplace uses the current job, shift and recorded location to
  identify coworkers. Conversations retain the actual workplace jurisdiction. An
  unsolicited opening scene requires recorded reasons when no current event or
  continuation selects it.

- **The world report is written even when a run is too large to keep** A
  development tool fix. The world report serialized the whole finished world
  before writing its report, so a long run (four or more years) lost its report
  to the JavaScript string limit. The report is now always written. A new
  `--summary <path>` option writes the behavior checks (bills filed and enacted
  by year, states enacting, chambers that never pass, presidents, months without
  pay, rent or tax) straight from the finished world.

- **Worlds run past their first day again** Two changes that each worked alone
  met on main and stopped every new world on its first day with "onDate is not
  defined". People's traits are now seeded on the day the game asks for, and
  worlds run on.

- **Unrest no longer ends just because nobody measured the anger** Unrest in a
  state used to count as over whenever no reading of public anger was on hand,
  as if no news were good news. Now it ends only when the anger read for that
  quarter has fallen back to the line, or when the quarter's reading found
  nothing left to carry. A threat made during that unrest stays open the same
  way, rather than lapsing because a reading was missing.

- **Year skips in childhood take a fraction of the time on big saves.** "Let the
  year run on" advanced a month at a time and checked the entire save after each
  month. Each scheduled item also triggered its own check. A year skip now
  checks the save once. The checks for laws, national elections, political views
  and their sources look records up instead of searching every record, and a
  view's earlier version is found in one pass instead of once per record. On the
  68 MB Ketchikan save, two year skips went from 49 and 61 seconds to 9 and 11
  seconds in Node, and the saved world is byte-identical.

- **You can turn down a personal invitation again** Somebody asks whether you
  will come to something on Saturday. It goes on your calendar as a tentative
  hold, and you can say no to it. That last part had stopped working. The hold a
  personal invitation writes now names you as the person responsible for it,
  because an invitation you mean to keep is one you have to be able to walk to,
  and the game refuses to perform an activity nobody is responsible for. The
  screen that offers the refusal was still looking for a hold with nobody's name
  on it, so it found none and offered nothing: every invitation sat on the
  calendar until its day came and went. The refusal now recognizes a hold in
  your own name, and still refuses one that somebody else owes the answer for.

- **The room's "What's happening" button is now "Your choices here," and you can
  reach it** In a room, the button that opens who is with you and what you can
  do now reads "Your choices here" instead of "What's happening." It also sits
  above the bar at the bottom of the screen: before, the bar covered its lower
  half, and a click on it could land on the bar instead. Once the choices are
  open, "Return to the room" sits clear of the bar too. The People window, and
  the other windows opened from the bar, now end above the bar as well. Before,
  the last conversation starters in People could sit under it where a click
  could not reach them.

- **Your own election result now reaches you** When your own race was decided,
  nothing outside the campaign screen said so. The note of what happened while
  time passed left out anything you had taken part in. Your election result
  names you as a candidate, so a lost race for city council or mayor went by
  with only the week's city business in the note. Your result, win or loss, now
  appears with the vote shares in each place a decisive moment in your life
  does: - The note of what happened while you were away leads with it: "You lost
  the race for Mayor, 45.6% to 54.4%." The other candidates are there to open,
  and once you have caught up it is not shown again. - The next moment of your
  life tells you. When it covers the time since your last choice, the result
  comes right after how long it has been. A first moment includes it for a week
  after the election. - The record in your Journal keeps it beside the rest of
  your life. The line under the result on the campaign screen also used "them"
  and "theirs" for everyone. It now uses your character's own pronouns: "That is
  a thing that happened to him, not the end of him." After a win, the campaign
  screen now says so once the term has started: "The term began January 1,
  2027." Before the term starts, it says the office is not yet his, hers or
  theirs, using the character's pronouns. On the legislative office page, the
  line about the chamber's business uses the character's pronouns too. A victory
  speech or concession can be given for three days after the result. After that
  it is no longer offered, and a speech you gave stays on the record. A state
  legislative seat is now named for the state's own chamber. Your role, your
  campaign committee and your rivals' committees say "the Texas House of
  Representatives" or "the Nebraska Legislature" instead of "the House of
  Representatives". Races filed before this change keep the names they were
  filed with.

- **Your Journal says when a crime happened to you** A crime in your Journal now
  reads as what happened to you: "You were assaulted, and police in Fairbanks
  took the report." It used to repeat the town's police log word for word. That
  read like a stranger's crime showing up in your own life story. Crimes that
  did not happen to you stay in the news.

- **A split city no longer shows the wrong House member** The opening scenes
  showed a San Antonio life the U.S. Representative for Texas's 1st district,
  which is in East Texas. The scene took the first House seat in the home state.
  It now names a House member only when the state has a single seat, so that
  member is certainly the player's. San Antonio is split across several
  districts, so its lives see their two Senators and no Representative until the
  game can place a home in one district. The Windows packaging check was also
  red on every pull request, because a test of the macOS-only update helper
  called a program by its macOS path. That test now skips on other platforms and
  says why.

### Changed

- **A degree needs its credits, not only the years paid for** Paying for every
  term no longer hands you the degree by itself. Each term you take a share of
  the credits your program requires, and how many you earn depends on the hours
  you already owe to work and care that week. If you are short at the end of the
  planned terms, you keep studying, and paying, one term at a time until you
  have the credits, up to half again the program's length. Past that, your
  studies end without the degree.

- **A governor's resignation now reaches national papers, and a statewide
  officer's or member of Congress's gets more attention.** A resignation used to
  be weighed like any other named event about an office, so a governor standing
  down could stay in the state's own papers. Now the record of a resignation
  says how big the news is, by the office given up. A governor's is national
  news. A statewide officer's or a member of Congress's is notable. These
  weights are placeholders until the research question on how much coverage a
  resignation gets is answered.

- **A home costs what homes cost in your county** Buying a home no longer costs
  $250,000 everywhere. The price now starts from the median home value in your
  county, from the Census Bureau's 2020-2024 American Community Survey, and
  moves with the world's prices from there. The down payment and the monthly
  payment scale with the price.

- **Home prices get a measured monthly model, ready for the housing market to
  use** The game now has one place that works out how a town's home prices move
  in a month. It follows three things measured across every state from 1980 to
  2024: last year's price change, growth in income per person, and changes in
  the 30-year mortgage rate, and it pulls prices back when they run far above
  what local incomes support. Nothing is drawn. The housing market reads it once
  it lands, so home prices change with the town instead of standing still.

- **A town group against a law starts with the neighbor who founded it** When
  six people in a town lose the same right or the same eligibility to a law, the
  group they form against it now starts with a member: the first of them with
  the temper to stand up alongside the others. Until now such a group could
  appear with nobody in it. A patient neighbor leaves the founding to someone
  quicker to act, and the group's record says what the law took from the person
  who founded it.

- **A parent's pay arrives while a child plays** When you play a child and move
  time forward, the working parent in the household is now paid each week, as an
  adult's own pay already was.

- **A state law on official pay changes the paychecks of its governor,
  legislators and judges** When a state's legislature passes a law that sets its
  governor's, a legislator's or a trial court judge's salary, the office holder
  is paid the new salary from the first week that begins on or after the law's
  effective date. A law can say it reaches only terms that begin after its date,
  and then a holder already in office keeps the old pay. Congress is not covered
  yet. California legislators are now paid $128,215, the salary in effect since
  December 4, 2023. A legislator in a state that pays by the day or the week
  (Utah, Vermont, Virginia, New Mexico) is paid an estimate from what the other
  states pay, not the flat $60,000 it was before.

- **A research question is filed on how mortgage rates follow the policy rate**
  Home prices in the game move with changes in the 30-year mortgage rate. Where
  the game carries only the policy rate, it uses that change as a labeled
  stand-in. The new question asks how much of a policy-rate change reaches the
  mortgage rate, so the stand-in can be replaced with a measured value.

- **A recorded outside shock ends when forces withdraw** High or severe
  international tension, or U.S. forces introduced into a crisis, now produces a
  public outside-shock event from the recorded crisis. War Powers reports and
  decisions keep its cause chain visible, and recorded de-escalation or
  withdrawal ends it.

- **Whoever files a bill has met the questions it is about** A bill names the
  policy questions it bears on, such as whether the state should levy an income
  tax. Until now nothing read that list, so nobody in any save had ever come
  across a policy question except in the demo. The person who sponsors a bill
  now has each of its questions on record as something they have encountered,
  dated to the day they filed it. That says only that they met the question, not
  which way they lean. It is the first input the pass that forms people's
  political views will read.

- **A town has the kinds of businesses it really has, with staff and sales to
  match** The grocery stores, hardware stores, diners, garages, law offices,
  accounting offices, contractors and salons in a town now follow the county's
  published counts and the town's share of its population, instead of the same
  eight businesses in every town. A town of under a hundred people may have one
  diner and nothing else; a city of 240,000 has two of every kind in the game.
  Staff per business follow the county's employees per establishment, and what a
  business takes in follows the state's published sales per employee. A new life
  seats at most two businesses of a kind and three staff in each, to keep it
  quick. Puerto Rico and the territories keep the earlier list, marked as
  unresearched, because their town populations are not held.

- **A town's households follow the town** The mix of people living alone,
  couples, couples with children, single parents and housemates in a town now
  comes from the Census Bureau's counts for that town, not one fixed mix for
  every town. A town with no count uses its state's, then the nation's.

- **National laws use their saved federal court** A court test now carries an
  authored federal tax bill through Congress and the President's actual desk,
  then binds the enacted law to the saved Supreme Court and its seated judges.
  The same binding survives saving and loading; removing that court leaves no
  substitute court. This proves court selection. It does not add a judicial
  challenge or a national tax-law precedent, and does not claim that a national
  judicial ruling occurred. The complete changed judicial-review test file
  passed five tests. Scoped types found no diagnostics. The A100 static checks
  increased from seven of eight on the pinned main source to eight of eight on
  this branch.

- **Court review requires a recorded claimant and filing** A matching law no
  longer receives an automatic ruling from the precedent table. A personal
  challenge must name a saved court, a real claimant, their complaint, and a
  prior stamped consequence naming them under that law. The filing is saved
  before the existing review writer runs; a ruling points back to that filing.
  The reader admits stamped events and saved legal-outcome consequences.
  Organization claimants, other harm-record families and a player-facing filing
  entry are not included. Positive filing inputs in the unit tests are
  explicitly authored compliance burdens, not native gas-law effects. The
  national enactment test retains its full canonical save check; the legacy
  authored state-law fixture uses only a JSON data round-trip. The complete
  changed court test file passed six tests with unchanged limits. Scoped types
  found no diagnostics. The A101 static check moved from zero of one on the
  pinned main source to one of one on this branch. Earlier failing fixture and
  setup receipts are retained.

- **The saved criminal case supplies its sentence floor** The prosecution writer
  resolves the actual case's operative custody floor once, then supplies it to
  the existing judge's sentence-kind and sourced term decisions. The saved
  sentence names that floor's law and source records. The sentence-kind trace is
  still saved before term selection; an already saved pending-case decision is
  reused with the published actor and case guards. No sentencing range, offense
  coverage, weight or legal amount is invented or changed. Unsupported ranges
  still leave the case pending. The complete changed legal-outcome test file
  passed 71 tests, including five actual case fixtures drawn from all 56 places.
  Scoped types found no diagnostics. Existing sentence ranges, recorded choices,
  repeal, attribution and replay assertions remain.

- **How long a criminal case takes now follows the state it is tried in**
  Before, every case in every state took the same number of days to reach its
  plea or trial. Now eighteen states use the median days from felony filing to
  disposition that their courts reported, so the same plea hearing falls on
  different days in different states. Every other place uses the average of
  those figures.

- **Making Laws schedules use the shared session calendar** State governing
  seasons, institutional bill steps, local member agenda intake and ordinary
  council readings use the body's shared session calendar. Saved calendar rows,
  session-year admission and legal reading minimums retain their separate force.
  The existing game timing rows remain provisional game profiles.

- **Legislative scheduling shares one calendar reader** Congress and council
  sittings read their timetable from the same calendar reader. The existing game
  schedules keep their dates, and legal reading limits keep their separate
  force. This change does not claim those game schedules are the bodies' real
  meeting dates.

- **Town elections follow candidate and voter choices** Residents decide whether
  to run for town office from their temperament and health. Their views of the
  candidates determine their votes. Office service anniversaries prompt
  resignation reviews. An unresolved tie produces no winner.

- **Arizona and Florida legislative ages follow constitutional records** Before:
  Four legislative office ages carried game-profile values. After: The existing
  dated candidacy and guidance path reads Arizona's constitutional age 25 and
  Florida's age 21 for both chambers from October 2, 2026 observations. Earlier
  history remains unproved. No filing, residency or term-limit rule changes.
  Replaces: Four profile-only age values through existing dated qualification
  records; no new export or qualification engine.

- **Midterm standing follows recorded views of the president** The national
  component follows the change in favorable views of the sitting president since
  entry into office. Each recorded adult counts once. Dated records and their
  sequence preserve the inauguration baseline. This draft checkpoint estimates
  missing new presidential views from current in-game official support. It
  reports the donor people and belief records, recorded office-entry stage,
  mean, and observed game spread. New presidential view records take precedence.
  It uses no static historical curve. Validation: the changed test file passed
  9/9; the scoped strict check passed with zero diagnostics. This checkpoint is
  not READY. An empty support donor pool still reaches the old zero behavior:
  NOT FIXED. The estimate label and its provenance are query metadata; the
  player estimate UI is NOT WIRED.

- **Game estimates reuse one mean and spread calculation** The existing mean and
  population spread calculation is available to game estimate readers without
  importing payroll. Withholding keeps its existing public exports and
  calculation.

- **New couples in town are two women or two men as often as the town's first
  couples** Before, every resident had their own 6% chance of looking for a
  partner of their own gender. That share was set by hand and is about four
  times the share of same-sex couples a town opens with. Now each town's women,
  and its men, have exactly the town's share of people looking for a partner of
  their own gender: the same 1.5% the opening uses for its couples, rounded to
  whole people. Nothing is drawn. Who among them it is stays the same from
  season to season, so nobody changes from one quarter to the next. No place has
  its own figure on file yet, so every place uses the national estimate.

- **How well off a child's family is now comes from what the household actually
  earns** A child's family money now comes from the household's recorded pay,
  compared with the poverty line for a family of that size in that state: at or
  under the line is hard scarcity, up to twice the line is strained, and above
  that is secure. When the game has no pay on record for that part of someone's
  childhood, such as an adult who grew up before the story begins, they take the
  median American child's level, which is secure, instead of a random one.
  People already in a saved game keep the temperaments they have.

- **People's temperaments and everyday preferences now come from how they were
  raised** The notable qualities a person starts with, their five core leanings
  and their everyday preferences (how they talk with people, how they spend free
  time, what they want for themselves) now come from their upbringing: the
  family's money, the care they had, school and a first job. Two people raised
  the same way start out the same. Where an upbringing leans neither way, the
  person starts in the middle instead of at a random end. People already in a
  saved game keep what they have.

- **The people the world invents now match the country's real mix of women, men
  and nonbinary people** People the world makes up are now about 50 percent
  women, 49 percent men and 1 percent nonbinary, following the national census
  count and a national survey, instead of an even split with 4 percent
  nonbinary. People already in a saved game keep who they are.

- **Where a town household lives now follows what it earns** At the start of a
  game, each household in town lives in the home its pay carries: an owner when
  the pay covers the town's monthly payment, in a house, a large house, a
  farmhouse for a farm household or a mobile home, and a renter in an apartment
  or rowhouse otherwise. The kind of home is no longer chosen by chance.

- **Recorded family estimates retain their game inputs** A pure family reader
  reports averages and population spread from the game's saved kinships, with
  contributor people, relationship IDs and existing birth dates. Its samples
  distinguish recorded family links from missing links; it creates no people or
  birth dates. Opening-family generation now reuses the recorded pattern across
  their observed spread and estimates whole-year intervals from saved parent
  ages, rather than rolling external percentages or ranges. Generated family
  links retain the estimate label and compared record IDs; missing age evidence
  does not create a guessed birth date. Town job matching now reads active work,
  education and primary care records. Age and world seed no longer invent
  retirement or job-seeking status. Old enrollments and household hints do not
  replace active records; unmatched eligible residents still use the existing
  job-matching and summary paths. Adult opening families read the existing town
  cohort before family selection. Stable receiving keys retain saved patterns
  and their population spread, with siblings also identified by their recorded
  shared parents.

- **Track explicit assumptions through their review backlog** The source-marker
  review now records exact marker text and rejects newly introduced or edited
  markers without a mapping. Recorded review steps remain open until their
  sourced replacements are delivered. Explicit warnings about unapproved
  candidate scene geometry remain warnings, with their asset review still
  pending.

- **School tuition readers live with the existing education price records**
  School tuition coverage, operative-price quotes and saved-term installment
  readers now live in the existing education tuition module. Billing and the
  price-cost engine consume the same readers from that module. Their
  calculations, prior terms, family payments and recorded public backfill stay
  unchanged; the old one-law budget module is retired.

- **A state transit law now reads like every other spending law.** A passed
  state transit law no longer has its own separate route. Its money is shown as
  one spending authority, with the same dates, payments and service as any other
  program, and a rider's completed trip is recorded the same way as other public
  services. Saved games with a transit law keep working.

- **Automatic bill profiles resolve their actual jurisdiction's legislative
  pack.** Federal automatic bill profiles use the existing jurisdiction-to-pack
  and legislative work-key readers. Supplied contexts must match that
  jurisdiction's pack and work key. Existing state and local mapping paths and
  authored bill wording remain intact.

- **Recorded local governments carry their own employer identity**
  Local-government organizations created from an active, dated government-unit
  record now retain that government's exact identity. This prepares their
  payroll records to resolve the canonical public account without treating a
  workplace name, location or funding source as proof of legal ownership. This
  change opens no cash account and invents no balance. Separate generated
  schools and offices still need their own sourced legal-employer relationship;
  they are not assigned to the nearest government by this change.

- **Payroll uses the recorded government's canonical public account** A town job
  whose employer profile records a sourced government identity now uses that
  government's canonical public account as its payer. Its workplace remains the
  employer for pay periods and teacher salary rules; settlement and law
  consequences validate the recorded worker against the dated public payer.
  Existing account cash is reused. No cash estimate, tax rate, pay formula or
  payment-cap rule changes. Employers with no recorded government identity keep
  their existing payer; neither their name nor location invents a public owner.
  City-office and county-clerk jobs now use the actual canonical municipal or
  county government organization during setup. Existing government identity and
  cash records therefore reach normal town payroll. Civic role counts reuse
  those same organizations when another household is written. A place with an
  ambiguous government join is not assigned to the first listed government.
  School, police, fire, state-office and federal-office ownership is outside
  this bounded producer repair until their employer ownership records are
  admitted; a program's funding government is not treated as its legal employer.

- **Opening jobs read recorded employers directly** Ordinary adult hiring reads
  the existing recorded employer selector without loading the legacy business
  revenue engine. Selection, pay, work records, repeat behavior and saved games
  keep their existing behavior.

- **Council title checks use the declared rule template** The D.C. council title
  check uses the shared title renderer and its admitted rule pack's title
  template. The unused council-specific forwarding function is removed; bill
  filing continues to use the same declared template.

- **Town profiles declare their existing single-vote reading interval** The town
  council game profile explicitly records zero elapsed days between readings for
  its existing single adoption vote. The shared bill driver reads this rule
  alongside the profile's declared quorum. The profile remains labeled as
  authored game procedure; it introduces no extra reading or waiting period and
  makes no claim about an unread town charter.

- **Administration bill families now read authored wording from data rows.**
  Three administration families retain their nine variants, standing assistance
  authority, ordered terms and evidence through the existing bill-family
  materializer. Existing number and year wording formatters preserve annual and
  singular/plural boundaries. The old inline bank and rendering path is removed.

- **Automatic amendment proposals share their initial writer** State amendments,
  federal reform proposals, and Article V proposals now share their initial
  proposal setup. Each route keeps its supplied wording, designation,
  constitutional change, deadline, and convention identity. An explicit text
  version or operative date remains part of the saved proposal. The existing
  producer still refuses invalid dates, sponsors, and ordinary bill links.
  Chamber votes, ratification, and replay guards keep their existing rules.

- **Appointments go to people the appointer knows, and a personal appointment is
  a favor owed** When a President fills the vice presidency or the Chief
  Justice's seat, a governor fills a Senate seat, or a mayor fills an empty
  council seat, they now choose from people they know instead of drawing a
  stranger. They weigh how much they trust the person, whether the person is in
  their own party, whether the person already serves in office, and what they
  owe or are owed. Their own temperament decides how much each of those counts.
  An appointment made on the merits is just the job. It is remembered as a favor
  owed only when it was personal: when someone better placed on party, record or
  experience lost to the appointer's trust or a debt. Someone who helped the
  appointer and was passed over remembers that too. Favors are paid back. A
  legislator who owes the person carrying a bill leans toward voting for it, and
  in a town election the households that owe a candidate lean toward that
  candidate. An ordinary hire is just a job, not a favor, and town public jobs
  are never handed out for politics: since the Supreme Court's 1990 ruling in
  Rutan v. Republican Party of Illinois, public employers may not hire
  rank-and-file workers for their politics. A debt fades with time, so a
  following lasts only while the favors keep coming.

- **Approval seeking affects public press responses** When someone named in a
  press request has a marked approval-seeking tendency, their recorded
  inclination now favors disputing the allegation.

- **An argumentative defendant weighs contesting the charge over a plea** A
  person who treats a disagreement as a contest over who is right now counts
  that habit as one reason to take a charge to trial instead of accepting the
  plea on offer. It weighs beside the evidence, the sentence, their principles
  and everything else they carry, so the same defendant with the same facts and
  no such streak still takes the plea.

- **Character art cannot reach a person without someone's name behind it**
  Nothing a player sees changes. What changes is what the game will accept. The
  rule that banked art and catalog art are different things, and that a person
  has to look at art before anyone can wear it, has been settled for some time.
  Where that look got written down was settled nowhere, so it was written down
  nowhere, and the function that admits a piece of art into the catalog had
  never once been called. Acceptance is now a file that names who looked, when,
  at what, and which pieces they accepted. Nothing in the project writes to it,
  including the tool that reads it, because a program that can record an
  approval is a program that can approve. The catalog is checked against it:
  every piece of art a person can be drawn from is either one of the forty-six
  development fixtures, listed one by one, or named in an approval. Listing them
  one by one rather than by batch is the difference between a rule and the
  appearance of one — a forty-seventh fixture appended to the old batch would
  have walked straight through the easier version. The file is empty, which is
  the truthful state. Nobody has accepted anything yet.

- **The art queue can ask for chart, card and tab-strip grounds too** Nothing
  changes in play yet. Behind the scenes, three more requests join the art queue
  for interface art the game currently draws from color values alone: the ground
  behind every economic chart, the card the Guide opens on a civics term, and
  the strip along the top of the Politics hub. Each was checked against the art
  already owned before it was asked for, and none of them is approved art yet.
  Alongside them, the width floor that says a picture must be at least 4608px
  wide now applies only to the art it was written for — a room plate and a title
  tableau, which fill a screen. A nameplate, a card ground or a chart frame is a
  few hundred pixels wide on purpose, and was being turned away for being the
  size it is meant to be; each of those is held to the width its own request
  declares instead.

- **Automatic bills use sponsor reference terms** Automatic appropriation bills
