  shown with what the record says and what your advisers said. A resident reads
  the emergency and is never handed a decision that is not theirs. If one of
  these comes up while you are passing time, the game says so and takes you to
  it.

- **Your office now shows what it is answerable for — programs, staff, your
  bills and casework.** Under Work, "Your office" now reads the rest of the
  officeholder's desk beside the staff briefing: the public programs your
  government has on record, what those services have to work with, what money
  has been put to the office and what the office has actually committed, who
  works there and since when, the bills you sponsored and where each one stands,
  and how the office handles constituent casework. An amount put to the office
  is plainly not a decision the office has taken. Where your government has no
  record — no program, no staff, no bill, no workflow — the panel says so in
  words instead of showing a zero or an empty post.

- **Name a birthday when you start a life** New Game still asks how old you are,
  and now also lets you name a birthday month and day. The game writes one date
  of birth from that age and the calendar of the place you start in. Lives you
  already saved keep the birthdays they had. The hometown question is now "Where
  are you from?"

- **Numbered district identity, with whole-place home membership where sourced**
  Legislative filings can name a published district identity. Residence in that
  district is proved only from World-established intervals. Choosing a district,
  a map point, a city, a county, or living in the state is not that proof. When
  Census relationship files show a home place wholly inside one
  state-legislative district, that membership is recorded with its vintage and
  chamber. Split places and older saves stay unknown. This is not a complete
  geographic system.

- **Real institution discovery and noncredit study options** Search the 2024–25
  NCES school and 2024–25/2025–26 college directories, compare reported
  offerings, and review supported game-authored noncredit study offers. Accepted
  study uses the existing calendar, attendance costs, interruption and return,
  and earned completion records. Directory listings never establish admission,
  historic attendance, official tuition, a degree or a license.

- **More of the places a life actually happens in, and honest edges where the
  picture runs out.** A community meeting room and an empty courtroom join the
  places the game can put you in, and the scene behind the life is chosen from
  where you are rather than repeated. Where a picture does not cover the whole
  frame, the game reports the uncovered part as uncovered instead of stretching
  the art to hide it.

- **Track supported executive decisions as office work.** Supported executive
  roles can receive appointments, assignments, and decisions through the same
  saved Work and history systems used elsewhere.

- **Win a governor's race in any state and actually govern, while Congress and
  statehouses keep turning over.** Running for governor now follows the office's
  own election calendar in every state, and a win leads to qualifying, taking
  office on the term's start date, and a staff briefing: choose a chief of
  staff, set a first priority, direct the agencies and see what they report
  back. Governors you don't play hold their own elections and make their own
  decisions, which show up in the news. Congress holds its regular elections, so
  seats no longer sit empty after January 2027. Where a state's real rules
  aren't in the game yet, the office says so and follows the game's own
  calendar.

- **Carry a real docket of bills, and keep working them week to week.** A
  legislator's desk now holds more than one measure at a time. Bills are drawn
  from composable families, so a docket can hold several unrelated ones in a
  single life and each still knows what it is. Filing checks the authority the
  life actually holds and refuses plainly when it does not hold it, and the work
  carries forward instead of resetting.

- **New everyday moments at work and in local politics, only when your life has
  them** A few more ordinary moments can now come up: a break shared with a
  colleague, being paired with somebody from your political group to knock on
  doors, and — when the people at your job really do direct your work — your
  supervisor asking you to pick up an extra shift, or to work one that falls on
  a class you have booked. These only happen when your life actually contains
  the people and the plans involved. A job and a course are not treated as a
  commute or a clash, a coworker is not treated as your boss, and the game no
  longer says you covered a colleague's shift just because you agreed to. That
  comes up later only if you actually worked it.

- **Build a life through study, work, and community commitments.** Pursue
  supported study and job paths, earn credentials and pay, recruit known people,
  and keep progress consistent across saves.

- **A political map of the country under Politics, Government.** Politics now
  has a map of all fifty states and Washington, D.C., with Alaska and Hawaii
  drawn as insets. Color it by House district or Senate delegation, or pick a
  state to see its state senate and state house districts, counties, and cities.
  Colors follow your world's actual parties, including any that form later.
  Vacant seats, ended terms, and offices your world doesn't track each look
  different, so a missing record never passes for a real result. Click a
  district to see who holds it, their party and term, and any elections there.
  Names open the person's card, and looking never moves you or changes the day.
  A history slider shows the map as your world recorded it on an earlier date,
  and later party switches don't change how the past looks. Your home and where
  you are now are marked. If your city is split between districts, the map lists
  every district your home might be in instead of guessing. Every district can
  also be reached from a searchable list.

- **Campaign planning now makes the next choice explicit.** Campaign planning
  now presents an attributed staff proposal, then lets you choose the priority,
  represented geography, and exact committee spending ceiling. The resulting
  report records what was chosen and what the campaign observed, while changed
  staffing or funds must be reviewed before committing.

- **Public meetings and private preparation for municipal members.** Residents
  of supported municipalities can inspect government records and attend public
  sessions created in their saved world. Members can prepare private meeting
  notes through scheduled work. Attendance and completed notes survive saving
  and reloading. The records distinguish researched government facts from
  sessions written for the game, and show when the sources do not establish a
  power.

- **See who governs where you live, and stand for your state's top office**
  Politics now has a place that names the city or town government and the county
  governments where you live, including a place that lies across more than one
  county and a place that has no city government at all. From the same screen
  you can see whether you may stand for your own state's executive office today,
  file if you may, and follow the honest outcome of that contest and its term.

- **Search the news you have already read** The published news you have
  collected can now be searched. Type in the search box at the top of the
  newspaper and only the stories that mention what you typed stay on screen —
  the headline, the story itself, the place it happened, anybody named in it,
  and any correction printed since. Clearing the box brings everything back. It
  searches the news this life has actually published. Nothing is hidden from you
  that you could see before, and nothing new is revealed: a story you have not
  been shown does not appear because you searched for it. The search box is
  available in ordinary play once published stories exist in your save,
  including after legislative proceedings appear in the newspaper.

- **Choose which established office to run for.** Explore supported offices by
  government level, inspect represented eligibility and recorded timing, then
  deliberately choose an office before filing. Browsing another office leaves an
  existing campaign and its money unchanged.

- **People opens as a relationship web with one person card** People now opens
  as a map of the connections this life actually has — family, household, work,
  politics, and recorded acquaintance — with search and a list still there as
  alternatives. Selecting someone, inspecting them in the room, or opening a pin
  uses the same card: portrait, what is known, who they connect to, pin, and
  Talk when that is available. Unknown stays unknown; a pin is only a saved
  reference.

- **A Places workspace for travel and attendance** You can now open Places to
  see where you are, which short walks and scheduled activities the game
  actually offers from here, and what each one would cost in time. Return home
  is disabled with the real reason when you are already home. Inspect opens
  local government without moving you or changing where you live. Attend uses
  the same canonical writers the rest of the game already uses. Reading Places
  does not advance time. Only explicit Travel, Return home, or Attend actions
  do.

- **Customize complete outfits and facial features in art review.** New internal
  art-review lives use six prepared standing body families with coordinated
  shirts and trousers. Supported skin, hair and clothing palettes, facial shapes
  and bounded proportions are saved with the person. Older people retain their
  existing appearance. These inputs remain unapproved candidate art.

- **Start with a birthday and import authored encounters** Player-facing change.
  Local-only candidate composition; no release-version, installed-app or
  production-art promotion. Existing private export holds remain. Receives J
  birthday with optional legacy-compatible month/day and explicit calendar
  refusal at creator Next; receives corrected structured X press with
  known-source wording and no compulsory prose. Options imports immutable
  external authored JSON through the existing save callback. Pack-bearing lives
  use16; ordinary unmodified lives remain15. Old readers refuse packed lives.
  Preserved private prepared materials accompany newer study/geography/focus
  root behavior. No saved person is repainted. Quiet UI, planner, coherent
  fresh-default and LIFE repairs remain independent owner continuations, not
  claimed present. Current T source is received separately, not an ordinary paid
  or installed claim.

- **Move a saved life with a file** Saved games can export a life to a file and
  import that file as a new save, in the browser or the installed app. Import
  never overwrites a life you already have. A preview-only life stays out of the
  ordinary game.

- **Ask a civic reporter about a public development** When this life has no
  journalist, you can establish a new authored civic reporter and news desk,
  then ask about a public development you were part of. A request is not their
  agreement. You can sit for the exchange without a preparation adviser.
  Off-the-record talks still stay unpublished.

- **Newspapers, reporters who come back, and money questions with
  consequences.** The news now has names. Three national outlets exist from your
  first day, and a state newsroom appears once state politics touches your life.
  Their reporters keep their jobs, remember who they have covered, and come back
  to you. A reporter can ask for your response before a story runs. You see the
  exact words of every answer before you give them, you can decline, and if an
  answer is a lie the game tells you so first. Staying silent is reported as
  silence, not as guilt. Sources agree ground rules before they say anything: on
  the record, background with an agreed description, or off the record.
  Off-the-record material never runs. Stories cite what actually happened. When
  the facts change, the story gets a follow-up or a correction, and the original
  stays on the record. Campaign money can now become a scandal, whether the
  misuse is real or only alleged. A rival can file a complaint about a payment
  that was legitimate, and the complaint can be dismissed. A real misuse can
  stay hidden unless someone who keeps the books finds it. Complaints follow the
  actual steps of the body that hears them, such as the Kentucky Legislative
  Ethics Commission or the Federal Election Commission. A complaint is never
  treated as a finding. Where the game has no researched procedure, it says the
  inquiry is simulated and cannot punish anyone.

- **People bring up what is actually happening in your life, and a lie can come
  back.** The people around you now start real conversations about real things:
  a housemate asks where you'll be on an evening you already have plans, a local
  party organizer calls to invite you to an open meeting, somebody close reacts
  when an election is decided, a friend or coworker asks a specific favor, a
  staff member asks what to do about a pending bill, and a reporter asks about a
  promise you made. People you know can now be asked to meet, and they answer
  for themselves: yes, no, or a different day that you can turn down in turn.
  Somebody you have not seen for a long time may call you first. Agreeing puts
  the evening on the calendar; it does not spend it. When somebody raises
  something they asked of you long ago, you can tell them the truth, say what
  you think you remember, refuse to get into it, or lie. An honest mistake and a
  lie are not the same thing, and the person who was there is the one who knows
  better. A childhood is now lived before it is directed: in the early years the
  adult at home decides and the child is left with the memory, and the choices
  become yours as you grow. Two people who live together can decide to have a
  family, and the day arrives in its own time. After a death, the people who
  were told can say something to each other about it; nothing is required. Each
  of these comes in more than one situation. An organizer checks in after you
  turn a meeting down, or asks whether you want to join after you went. The
  person you live with asks whether you are really running once you file, and
  marks your first day in office. A reporter asks why you are running. If you
  promised someone a quiet evening at home and something else is on the same
  night, they ask about it. Some answers can now be a deliberate lie, clearly
  marked before you choose it. Nobody finds out by magic. A lie is only
  discovered when someone who heard it later learns otherwise, such as a
  housemate seeing you leave or a source confirming your promise to a reporter,
  and then they bring it up with you. Talking still takes no time.

- **Public budget and dated economic context** Inspect Budget & economy from
  Politics without holding office. Available graphs identify their geography,
  units and dates, with exact-value tables. Reference observations stay separate
  from saved government history; missing information remains unavailable.
  Reading the page does not change budgets or move time.

- **Follow the publications you read** News now has For You, All, and
  publication views. You can follow a publication from its page, and that choice
  stays with the saved life when you come back. For You says why a story is
  there. All still carries every published story, and search, corrections, civic
  explanations, and links to people remain available in every view.

- **Attend a public sitting and appoint a compiled city manager** In
  Charlottesville, a resident can attend an authored public council sitting
  without gaining a seat. A seated council member can advance a City Manager
  election only when the same government's recorded vote meets the compiled
  Charter § 12 quorum of three councilors; one member's standing is not the
  appointment. That office and history survive save and reload. A visitor, a
  lone-member or no-quorum roll, a vote of another government, a second
  appointment, and a repeated write after reload are refused. Ordinance
  introduction and votes stay closed until the compiled introduction, readings,
  and post-adoption gaps are filled. Inspection does not create a sitting. The
  60/90-minute public-session helper remains authored review convenience, not
  ordinary meeting discovery.

- **Inspect recorded transit funding cash before requesting service** Expose a
  dated read-only cash snapshot through the supported transit office projection,
  with exact staged contract costs and distinct missing-account, missing-balance
  and recorded-cash states. Actual receipts determine cash; appropriations and
  estimates do not create it. Inspection reserves nothing and the existing
  payment writer rechecks authority and cash at settlement.

- **Charlottesville councilors can carry an ordinance from introduction to law**
  A seated Charlottesville councilor can now introduce an ordinance, put it on
  the council agenda and bring it to a vote once the city code's required three
  days have passed. The recorded result takes effect the day it passes. The
  rules come from the city's own code and charter. Other councilors' ballots are
  clearly labeled as game-authored until the game models how they decide. Nevada
  legislative candidates are also no longer turned away on the game's opening
  date by a qualification law that has been in force since October 1, 2025.

- **Record how your office handles votes and casework.** A seated member can set
  revisable voting and constituent-work preferences for the current office and
  read a briefing from actual staff. Preferences save only when Record is used.
  Opening the office does not cast a vote. Staff show known items on file, not a
  recommended package. A saved instruction applies only to the bill as it then
  read; a changed measure waits for a new decision.

- **Constitutional and charter process records** Track Congress-proposed Article
  V amendments, California legislative amendments/revisions, and Carson charter
  changes through Nevada measures. Retain proposal text, recorded bodies and
  denominators, authenticated state ratification, filing/effective/operative
  dates and historical rule versions. Supported prospective proposal-threshold
  changes affect later proposals. Open-ended text effects and unsettled
  procedures remain explicitly unavailable. The feature-local Politics tracker
  supplies A's mount; integration, independent review and installation remain
  separate delivery states.

- **Resolve supplied national electoral records separately from office entry.**
  Add dated national allocation, explicit certification and electoral
  ballots/count, separate presidential and vice-presidential outcomes,
  contingent-body checks, and qualified term entry/expiry through the existing
  clock and work histories. The results component reads supplied canonical
  totals. Missing national producers remain unavailable; no complete
  presidential campaign or media forecast is claimed.

- **Places — where you are, where you can go, and what is on** A new Places
  entry in the navigation collects what this life can actually do about where it
  is. It shows your current location, the places you can travel to with the time
  each one costs, and the public meetings you may attend with their duration
  stated before you commit to them. Going home is a separate action from
  traveling, and when you are already home it says so instead of offering you a
  trip you do not need. Looking at any of it changes nothing: reading the list
  does not move you, spend your time, or record anything.

- **Keep a government one click away** A government you are looking at can be
  pinned, and it stays on your shortcuts through a save and a reload. Opening it
  again brings that government straight back up. It does not move you, change
  where you live, or give you any standing there — it is a way back to a page,
  and where you live and what you may do are still whatever the world says they
  are. People you know can be pinned from the people list as well, so anyone you
  want to hand can be kept there whether or not they are in the room with you.

- **Your office says who has your bill next** The office already named the bill
  you are working on. It now also says who has it next and what they decide —
  the committee, the chamber's leadership, whoever the rules of that legislature
  actually put in the way — along with the bar a vote has to clear when there is
  one. That answer was already in the game, but only inside the bill itself, two
  steps away. Standing in your own office is where you should be able to see
  that something is waiting on you, or that nothing is.

- **Each new world starts differently, and parties can change** A new game now
  starts from its own political and economic conditions, drawn around the real
  2024 election results: most worlds look familiar, some differ modestly, and a
  few differ a lot. The President and Chief Justice now live in Washington
  instead of your hometown. Parties are no longer fixed at two: local party
  chapters have standing committees, and people who keep losing the same
  argument can found a new party or split away. Parties can also combine, rename
  or dissolve, and older records keep the party labels they had at the time.
  Replay links made before this update rebuild exactly the world they described.

- **Storms and floods happen on their own, and starting politics fit their own
  evidence** Floods, flash floods and severe thunderstorms now occur by
  themselves where you live, drawn from twenty-five years of recorded storm
  reports for your state, and they run through the existing damage, request,
  declaration and repair chain. A place with nothing to damage gets nothing.
  When someone dies, each relative learns of it once, and a private cause stays
  private unless it was actually shared with that person. Starting politics now
  use each office's own evidence: a seat with no real two-party contest keeps
  the party its record shows instead of borrowing the state's presidential
  result, and governors keep theirs.

### Improved

- **Review appearance and keep room placement consistent** Supported illustrated
  people can use matching listening and seated poses in private previews. Room
  placement uses the available floor and seat positions, and opening
  conversation keeps the people and camera in place. An outfit without a
  matching pose keeps its supported standing view. Review a supported appearance
  before beginning a life, keep matching portraits across People views, and
  import compatible art through the reusable private asset kit.

- **Identify the running game and clarify the birthplace question** The quiet
  corner stamp identifies the running game build. Patch Notes shows its full
  source identity and whether it includes uncommitted changes, keeping it
  distinct from another installed controller or saved life. The birthplace
  question now asks “Where are you from?”.

- **Keep accepted study terms and arrange tuition without stopping ordinary
  life** Accepted study keeps its price, duration, credential and cash-funding
  terms even when later offers change. Review an editable, game-authored tuition
  grace before enrolling. If a period cannot be funded, its disclosed deadline
  pauses only study; ordinary work and pay continue. Pay from available personal
  funds to resume, without an automatic loan or a second charge. Personal shows
  accepted weekly demands and reversible interruption choices. Saved noncredit
  offers support parallel applications without written assignments.

- **Office work and press exchanges use recorded choices instead of blank
  writing boxes** Office work and ordinary press exchanges now ask you to choose
  from what the record already holds: the subject, what you are asking for, your
  stance, and the exact wording shown before you commit. The reporter still owns
  the question. If you look for a journalist covering public affairs, that
  search is a contact in this life, not a developer construction. On-background
  attribution comes from a recorded work title. A planned meeting place names
  the arranged written or spoken channel; it does not invent a room. An
  interview answer cites a recorded fact, or an attributed stance when no
  correcting record is in hand. It does not treat the reporter’s question as
  something already established. Older typed statements stay in the saved
  record. Executive officeholding still has to be reached through a supported
  start; this does not add a new way to take office.

- **Attend now includes its disclosed journey** Moving to tomorrow now stops at
  unanswered commitments while routine work keeps its real time and pay.
  Optional holds can be declined without attending them. Where an authored
  journey exists, Attend shows and performs that travel as part of the same
  choice; unsupported routes say what is missing.

- **Browse local government from home, not a nationwide list** Local government
  now opens on where you live and your actual standing there, using the saved
  place scope so a state home reads as Kentucky rather than Kentucky, Kentucky.
  You can search other supported governments by name or state, see known people
  and public meetings, review pending work and attendance history, and keep a
  government pinned without it trapping the dropdown when you inspect somewhere
  else. Publisher references and research material stay available under Source
  review rather than on the normal screen. Inspecting another government still
  does not move you, change your residence, or grant any role there.

- **Lighter loading when opening a legislature.** Legislature screens no longer
  load the full municipal research inventory to look up their procedure. Local
  government records remain available when you inspect them, with the same
  source limits and authority checks.

- **Quiet rest keeps pending decisions and honest calendar actions** Begin still
  opens in the room without an intro or idle activity card. Pending situations
  stay reachable from a compact return. Calendar lists upcoming and ongoing
  events first, with history separate and event actions beside the selected
  future item. Refused simulated attendance does not move time. Contact, Meet,
  and Travel only run supported conversation, invitation, or journey writers, or
  they stay unavailable with a specific reason.

- **Quiet rest, honest talk, and a calendar that does not invent travel** New
  Game drops you into the room instead of an idle activity card. Talk is the
  in-person conversation in that room, with faces and no clock movement per
  line. Contact, Meet, and Travel stay separate: there is no remote inbox on the
  card, Meet returns you to the scene when they are actually there, and Travel
  names the recorded places and stays unavailable when no journey is authored.
  The calendar opens on upcoming and ongoing events, with History off to the
  side. Simulating an event stays unavailable unless standing preferences
  authorize attendance, and that check happens before time moves. Advance and
  Play remain their own actions. People on the web show portraits; a list face
  is not the same drawing.

- **The chosen hometown is introduced as a place, not a briefing** After you
  pick a town, the creator now shows its name and, when the accepted records
  name a county area, that county and its data year. It no longer repeats formal
  labels or talks as if politics were impossible there. Removing that extra
  commentary does not remove this name and county.

- **New Game stays on the title screen** Choosing New Game now keeps you in the
  same upper-left menu on the same room. The next step replaces the last one
  there, instead of opening a separate form further down the screen.

- **Today and Work are now two clear places instead of one crowded pile.** Today
  now answers four things and nothing else: what is happening, what is next on
  your calendar, what is waiting on you, and what you can do with the time. It
  links to Work, the calendar and places instead of carrying their panels. Work
  is one page for every kind of life — your office if you hold one, running for
  office, jobs and study, and hiring — and starts by saying what you do now,
  with a short list to jump to each part. The menu is grouped, and the duplicate
  "Life scenes" entry is gone: the scene is the room, and closing any page takes
  you back to it.

- **A chosen hometown can show its sourced population** After you pick a town,
  New Game can show a matching BEA population with the observation year and the
  geography that count belongs to. Towns without a matching place-level count
  stay unnamed rather than borrowing a county figure or showing zero.

- **The first minutes of a new life say where you are and what your choices do**
  The opening moments at home now say plainly what is going on: fifteen free
  minutes are called fifteen free minutes, and somebody you live with is named
  with who they are to you. A new moment lets an adult decide what to make time
  for — learning something, the people they know, or themselves — and reading or
  resting afterwards can keep that plan. A child's bedtime-shadow and
  dinner-plate moments now only come up in the evening, instead of at ten past
  nine in the morning. Moments you have already seen keep the words they were
  shown with.

- **Talking to somebody now happens in one conversation box in the room.**
  Choosing a person — in the scene, from their record, or from People — opens
  the same conversation box over the room. It shows who you are talking to and
  who they are to you, their face when the game has one, what the conversation
  is about, what you just said and what they said back, and what you can say
  next, all without a scrollbar. Earlier exchanges page back inside the same
  box, and Back returns you to the scene. Turning to somebody else keeps the
  conversation going and says whether they heard what was just said, instead of
  starting over with a greeting. Listen is offered only while somebody still has
  something to add. Conversations take the same time they always did.

- **Clearer conversations and choices** Household errands now name the tasks
  being discussed. Conversations distinguish an agreement from completed work
  and keep the details recorded in your life. Opening scenes and ordinary
  dialogue use clearer choices and replies. Revised optional setup questions
  preserve earlier answers and distinguish requests from guaranteed solutions.
  Older saved requests keep their original level of detail.

- **Clearer People, campaign and character creation screens** In People, a face
  is now the whole thing you click. Choosing someone lights up their connections
  and says, in plain words, how you know them, and the categories no longer call
  everyone you have met a friend. The campaign screen has one button for each
  thing you can do right now, keeps rule sources and dates tucked under a detail
  link, and no longer runs labels together. Character creation offers month
  names for your birthday, a button to pick a random name, a town list that says
  how many places there are, and it tells you straight away when an age is out
  of range.

- **The scene-first interface from the owner's sketch** The room rests quietly
  with a small corner control for who, when and where, plus Day and Week. The
  menu opens on click into Calendar, People, Politics, News, Journal, Personal,
  Travel, Options, Save and Quit, and Quit asks before leaving an unsaved life.
  Anybody you click — in the room, on the People web, in a list or a pin — opens
  one card on the right with their face, what they are to you, what you actually
  know, who they are connected to, and the real actions. Conversation sits at
  the bottom center with the faces along the top, the speaker lit, narration in
  italics and one line at a time; browsing elsewhere keeps the exchange and
  offers a way back. The Calendar opens on today and what is next, keeps History
  separate, and carries the interruption checklist that decides where a day or
  week skip stops.

### Fixed

- **One Back from a Politics tab returns to the conversation you left** Leaving
  a conversation to look something up in Politics no longer costs two presses to
  get back to it. Back from any tab of the hub closes the hub and returns you to
  the room with the same line still waiting, and Return to conversation keeps
  whatever you were reading before underneath, so the next Back goes where it
  always did.

- **Hometown lists start at A, not at Lexington** When you pick a state and
  browse towns, the list is alphabetical by the names you read. Lexington is
  still Lexington, Kentucky, and it is no longer pinned to the front of the
  first page just because the game already knew that city.

- **Keep a completed public meeting visible during the opening** The opening
  scene now uses the same source-backed municipal venue binding as later play.
  Completing an eligible public session keeps its actual canonical venue visible
  without creating attendance or borrowing another jurisdiction's geometry.

- **Letting time pass now says where it will stop, and moves once per click.**
  The "Let time pass" choice now shows the date it will reach before you press
  it, and it never runs past something already on your calendar. A click that
  arrives after time has already moved no longer moves it again.

- **Talking to somebody you live with actually opens a conversation** Choosing a
  household member who is in the room with you and asking to talk now opens the
  real conversation, rather than refusing without one. The words come from the
  same producer the rest of the game already uses, so what is said is what this
  life actually knows. Where a conversation genuinely is not available yet, the
  room tells you why instead of leaving the action grayed out with nothing said.

- **Stop a previous room's art from lingering in one with none.** Switching to a
  room that has no released art no longer keeps showing the last room's picture
  behind it. The rest of this change is test-only: widening an assertion to
  accept a legitimate household relationship, retiring one test for a shell
  layout that no longer exists, and giving a slower test more time to finish.

- **Preserve saved character catalog identity** Preserve saved character
  appearances when the candidate catalog expands. Freeze the published review
  generations and retain each old life's catalog on loading and saving, without
  changing its history or selected appearance.

- **Keep wardrobe editing with your own character** Keep other people's
  appearance read-only during normal play. Add an explicit own-wardrobe route
  from Personal using the controlled character's saved choices. In candidate
  review, show the complete outfit and an honest seated-fit refusal through the
  existing character compositor.

- **Readable title and creator over the room.** The title and New Game screens
  keep the compact menu on the cycling room, with type that stays readable on
  bright and dark frames. Disabled Continue and Saved games, keyboard focus, and
  the quiet corner version remain usable.

- **Verify old appearance identities through Personal and save reload** Remove
  the rejected NPC wardrobe shortcut. Follow the controlled player's Personal
  route and the conversation Back control in the browser proof. Compare both
  distinct donor lives with their captured component recipes, including the
  rendered wardrobe layers after migration and save/reload. Requires A's
  committed Personal route; no new pose artwork or production visual approval is
  included.

- **Personal routines use one consequential clock** Private citizens can reach
  jobs, study and outings through the Personal routine leaf. Combined skips
  summarize actual work, posted or pending pay, tuition and arrival. A newly
  posted ordinary meeting includes its disclosed local journey. Long skips stop
  for protected commitments, and interrupted study does not earn progress while
  inactive. Existing lives and earned history remain preserved. Recorded results
  retain exact minutes and also show readable elapsed duration, with separate
  lines for work, posted or pending money, arrival and study outcomes.

- **Play links are clearly separated from review tools** Local launchers show
  Play first and clearly label candidate art with separate saves and developer
  review tools. Double-click Play continues to open the selected art mode
  without running the full validation suite.

- **Source checks work in repository paths with spaces** Source tools correctly
  locate locked artifacts, qualification research matrices and fiscal
  inventories when the repository folder contains spaces, percent signs or
  Unicode. Existing hash and missing-file checks remain enforced.

- **Keep neckline repairs separate from saved character art** Add
  source-preserving collar openings for two fitted candidate shirts in review
  generation 3. Preserve existing face/body/hair, garment families, fit
  transforms, source bytes and prior generations. An explicit creation pin keeps
  new preview replays stable; ordinary and historical setup defaults remain
  unchanged.

- **The people you live with have an appearance of their own** A guardian, a
  parent or a sibling now carries an appearance the same way your own character
  always has. Nothing about who they are changed, and the same person looks the
  same every time you return to them.

- **Keep candidate shirt collars in front of the neck** Two candidate shirts
  resolve one clothing choice into coordinated torso and front-collar layers
  using their existing per-body fit. Preserve face/body identity, source pixels,
  cloth volume and prior catalog generations. No art approval or new renderer is
  implied.

- **Fit standing people to the existing calibrated apartment** The supported
  apartment now places a full standing figure at its authored floor/depth scale.
  Framing uses the composed figure, and furniture occludes rear depths without
  covering the foreground person or filling the open floor beneath the table.
  Retain both drawn-person missing-calibration diagnostic regressions using a
  scoped uncalibrated copy of the now-authored room. The original diagnostic and
  drawn/not-refused assertions remain; the room's actual calibration is asserted
  before the negative control. This corrects the old test premise without
  removing diagnostic coverage or changing runtime code. The named-presence test
  likewise retains the explicit uncalibrated refusal control. Refresh the
  framing JSON/Markdown through the existing capture command after scene
  authoring; ordinary tests still check the committed report read-only.

- **Keep appearance and clothing changes together.** Clothing and body edits are
  checked as a complete outfit before saving. A body change that needs different
  clothing offers a compatible outfit to preview and confirm. Unsupported views
  stay unavailable, and older saved outfits remain intact until explicitly
  changed.

- **Resolve invalid birthdays before leaving the character step** New Game
  explains an invalid named birthday at the character step and keeps Next
  unavailable until the fields form a supported date. Existing saved birthdays
  and unnamed replay descriptors remain unchanged. This receiver adds no art,
  save schema, updater or installed-profile changes.

- **Keep life requests concrete and charge time for activities** Requests retain
  the person, task and any agreed condition when saved. Answer a favor first,
  then carry it out or withdraw the commitment. Dialogue and reading dialogue
  take no time; actual activities keep their disclosed duration. Small
  conversational choices no longer skip large stretches of life. Family labels
  and child conversations follow established relationships, and repeated
  completion cannot grant another reward.

- **The opening room follows the introduction or scene actually in front** The
  household introduction now shows current household residents in its home, not
  people from a later ranked story. Opening scenes use their recorded setting
  and participants; stepping into the continuing life restores that story's own
  context. Back from conversation retains the foreground. A school or
  neighborhood without a released plate does not borrow apartment art. This is a
  read-only presentation correction. It changes no canonical clock, history,
  scene command, saved identity, simulation rule, or asset release status.

- **Keep unapproved location art in development review** The storefront now
  appears after an actual campaign phone session in the isolated development art
  preview. Unapproved location art stays out of ordinary production play.
  Development reviewers can also inspect the pavilion and press room with people
  and foreground furniture through the shared scene compositor.

- **Quiet version placement.** The game version now stays in one quiet
  bottom-right corner on the title, creator, questionnaire, and play screens.

- **Keep selectable people transparent on hover.** Selectable people no longer
  turn into a large green rectangle when the pointer passes over them. They
  remain real keyboard and pointer controls.

- **Keep creator navigation clear.** The creator now shows Begin only when the
  setup is ready, while Back and the current stage's Next action remain clear
  and usable.

- **Clarify selected-place copy.** Place selection now keeps the canonical
  resident-facing place name without formal-name or capability jargon. Any
  unavailable option explains its own scope instead of making a broader claim
  about political life.

- **New Game no longer starts you in Kentucky** A new life now asks you to
  choose a state, then a town in that state. Nothing is selected for you. After
  you pick a town, searching again or choosing Change lets you pick a different
  town in that state without dropping the first one until you choose the new
  one. Changing states still clears a town that does not belong there.

- **The corridor scene says what got broken, and who broke it** A childhood
  scene used to open "Something got broken in the corridor at your school and
  your name is the one that came up", and then offer "Say who did it" with
  nobody named. There was no object, no accused, and no way to tell what you had
  seen from what you had been told. It now names the thing — the trophy case,
  the fire-alarm cover by the stairs, the projector cart outside the science
  room — and names the classmate who broke it, taken from the school you
  actually attend. The option that names them says their name. That person and
  that object hold through the choice you make, the memory it leaves, a reload,
  and the later continuation. The branch that says a year has passed now waits a
  real year before saying so. Where a school holds nobody else, the scene is not
  offered rather than written around somebody who does not exist. At home, the
  scene about somebody coming in late names the adult you could tell instead of
  "a grown-up at home" — and two people in one house no longer answer to the
  same name, which is what had made that choice unreadable.

- **School scenes and recovered everyday moments now stay grounded in the life
  on record** The repaired school-corridor sequence now runs through the current
  playable screen: it names what broke, names the classmate who broke it,
  distinguishes what the office was told from what the player saw, keeps both
  facts through a save and reload, and carries them into the later scene. School
  copy uses school context and never borrows household art when no released
  school plate exists. People shown at home come from current household
  presence. Several reviewed everyday moments have also returned where the world
  already contains their premises. A supervisor or colleague must be a real
  recorded person, a class conflict must use a booked class, and agreeing to
  cover a shift puts the future activity on the existing Calendar and Places
  route. The favor is not treated as completed until the player performs that
  scheduled activity. This recovery adds no replacement prose engine, save
  migration, or separate shift-management screen. Existing identity, appearance,
  scene, menu, clock, Calendar, and Places behavior remains the integration
  base.

- **Bind constitutional proposal rules to the saved World** California
  constitutional proposals can use the existing canonical governing state
  identity in an ordinary locality-started life. Current-World proposal
  commitments consume only that life's operative rule history; earlier proposal
  snapshots remain stable. Detached rule reads protect saved law and the shared
  source baseline from accidental caller mutation. Institutional record
  ingestion grants no player seat and preserves ordinary-bill and charter
  boundaries.

- **Recover the next day's available time and enter supported legislative
  terms.** Keep the same character and home when a busy day gives way to the
  next morning. Kentucky House and Senate results now plan the recorded winner's
  office work for the supported term boundary, including a rival. Office entry,
  changed work, expiry and saved reopening use the existing clock and work
  records. A result alone grants no current office authority.

- **Accept prior canonical legislative record references** Historical events can
  reference existing legislative provisions, commitments and negotiations when
  those records were available at the event's date and history sequence.
  Preserve rejection of missing, future and later-appended records and retain
  the same checks when loading a save. This adds no player choice, time advance,
  spending authority or save migration.

- **The unsaved-life note no longer covers the button underneath it** On a
  phone-sized screen the note reading "This life has not been saved yet." sat on
  top of the main button above the current moment, leaving it half readable. It
  now sits at the foot of the screen instead, and none of these status notes can
  take a tap meant for something behind them.

- **The game no longer says someone is elsewhere when they are in the room**
  When there was nothing to talk about with somebody, the game said they were
  "not somewhere you can talk to them right now" — even when they were standing
  in the room with you. It now says what is actually true: there is no
  conversation established with them here yet.

- **Menus that go somewhere, and refusals that say what they mean** Who you are
  and Money and property used to open the same page; they are now two places,
  and your own name and age come before the regional figures rather than after
  them. The day no longer carries a second copy of study and work — it opens the
  one workspace those belong to — and the menu entry that only returned you to
  the room is gone, since closing a workspace already does that. The list of
  people beside the room is now the people who are in it. Everyone you know is
  still in the people list, and can be pinned from there. Walk home is no longer
  offered while you are already home, and a refusal says what is actually
  stopping you instead of telling you to check your commitments. When something
  does happen, the time it took and where it left you are shown, so you do not
  have to guess whether you went anywhere. Patch notes list the releases the
  game has actually had, with the version and the date each one carries, and the
  version is on the title screen. Work in progress is no longer listed as though
  it had shipped, and a reserved version number is no longer shown as a release.
  Where a release records no date, the notes say so rather than inventing one.
  In the office, the measure you are working on is named, and a different
  document that happens to be open is named separately as what it is.

- **People stand on the floor of the room, not through it** In some rooms a
  person was drawn standing below the floor — sunk into it by anything from a
  few inches to most of their legs, depending on the room. They were being
  pushed down by however much of them did not fit above the floor line. Everyone
  now stands exactly on the floor where they are. Somebody too tall for the
  space the room leaves above them is framed from the head down, the way a
  photograph taken in a small room is, rather than sinking to make themselves
  fit.

- **School scenes no longer play in the living room** A corridor scene at school
  used the apartment backdrop, so classmates appeared to be standing in the
  house. The current moment now keeps school, home, and a finished activity
  apart: school uses its own location, and when there is no school art the room
  stays empty rather than borrowing home. People who do not live there are not
  shown as being in the house.

### Changed

- **Filed measures keep their world identity from drafting through the floor**
  An ordinary legislative life no longer depends on one fixed bill number or a
  component-owned measure identity. The jurisdiction's recorded chamber and
  numbering state now assign each filed measure its designation, while the
  office, members' room, working paper, fiscal note and vote all follow the same
  saved measure record. The chosen authored deliberation remains deterministic
  for the same world and must agree with the filed measure and institution
  before the room is offered. Different supported worlds may select different
  authored measures and numbers. Saving and reopening preserves the selected
  measure, designation, cast and working record instead of selecting them again.
  The runtime census now guards the ordinary-player import graph against newly
  unclassified world-instance literals. Existing authored and development-only
  fixtures remain classified rather than being treated as ordinary generated
  play.

- **Screens say what they know, not how they came to know it** A person's card
  used to end by telling you what it had not been able to look up — "their
  education and work history are not known to you here" — and an unrelated
  person's opened with "No record establishes a relationship." The economic
  figures came with the dates they were retrieved and the digests of the files
  they came from. The charter screen carried publisher identifiers and a link to
  the survey behind them. Standing for office offered to explain what the game
  did not know about the seat, which unfolded into notes about which documents
  had and had not been read. All of it was true, and all of it was addressed to
  whoever builds the game rather than to whoever is playing it. Those screens
  now say what they have to say and stop: the figures without the paperwork, the
  charter as a document, the directory admitting it does not list tuition the
  way a person would admit it. Nothing became less careful. What the game
  declines to tell you, it still declines to tell you — it simply no longer
  reads out the reason. The one place that gained something is public office:
  whoever holds it now appears as holding it on their card, whether or not you
  have ever met them, because that is a public fact and waiting to be introduced
  to the governor was never how anybody learned who the governor was. Developers
  keep every line of it, on the routes built for that.

- **Ordinary jobs no longer demand a written shift report** Holding a regular
  job still takes time and still pays on the employer’s terms. You no longer
  have to pick a task and type a work report for every shift. Time you choose to
  skip can carry out that established routine; campaigning and other decisions
  still wait for you.

- **Begin in a quiet room with choices available on demand** Local-only
  candidate composition, not installed-app or production-art promotion. Receives
  the current C quiet-shell and content-viewport net while preserving landed J,
  existing structured press, pack import, save callbacks and focus flow.
  Personal retains authored choices and pending favors on demand. Receives
  LIFE's completed saved offers and concrete favors. Agreement preserves the
  actual proposal and spends no time; the supported activity owns its disclosed
  duration once. Brief dialogue is free, not a reward-farming bypass. Calendar
  simulation consumes C's standing-preference admission before mutation, with
  identical World/time refusal. Advance, Play and day/week passage remain
  separate controls. The later auto-open/idle-slab root delta is not mounted.
  Private material generations and old saves are preserved, not repainted.
  Receives the completed people and private paint returns: genuinely fresh
  candidate lives use generation6/complete-outfit-v2, old generation5/v1/absent
  pins stay exact. Recorded attributes guide fresh defaults without
  name-to-identity rules. Existing MaterialImage now carries the same saved
  material/features into the room; canonical portraits use authored face
  framing. Apply/Cancel remains one validated saved write. Candidate paint
  remains private, pending human approval.

- **A Politics hub, and a person card that opens beside the person** Politics
  now opens as one hub with Your office, Campaigns, Government, Parties and
  Issues and budget. Government shows who governs where you are right now, at
  the local, state and federal level, split into legislative, executive and
  judicial branches, and says plainly when the game has no record of a branch.
  You can switch to your home when you are elsewhere, and looking costs no time.
  Clicking someone in the room now opens their card right beside them, kept
  inside the window and clear of the controls at the bottom; a card opened from
  a list keeps its usual place. Your own card no longer talks about when you
  last spoke to yourself. News now opens on a front page that leads with
  national stories, and you can switch to one paper's own front page; what is
  happening around you, the outlets you follow and your press office each have
  their own view, a tab away. The menu now has a single Politics entry;
  everything that used to sit beside it is a tab inside the hub. The Journal
  reads by chapters of your life or year by year, and can be narrowed to one
  year. A proposal can be compared side by side or read as one document, and
  your working changes stay while you switch. The game remembers these choices
  for each life. Menus and choices throughout play now use the game's own
  controls: lists open in the game's style instead of the system's white menu,
  with full keyboard support. Creating a character now asks gender, then name,
  then your whole birthday, and shows the age you start at instead of asking you
  to type it.

- **Government shows who holds each seat, and Politics has one route to each
  place** Politics → Government now lists Congress seat by seat with each
  current member, recorded vacancies with their date and reason, and seats with
  no current record, with counts for each. A "Represented by" line names your
  districts and senators apart from where you are and your home. Issues and
  budget follows the place you chose in Government. Campaigns now holds every
  campaign, Your office holds only the office, and transit and tax proposal
  forms appear only when your office can use them, laid out as objective,
  proposal and filing.

- **People stand in the room, and the only list is the one you make** The strip
  of faces above the room is gone. It filled itself with whoever happened to be
  present and was the only way to choose anybody, which made it a list you never
  asked for sitting where the room should be. You now click the person
  themselves, standing where they are. The same things follow: a quick look,
  their full record, talking to them, keeping them to hand. Keyboard alone
  reaches them too — they take focus in turn, with a visible ring, and Enter
  opens the same menu the mouse does. One rail is left and it is yours. It holds
  only what you chose to keep there, it can mix people with the governments you
  follow, and nothing puts anything in it on your behalf. What you kept is still
  there after you save and come back. Browsing everyone you know, rather than
  everyone in the room, is under People, and that is where you can keep somebody
  who is not standing in front of you. When an action is refused, the reason is
  now written beside it rather than left to a grayed-out button. If you cannot
  talk to somebody yet, the room says why.

- **Education advances by academic periods** Simplified education progression to
  academic periods and program-level tuition instead of per-session scheduling
  for new noncredit and degree enrollments. Preserves legacy session-based
  saves, earned credentials, and accepted cost histories.

- **News speaks about the place; the Journal tells the life you lived** News now
  opens with the public situation where you live: who governs the place, what
  serves it, who holds office and since when, and what has happened in public
  lately, told plainly rather than as a list of records. The Journal tells your
  life year by year in your own second person, with what you privately think and
  what you said in public attributed to you; your private notes and the exact
  record remain underneath.

## PRE-ALPHA 0.3.0 — "A Life, Not a Fixture"

_Proposed September 2, 2026, accepted as it stands. Everything below is in
the game: the title screen, the new-life setup, saved games and the childhood
you play through all merged before this was accepted, so the number describes
what is there rather than what was hoped for. Work finished after September 2
is not part of this release and is recorded in the sections above it._

Opening the game now starts a game. There is a title screen, a new life to set
up, saved games to come back to, and a childhood you actually play through
before any of the politics arrives.

### Added

- **A calibration before the life, and it is not a quiz.** Five situations, or
  a longer set, or none at all. There are no right answers, nothing you pick
  becomes part of your character's history, and the game never tells you what
  it concluded — because a game that hands you a label has stopped being able
  to be surprised by you. What you actually do counts for around five times
  what you said here, so two or three real decisions in the other direction
  settle the matter.
- **An adult with something to do.** Thirty-five situations across the
  household, family, care, work, money, housing, friendship, the area, civic
  life and politics — offered because the thing they are about is already true
  of your life, never because a die said this was the year. A life with a job
  and a group in it is offered more than one without, which is the honest
  answer rather than a shortfall.
- **Moments that come back.** Something you said you would do, somebody you
  helped, a position you took where people could hear it. Months or years
  later it may return — and it may not, because the person moved on, the thing
  ended, or nobody was listening. Whether they raise it is their decision, not
  the game's.
- **Ordinary life, kept ordinary.** A good day, a quiet evening in, a week that
  simply worked. Roughly a third of what an adult meets asks nothing much of
  them, which is what keeps the rest able to ask something.
- **New game.** Choose where the life begins, how old the character is when you
  pick them up, how much of the earlier years you play rather than skip, and
  whether they already work in a legislature. Leave the name blank and you will
  be given one.
- **Saved games.** Keep a life, come back to it, and find the same people, the
  same history and the same face. Keep several and tell them apart at a glance.
  Deleting one asks first.
- **The growing-up years.** Fifteen situations across early childhood,
  childhood and adolescence, each a described moment rather than a summarized
  choice, and each with a real second option. Six of them are new. What the
  character remembers afterwards is written as a memory, not as the words that
  were on the button.
- **Quiet years.** Not every year has a turning point in it. The ones that do
  not simply pass.
- **Wider growing-up years.** Ten of the childhood situations gained a third
  way through them — writing it down instead of saying it, finding somebody
  else to go, doing both badly — because two options is a fork and three is a
  decision.
- **An ordinary day.** A character who does not work in a legislature still has
  a week that needs covering and a public meeting they can go to or skip.
- **Talking at home.** The conversation system now knows what it is talking
  about. Settling who does the shopping runs on the same rules as an office
  briefing and offers none of the same things to say.
- **Run for something.** A character old enough, living somewhere the game has
  read the rules for, can put their name on a ballot. A committee opens with
  nothing in it, an opponent turns up, and there is a date to work towards.
- **Afternoons that cost afternoons.** An hour and a half on the phones brings
  in money. The same time on the doors changes more minds and brings in none.
  An advertising buy spends what the committee actually raised, and cannot
  spend what it has not. Something already on the calendar can get in the way of
  any of them.
- **A field memo, and nothing more certain than one.** After a session somebody
  writes down roughly where the campaign stands, give or take four points — and
  sometimes it is further out than that. There is no bar filling up and no
  number to beat. What the electorate actually thinks is not on the screen and
  cannot be reached from it.
- **Election day, and the morning after.** The election arrives because the
  weeks went by. Losing is a thing that happened to somebody: the committee
  closes, it goes into the record, and the same day screen is there tomorrow.
  Winning puts them in the seat, and the office opens because they now work
  there.
- **Three more bills to push.** A road-signage bill the governor simply signs, a
  credential bill that dies in committee where most bills die, and an Alaska
  ferry-notice bill that is not a spending bill — so its veto falls at 40 of 60
  instead of 45.
- **A way to read what has been written.** A development-only content browser
  at `?view=content` lists every authored bank the game registers — the
  growing-up situations, the life episodes, the ordinary week, the conversation
  subjects, the setup questionnaire, the bills, the sourced legislative
  procedure and both sets of definition catalogs — and says for each one where
  it came from, what has to be true before it can happen, and whether a player
  can reach it at all. Where a bank does not say something, the browser says so
  and why, rather than filling it in — and where a bank says something that is
  neither a condition nor a choice, like a legislature's chambers or a bill's
  authored vote counts, it is shown as what it is instead of being filed under
  the nearest heading. Citations stay citations; unresolved research stays
  unresolved research. One button exports the whole thing as a Markdown report
  and as JSON. None of this is reachable from playing the game, and no content
  was written to fill it out.

### Changed

- **Where you start is where you live.** The game names the places it can
  honestly begin a life in, and says more are coming rather than pretending to
  cover the country.
- **Politics is not compulsory.** The office and the legislature appear because
  the character works there. A ten-year-old is not handed a bill, and when a
  surface is missing the game says why.
- **A legislature only plays by its own rules.** A place with no researched
  procedure gets none, rather than quietly borrowing another state's.
- **And neither does a ballot.** The game knows a seat is elected because it has
  read the instrument that creates it. It has not read who is allowed to stand
  for one, so it applies its own adult rule and says that it is its own rather
  than pretending to quote a law. Lexington-Fayette has a life to live and
  nothing to run for, and says why.
- **Bills say what they are.** Every measure now carries a line making clear the
  procedure is researched and the bill itself is written for development.

### Fixed

- A second new game in one sitting no longer overwrites the first.
- "Canonical minutes" is now just minutes.
- The unbuilt second office no longer says it is "not available in Run A".

### Developer tooling

_Nothing in this section is visible in the game. It is here because it changes
what the team can see, not what a player can._

- **Causal trace inspector.** A development route at `?view=causal-trace` that
  reads a save and shows how canonical truth, what somebody said, what a
  listener learned, what they concluded, and what they then decided actually
  connect. Every link it draws is a field the record already carries. Where the
  repository recorded no parent it says UNKNOWN, and where a walk stops it says
  which of five things stopped it.
- **Who heard it.** The same two conversation turns run quiet instead of
  normal now demonstrably produce a different set of listeners, a different set
  of knowledge records, a different set of perceptions, and a second-turn
  decision resting on a different recorded chain. Audibility was already
  causal; it is now legible.
- **Traces you can paste into a bug report.** `npm run trace:export` writes
  Markdown or JSON carrying the seed, world id, history frontier and content
  hash. The same replay and the same request produce byte-identical output.
- **Do different seeds make different lives?** `npm run compare:seeds`
  generates several worlds and reports where they actually differ, keeping
  structural differences apart from differences that are only a name.

### Known issues

- The longer calibration has twenty-six authored questions against a design
  target of thirty to fifty, and fifteen of the twenty-six read as policy
  questions rather than as life fragments. Those fifteen are ranked last, so a
  short calibration never reaches them, and the shortfall is reported in
  numbers rather than hidden. Closing it needs authored copy from the research
  lane; the implementing lane is not permitted to write questionnaire text.
- An adult whose earlier years were summarized rather than played arrives
  already carrying a community-service participation and a civic commitment
  from the scripted background generator, which is why a group situation can be
  offered on the first beat. The record says it is true, so surfacing it is
  consistent — but the generator that wrote it is development scaffolding and
  is due to be replaced.
- Housing situations need a tenancy the world does not yet establish for a new
  life, so they stay unavailable rather than being offered against nothing.
- Only four places can start a life, and none of them is a city with its own
  council. A national place corpus is the missing piece.
- Nobody has written down who may stand for office anywhere, how long a term
  runs, or when one begins. A candidacy is a general-election candidacy with no
  primary, no ballot access and no campaign finance rules behind it, and a seat
  is a seat in a chamber rather than a numbered district.
- A campaign is the candidate alone. There are no volunteers to recruit, no
  endorsements, no forums, and no NPC deciding to run for something.
- The opponent turns up when you file and does not campaign back.
- Generated people have no drawn likeness. They get initials and a name.
- The office scene, calendar, work list and working document still belong to the
  development fixture route and have not been rebuilt on the loaded world.
- The office referral conversation is still one topic; the new subject
  boundary makes a second one possible but does not itself write more of them.

## PRE-ALPHA 0.2.0 — "The Bill Becomes Law"

_Revised September 2, 2026 after an independent review of the legislating build._

You can now actually legislate. Write a bill, get it through committee, win the
floor votes, survive the other chamber, and find out what the governor does with
it. Sometimes the governor says no — and then you find out whether your
colleagues will stand with you.

### Added

- **File a bill and follow it all the way.** Pick a bill to carry, introduce it,
  watch it get a number and a chamber, and take it step by step through
  committee referral, a public hearing, a committee vote, the calendar, floor
  amendments, a recorded floor vote, the second chamber, enrollment, the
  governor's desk, and — if it comes to that — a veto override. Bills that make
  it are law; bills that fail stay in the record as bills that failed.
- **Every vote is a real vote.** Each member is counted by name and by decision:
  in favor, against, not voting, excused, absent. The chamber's own rules
  decide how many votes you need, and the game tells you the number before you
  call the question. Nothing is nudged, weighted, or smoothed.
- **Three legislatures that genuinely work differently.**
  - _Kentucky_ — an ordinary two-chamber legislature. 51 of 100 in the House,
    20 of 38 in the Senate, and the same majority sustains or overrides a veto.
  - _Nebraska_ — one chamber and no second one. There is nowhere to send your
    bill after it passes, so it goes straight toward enactment. In exchange,
    every bill is guaranteed a public hearing, and it must clear three separate
    floor stages instead of one.
  - _Alaska_ — two chambers, but the veto override is not fought chamber by
    chamber. Both bodies sit together, and an ordinary bill needs 40 of 60 while
    a spending bill needs 45 of 60.
- **A running story of your bill.** Plain-language answers to where it stands,
  who decides next, what that will take, what just happened, and what the rules
  do not settle — plus the full history and the complete roll-call record.
- **When the other chamber changes your bill, you have to win it twice.** If the
  second chamber amends the bill and passes it, it goes back to the chamber it
  started in, and your colleagues there vote on whether to live with the change.
  Agree and the bill goes on to the governor. Refuse and it dies with two
  chambers holding two different bills.
- **Waiting is a move.** Some things are not yours to decide. When the bill is
  on the governor's desk, the only thing on the screen is "Wait for the
  governor's decision" — and you find out what they did after you wait.
- **Days you have to sit out.** Where a chamber's rules say its stages fall on
  separate days, the bill cannot be reached again that day, and the screen tells
  you so and lets you wait for the next one. Nebraska's three stages now happen
  on three different dates, and the vote record shows them.
- **Legislation on the main menu.** A new destination in the navigation menu.

### Changed

- **The game now says what it doesn't know.** When nobody can establish
  something — such as when a new Nebraska law actually takes effect — the game
  says so plainly instead of inventing a date. "We don't know" and "there is no
  such thing here" are told apart and never blurred together. And where a rule
  _is_ settled, it is now stated as a plain fact about your world: in Alaska a
  law takes effect ninety days after enactment, and in both Alaska and Nebraska
  a bill the governor ignores becomes law anyway.
- **Buttons say what you do, not what happens.** You ask, you move the question,
  you wait. The committee, the chamber and the governor answer for themselves.
- **Committees answer twice.** A committee can send your bill to the floor while
  telling everyone it should not pass — that is a real report and the bill moves
  on. A committee that simply will not report it is a different thing entirely,
  and now reads that way.
- **Your committee has a name.** Bills go to a Transportation committee, not to
  "the House of Representatives standing committee of the House of
  Representatives".
- **Passing a bill and the law taking effect are two different moments.** The
  game tracks them separately.
- **Committee hearings take real time.** A hearing is scheduled on the calendar
  and happens when that day arrives, on the same clock as the rest of your life.

### Fixed

- **"The governor signs" could veto your bill.** Both outcomes used to sit under
  "What you can do", and picking one had no effect on what the governor
  actually did. There is now one honest wait instead.
- **A bill could quietly skip the second chamber's agreement.** After the Senate
  amended a bill, the game announced the bill had cleared the legislature. It
  had not.
- **Saving twice and carrying on could break the bill.** Amend, save, reload,
  amend, save, reload and amend again used to fail. A bill's record now belongs
  to the save, not to how long the tab has been open.
- **A finished bill could be un-finished.** It was possible for the record to
  say a bill was both dead and law, or to enter the same bill in the law books
  twice. A bill now resolves exactly once, and a save containing an impossible
  sequence of events is rejected rather than quietly tidied up.
- **The governor could act before the bill existed.** One decision now carries
  one date, and it can never fall before the bill reached the desk.
- **Rules the game had not settled were being treated as permission.** An
  unresolved rule and one that does not apply now both mean no.
- **Corrected what the game says the law is.** Kentucky's two chambers were
  sharing one chamber's rule book; Alaska's deadlines were attributed to the
  wrong section of its constitution, and it does have a settled effective-date
  rule and a settled answer for a governor who does nothing; and Nebraska's
  "most bills get a hearing" had been rounded up to "every bill is guaranteed
  one". Alaska's governor also no longer describes reducing an appropriation
  while actually returning the whole bill.
- The navigation menu no longer stretches down over the office when it has more
  destinations in it. It now has a fixed ceiling and scrolls past it, so the
  scene stays clear at every window size.

### Known Issues

- Only three legislatures are modeled. The rest of the country is not in yet.
- Members vote from the scenario's written record. There is no bargaining,
  lobbying, whipping, or persuasion yet — the seams for it are in place, but the
  arm-twisting is not.
- Committee substitutes, conference committees between two disagreeing chambers,
  procedural motions on the floor, and appropriations rules are not modeled.
- Adopting an amendment does not yet rewrite the bill's text. The change is
  recorded, and the other chamber really does have to agree to it, but you
  cannot read the two versions side by side.
- Session deadlines are shown to you but do not yet end a session on their own,
  and no bill can currently die at adjournment: none of the three legislatures
  has a settled rule for what happens to a bill still pending when the session
  ends, and the game will not guess one.
- Alaska will not let you amend on the floor. Nothing consulted establishes the
  authority for it there, and the game refuses rather than assuming.
- The governor ignoring a bill until the clock runs out is described but does
  not yet happen on its own.

---

## PRE-ALPHA 0.1.0

The first playable build: your office, your calendar, the people you work with,
and the documents on your desk.

### PR79 current-main integration

The bargaining room now uses the current conversation subject contracts and
canonical scene-surface projection. Existing commitment obligations, question
identity, supersession and decidable-condition repairs are preserved. Bargaining
decisions are D-081/D-082; main's decisions retain their identifiers. The
developer floor route remains separate from the pending PR85 player route.
